# CLAUDE.md

Guidance for Claude Code working in this repository.

## What this is

**Sovereign** — a modular, self-hostable workspace runtime. A shared platform
(auth, DB, email, UI) hosts installable **plugins** as first-class apps. The
plugin system _is_ the product, not an app extended with plugins. Open source,
privacy-first, single-tenant/multi-user in v1.

## Source of truth

These shared docs define project state and workflow. Read the relevant sections before any task —
they are authoritative over assumptions:

- `docs/sovereign-proposal-plan-srs.md` — Concept, Plan, Architecture, SRS,
  manifest reference, decision log.
- `ROADMAP.md` — Chronological task index (version → epic task ID → status).
  Full task detail lives in `docs/epics/`. Each task = one branch = one PR.

**Research precedes RFCs.** For an open-ended architectural or strategic
question with no concrete design yet, write a research doc in
`docs/research/` (findings, options, a recommendation — not a committed
design) before writing an RFC. Not every research doc graduates to an RFC;
a documented "not now" is a valid outcome. See `docs/research/README.md` for
the template and lifecycle, and `docs/documentation-structure.md` for how it
fits alongside `docs/rfcs/` and `docs/epics/`.

**Task workflow** — how to start, implement, and complete a task (including the
`CURRENT_TASK.md` mechanism and epic task IDs): `docs/development-workflow.md`.

**Multi-agent model** — how Claude Code and Codex divide work, commit attribution,
and the decision log behind these conventions: `docs/multi-agent.md`.

## Working conventions

- **One task at a time.** Implement a single task, verify its review checklist,
  then stop for human review. Do not start a task on an unmerged PR.
  _Exception — workstream legs:_ when executing a **workstream**
  (`docs/workstreams/`), the unit is a **leg**, not a task: one leg = one branch =
  one PR = one review gate. The agent runs every task in a leg uninterrupted, then
  verifies, opens a draft PR, and stops. The "no unmerged PR" rule applies at leg
  boundaries. One version bump per leg (semver follows the largest change in it).
  **Never merge automatically** still applies. See `docs/development-workflow.md`.
- **Tasks are sequenced** — each depends on the previous unless tagged
  `[parallel]`. Don't skip ahead.
- **Branch per task**, always cut from an **up-to-date `main`** — run
  `git switch main && git pull` first. Name by change type:
  - `feat/<slug>` — features
  - `fix/<slug>` — bug fixes
  - `docs/<slug>` — documentation
  - `chore/<slug>` — tooling, scaffolding, deps, maintenance

  e.g. `feat/shared-tsconfig`, `chore/scaffold-monorepo`.
  _(Post-v1.0.0 this changes: `main` becomes the production branch and `dev`
  the integration branch — branch from `dev` then. Until then, base off `main`.)_

- **Doc task numbers (e.g. `0.3.02`) are for local tracking only.** Never put
  them in branch names, commit messages, or PR titles/descriptions. Refer to the
  work by what it does, not its task number.
- **Epic task IDs (`<epic>.<seq>`, e.g. `9.9`) are permanent stable identifiers.**
  Use them in doc cross-references, RFC `incorporated_into_plan` fields, and task
  dependency lists. **Roadmap slot versions (e.g. `0.9.2`) are volatile** — they
  reflect current priority ordering and may shift when tasks are reprioritized.
  Always look up the live slot from `ROADMAP.md` rather than hard-coding it in
  docs; include slots only where shipping order matters (upgrade notes, version maps).
- **Commits** end with the Claude Code attribution trailer (model-agnostic — do not use a specific model name):
  `Co-Authored-By: Claude Code <noreply@anthropic.com>`
- **PRs** target `main` and are always created as GitHub drafts first with
  `gh pr create --draft`; mark ready for review only after explicit developer
  instruction. PR bodies end with:
  `🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)`
  Describe what changed and why, and cite relevant SRS sections — but no task numbers.
- **Multiple agents may work this repo** — see `docs/multi-agent.md` for the full model. Each agent uses its own clone. Commit trailers identify which agent authored the work.
- **Merge strategy: rebase and merge** (never squash, never create a merge
  commit). Keeps history linear — each task's commit lands on `main` verbatim.
- **Fix commit messages BEFORE merging the PR.** Once a commit lands on `main`, correcting it means rewriting/force-pushing `main` — avoid that.
- **When a task is done, update `ROADMAP.md` and the matching
  `docs/epics/<file>.md` task heading in the same PR.** Mark both ✅. Do not
  duplicate completion history in `CLAUDE.md`. The next task is assigned by the
  developer at session start, not inferred from a pointer in this file.
- **If the task implements or advances an RFC, update that RFC's own
  `Status:` line and its row in `docs/rfcs/README.md`, in the same PR.**
  These are a separate status record from `ROADMAP.md`/`docs/epics/` and do
  not update automatically when the epic task is marked ✅ — nine RFCs
  (0058, 0071, 0079–0083, 0087, 0088) were found sitting at stale
  `Draft`/`Accepted` status, in one case (0071) for a feature that had
  already shipped _and been retired_, before this was caught and fixed.
  Use `Implemented` once every "Incorporated into plan" task for that RFC is
  ✅, `Partially implemented (...)` with a short reason when only some are,
  and `Retired`/`Superseded` (with a pointer to why) if the shipped feature
  was later removed or replaced.
- **Verify before claiming done.** Run the task's review-checklist commands and
  show the output.
- Never merge a PR automatically. Either wait for explicit instruction to merge,
  or ask for consent before doing so.
- **Flag Docker-config impact immediately.** While building features or fixing
  bugs, whenever a change requires updating the Docker setup (`Dockerfile`,
  `apps/auth/Dockerfile`, `docker-compose.yml`, `docker-compose.prod.yml`,
  `.dockerignore`) — e.g. a new/renamed env var, a new port, a new on-disk path
  (writable dir or served asset dir), a new native dep, or anything affecting
  `next build`/standalone output — call it out in the same turn and update the
  relevant config (or ask), rather than letting it drift behind the code.
- **Docs are part of the change.** Changing the manifest schema
  (`packages/manifest`), the SDK surface (`packages/sdk`), or env vars
  (`.env.example`) means updating the matching doc in the same PR —
  `docs/plugin-development.md` (manifest fields, permissions, SDK methods) and
  `docs/self-hosting.md` (env vars). The `runtime/src/__tests__/docs-parity.test.ts` parity
  test enforces the enumerable parts (every field/permission/SDK key/env var must
  appear in its doc) and fails CI otherwise; review covers the prose.
  **The parity test is one-directional** (`.env.example` → `self-hosting.md`); it
  does not catch env vars consumed by the code but absent from `.env.example`.
  That direction is a human convention: every env var read from `process.env` in
  either app must be declared (or commented-out) in `.env.example`, even if it
  has a safe default, so operators know it exists.
- **Version bumps** are part of the PR — bump the relevant `package.json`(s)
  in the same branch, following semver tied to the change type:
  - `fix/` → **patch** (0.0.x)
  - `feat/` → **minor** (0.x.0)
  - Breaking change → **major** (x.0.0) — also requires a migration note in
    `docs/upgrade.md`
  - `chore/` / `docs/` → no version bump unless a public API changed

  **Plugins are the exception:** a plugin's version lives in its own
  `manifest.json`, never its `package.json` — see "Plugins version only their
  `manifest.json`" below. Never bump a plugin's `package.json` version; it
  stays pinned at `0.0.0`.

  The **SDK** (`packages/sdk`) and **UI** (`packages/ui`) are under an
  additional constraint per NFR-04: patch releases must never contain breaking
  changes; breaking changes require at minimum a minor bump and a migration
  note, regardless of branch type. Both packages are published to npm as
  `@sovereignfs/sdk` and `@sovereignfs/ui` — they are public contracts for
  plugin developers.

  The **platform version** in the root `package.json` tracks roadmap
  milestones — **each completed task bumps the minor version; patch versions
  are reserved for ad-hoc bug fixes and hotfixes between tasks; a single jump
  to `1.0.0` marks the public release.** See the "Status" section below for
  the current version and full per-release change history — do not duplicate
  that history here; a stale, independently-drifting copy of it used to live
  in this bullet (frozen at `0.76.1` while Status moved on to `0.94.x`) until
  it was removed for exactly that reason.

  **Per-package versions are independent of the platform version.** Internal,
  private packages (`@sovereignfs/db`, `runtime`, `auth`, `manifest`, `mailer`)
  follow normal semver tied to the change type above and **may cross
  `1.0.0`** on a breaking change — their versions are internal, not the
  user-facing product version (e.g. `@sovereignfs/db` is `1.0.0`). The published
  packages **`@sovereignfs/sdk`** (already `1.x`, the stable contract) and
  **`@sovereignfs/ui`** follow their own public semver per NFR-04 and are
  **exempt** from the platform's "stay under v1" rule.

  **Plugins version only their `manifest.json`.** A plugin's `package.json`
  exists for pnpm workspace tooling (deps, scripts) — its `version` field is
  not read anywhere in the platform and stays pinned at `0.0.0` forever, never
  bumped. The manifest's `version` field is the sole source of truth for a
  plugin's version: it's what the runtime, registry, and export/portability
  code all read (`docs/plugin-development.md`'s manifest reference). This
  applies to every plugin — platform plugins (`plugins/console`,
  `plugins/launcher`, `plugins/account`), example plugins
  (`example-plugins/*`), and everything the `sv plugin new` / `npm create
@sovereignfs/plugin` / `sovereignfs/sovereign-plugin-template` scaffolds
  produce.

  **Version-bump commit subjects and release tags use the same identifier.**
  Root `package.json` releases use `vX.Y.Z`. Package releases use the package
  slug plus version: `ui-vX.Y.Z`, `sdk-vX.Y.Z`, `runtime-vX.Y.Z`,
  `auth-vX.Y.Z`, and the same `<slug>-vX.Y.Z` pattern for other package tags.
  (`docs-vX.Y.Z` no longer applies here — `apps/docs`, the VitePress app that
  tag pattern deployed, was retired; the public docs site is now built and
  deployed from the separate `sovereignfs/sovereignfs` workbench repo. See
  `docs/repositories.md`.)

## Naming conventions

**"Plugin" is the architectural term; "app" is the presentational term.**

| Context                                                    | Term to use | Examples                                                               |
| ---------------------------------------------------------- | ----------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Code, types, APIs, DB schema, manifests, CLI               | **plugin**  | `PluginEntry`, `plugin.id`, `routePrefix`, `sv plugin add`             |
| User-facing UI strings, labels, placeholders, empty states | **app**     | "Search apps…", "No apps found", "App navigation", "Apps" drawer title |
| Documentation for plugin _developers_                      | **plugin**  | Plugin development guide, SDK docs, manifest reference                 |
| Documentation or UI visible to _end users_                 | **app**     | Launcher tiles, search overlay, mobile footer aria-labels              |

This split is intentional: internally "plugin" is the precise technical term for an installable module; externally "app" is what users understand. Never use "plugin" in a string the end user reads — it is a developer concept. This applies to all future shell chrome, Console user-facing copy, and any in-app help text.

## Code quality

Established in Task 0.3.3. Every package and PR must comply — no exceptions.

### Tools

| Tool                       | Purpose                                                       |
| -------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Prettier**               | Formatting — single source of truth for style                 |
| **ESLint 9 (flat config)** | Linting — correctness, best practices, SDK boundary rule      |
| `typescript-eslint`        | TypeScript-specific ESLint rules (recommended + strict)       |
| `eslint-config-prettier`   | Disables ESLint formatting rules that conflict with Prettier  |
| `simple-git-hooks`         | Pre-commit hook runner (lighter than Husky, no shell scripts) |
| `lint-staged`              | Runs Prettier then ESLint only on staged files (fast)         |
| `.editorconfig`            | Editor-level baseline — indent, line endings, charset         |

### Formatting conventions (Prettier)

- Single quotes
- Semicolons
- Trailing commas (`all`)
- Print width: 100
- Tab width: 2 spaces

### Rules

- **Never disable ESLint rules inline** (`// eslint-disable`) without a comment
  explaining why, and never disable the SDK boundary rule.
- **Prefix intentionally-unused identifiers with `_`** (e.g. required-by-signature
  stub params, ignored destructured fields). `no-unused-vars` ignores `^_`; this
  is the only sanctioned way to keep an unused binding.
- **Never add per-package Prettier overrides.** One config, entire monorepo.
- `pnpm format:check` and `pnpm lint` must pass before every PR. The pre-commit
  hook enforces this locally; CI enforces it on every push.
- **No Biome.** ESLint is required for the custom `no-restricted-imports` SDK
  boundary rule. Running both would be redundant overhead.

### Commands

```bash
pnpm format          # write formatting fixes across the whole repo
pnpm format:check    # check formatting without writing (used in CI)
pnpm lint            # run ESLint
pnpm lint:fix        # run ESLint with auto-fix
```

## Hard architectural rules — critical violations

The most likely rules to be accidentally broken. Full reference with context: `docs/architecture-rules.md`.

- **SDK boundary (ESLint enforced):** plugins must not import from `runtime/src`; use `@sovereignfs/sdk` only.
- **SDK zero-deps:** `packages/sdk` must not import `@sovereignfs/db` or `@sovereignfs/mailer`. Implementations are host-provided via `provideHost()` in `runtime/instrumentation.ts` → `runtime/src/sdk-host.ts`.
- **Extend `packages/tsconfig`:** every new package/app must extend `base`/`nextjs`/`library`.
- **Platform data layer is always async:** always `await getPlatformDb()`, `await getConfig()`, and all `packages/db` helpers — even on SQLite.
- **No secrets with defaults:** `AUTH_SECRET`, `SOVEREIGN_AUTH_SECRET`, etc. must throw on startup if unset.
- **Never `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` for runtime-varying values:** Next.js inlines these at build time; Docker images build without `.env`, so the value is frozen to its fallback regardless of what's in the container's env.
- **Middleware `/login` redirect must be `303` not `307`:** 307 preserves the HTTP method — an unauthenticated POST redirects as `POST /login` → 405. Also target `SOVEREIGN_AUTH_PUBLIC_URL` (browser-reachable), never the internal `SOVEREIGN_AUTH_URL`.
- **Intra-overlay navigation must use `<Link replace>`:** dialog is dismissed with `router.back()` — push-based navigation stacks history and a single back only closes one tab, not the dialog.
- **Never `'unsafe-inline'` in `script-src`:** CSP is nonce-based; the pre-paint theme script uses a hash (`THEME_SCRIPT_CSP_HASH`). Run `applyCsp` on every middleware return path.
- **CSP `form-action` must include the auth origin:** the logout form POST 303-redirects cross-origin to `SOVEREIGN_AUTH_PUBLIC_URL`; `'self'` alone silently blocks it.
- **`'use client'` components: never read browser globals in `useState` initializer or render** — causes hydration mismatches. Initialise to a safe value, read in `useEffect`.
- **Never add `runtime/app/api/*` segment without updating `RESERVED_API_SEGMENTS`** in `runtime/src/api-namespace.ts` — the dir-parity test will fail CI.
- **Profile self-mutations must clear both `session_data` cookie variants** (`better-auth.session_data` and `__Secure-better-auth.session_data`, `maxAge: 0`) — otherwise the cached name/avatar stays stale for up to 300s.
- **Never drop `pnpm-workspace.yaml` COPY from Dockerfiles** — `findWorkspaceRoot()` uses it to resolve `/app`; without it, SQLite files land outside the named volume and data is lost on container recreate.
- **Relative SQLite paths resolve against the workspace root** (nearest ancestor with `pnpm-workspace.yaml`), not the process cwd — every SQLite file lands in the single root-level `data/` directory regardless of which app opens it. Implemented separately in `packages/db` and `apps/auth/src/db.ts` (duplicated deliberately — auth doesn't depend on `packages/db`). Full detail: `docs/architecture-rules.md`.
- **The runtime Dockerfile must ship every plugin's `manifest.json` and `migrations/` folder into the runner image** (staged into `/app/.deploy/plugins`, not the full `plugins/*/` tree). A plugin missing its migrations there is silently skipped by `runAllPluginMigrations()` with no error logged — surfacing later as a production 500 (`relation "..." does not exist`) with nothing in the logs pointing at the cause. Full detail: `docs/architecture-rules.md`.
- **Plugin tables are slug-prefixed** (`tasks_lists`, `splitify_groups`); add `tenant_id` to every user-scoped table.
- **`session.freshAge: 0` in `apps/auth/src/auth.ts`** — don't re-enable without a re-auth flow (regression test asserts this).
- **Server-to-server calls to better-auth must send `Origin` header** equal to `SOVEREIGN_AUTH_URL` — CSRF check rejects originless POSTs with 403.
- **Invite-only mode is dual-written; the auth-server copy is authoritative.** Console's toggle writes `invite_only` to both the platform DB (`platform_settings`) and `apps/auth`'s own `auth_settings` table via a proxied PATCH, but registration enforcement reads only the auth copy — the auth server owns identity and never reads the platform DB. Never make registration read the platform DB instead. Full detail: `docs/architecture-rules.md`.
- **A quick-entry input that commits on Enter must also commit on blur**, via `useCommitOnEnterOrBlur` (`@sovereignfs/ui`). iOS's native keyboard-accessory Done/checkmark only fires a `blur`, never a keydown or form submit — an Enter-only handler silently drops typed input. Exception: a field inside a form with its own always-visible submit button (login, payment) should NOT commit on blur. See `docs/plugin-development.md`'s "Committing quick-entry input".
- **`touch-action`'s effective value is the intersection of an element's own value and every ancestor's**, not independently scoped. Declaring narrower values (e.g. `pan-y`) on nested perpendicular scroll containers (e.g. inside a `pan-x` carousel) can cancel both axes instead of routing between them — fix nested-scroller conflicts without touching `touch-action` on the nested pair.
- **Server actions must authorize inside the action; middleware path gating is not enough.** A `'use server'` function is a public POST endpoint dispatched by action id, not by the page it lives on, so the `adminOnly` gate on `/console` cannot be assumed to cover it. Start every action with `await sdk.auth.requireSession()` **and** an explicit `sdk.auth.hasCapability(session, '<capability>')` — `requireSession()` alone only proves the caller is some logged-in user. Critical in `plugins/console/app/**/actions.ts`, where `adminFetch` attaches `SOVEREIGN_ADMIN_KEY`. Full detail: `docs/architecture-rules.md`.
- **Never interpolate a request-derived value into a shell command string** — use `execFileSync` with an argv array. `JSON.stringify()` is not shell quoting: it leaves `$(…)`, backticks and `${…}` live inside the quotes it adds. Prefer resolving the value against a server-side allowlist and passing the resolved string. Full detail: `docs/architecture-rules.md`.
- **An anchored quantifier before `$`/`^` is quadratic even alone** — `/-+$/` with no `g` flag and no alternation still backtracks a whole non-matching run character-by-character at every starting offset. Dropping `g` or splitting `/^-+|-+$/g` into two replaces does not fix this; use a manual index scan instead. Full detail: `docs/architecture-rules.md`.
- **A row-less plugin defaults closed in production but is fully visible/open in local dev** — `bypassPluginVisibilityInDev()` (`runtime/src/plugin-status.ts`) checks `NODE_ENV === 'development'` exactly, never `!== 'production'` (Vitest sets `NODE_ENV=test` and must keep exercising real gating). Don't widen this check — it would silently disable access control under test or in a misconfigured deployment. Full detail: `docs/architecture-rules.md`.
- **Never make the service worker's `pages`/`pages-rsc`/`pages-rsc-prefetch` cache entries stale-serving** (`StaleWhileRevalidate`/`CacheFirst`) — Sovereign's pages are per-user SSR, so replaying a cached document risks showing a stale/different user's shell after logout/login. Keep them `NetworkFirst`; bound worst-case latency with `networkTimeoutSeconds` + `fallbacks.document` instead (`runtime/next.config.ts`). Full detail: `docs/architecture-rules.md`.
- **At-rest encryption (formerly RFC 0071, SQLite-only) was retired.** After repeated hardening passes still left it with an above-average bug surface (three separate incident/review rounds — see `docs/incidents/2026-07-24-rfc-0071-encryption-rollout.md` for the worst of them), the whole mechanism was removed rather than continuing to patch it: no dialect has an application-level at-rest encryption option today. `packages/db/src/sqlite-encryption.ts` (+ its `apps/auth` twin, `data-sovereignty.md`'s DB-URL/dialect workstream) now only keeps the narrow primitives `sv db migrate-to-postgres` needs to read a **legacy** encrypted source file during a one-time migration — nothing in the live server path reads `SOVEREIGN_DB_ENCRYPTION_KEY` anymore. A future resolution covering both dialects is tracked separately, not yet designed.
- **`sdk.device.getSurface()` / `x-sovereign-surface` (RFC 0080) is a presentation hint only, never a security boundary** — it derives from the shell's own, client-controlled User-Agent and is trivially spoofable. Never use it to gate authorization, entitlement, paywall, or data-access decisions; gate those by session, capability, or plugin permission instead. Full detail: `docs/architecture-rules.md`.
- **`apps/auth` reads the exact same `DB_DIALECT`/`POSTGRES_DB_URL` the platform does** — no separate `AUTH_DATABASE_URL`, so there is no config surface left for the two to disagree on dialect (a real production gap that used to exist: a Postgres-migrated platform could silently leave auth on its old SQLite default). Auth gets its own dedicated store instead — a Postgres schema (`sovereign_auth`, pinned via the connection's `search_path` startup option) or an sqld namespace of the same name — mirroring how every isolated plugin gets `plugin_<slug>`. `apps/auth` still deliberately doesn't import `@sovereignfs/db` (service-boundary independence); it duplicates the small amount of dialect/sqld resolution logic it needs (`apps/auth/src/db.ts`) rather than sharing code, but reads the same env var _names_. Full detail: `docs/architecture-rules.md`.
- **Every isolated-mode Postgres plugin needs its own `migrationsTable`** (`pluginMigrationsTableName(id)`, passed to `runPluginMigrations()`) — drizzle's node-postgres migrator tracks applied migrations in a table living in a _fixed_ `drizzle` schema regardless of the connection's `search_path`, so plugins left on the default table name silently share one tracking table and the second one's migrations get skipped as "already applied" with no error. Never apply this to isolated SQLite plugins — a separate file per plugin has no collision risk, and existing ones already have real history under the default name. Full detail: `docs/architecture-rules.md`.
- **A shared/isolated-mode plugin needs a genuinely separate `pgTable`-based schema file, not a variation on its `sqliteTable()` one** — `drizzle-kit generate --dialect postgresql` can't read a SQLite schema and silently reports zero tables. That Postgres schema file must use plain `integer` for booleans/timestamps, never native `boolean`/`bigint`, so the existing SQLite-typed query code keeps working once the physical columns serialize identically. Full detail: `docs/architecture-rules.md`.
- **A session-scoped Postgres primitive (`pg_advisory_lock`/`unlock`, session GUCs, temp tables) must be acquired and released on one pinned connection, never via independent calls against a `pg.Pool`-backed Drizzle client.** The pool is free to serve each call from a different physical connection, and `pg_advisory_unlock` silently no-ops unless issued by the exact session holding the lock — a real production incident: `runMigrations()`'s old lock/unlock pair leaked this way, hanging every subsequent container restart indefinitely. Check out one dedicated client via `pdb.db.$client.connect()` (drizzle's own exposed raw `Pool`) for the whole critical section instead. Also: never run a blanket `DROP SCHEMA "drizzle" CASCADE` in test cleanup — that schema is shared by every concurrently-running `.pg.test.ts` file's own migrations tables (per the bullet above), so a wide drop there races and deletes another file's in-use table mid-run; scope cleanup to the specific table names a test actually created. Full detail: `docs/architecture-rules.md`.
- **A plugin's page padding and max-width come from `@sovereignfs/ui`'s `PageContainer`, never from local `padding`/`max-width` in `app/layout.tsx` or a page-level CSS module (task 9.25).** The runtime shell applies no gutter of its own to plugin content anymore — only clearance for chrome it alone can measure (offline banner height, mobile footer height). Skipping `PageContainer` renders edge-to-edge; padding locally **and** wrapping in `PageContainer` double-pads. An overlay-shell plugin needs no special handling: `Dialog`'s content region sets `--sv-page-gutter: 0`, which `PageContainer` reads automatically to stand its own padding down, so the same page tree is padded correctly in both the modal and the full-page fallback. Full detail: `docs/architecture-rules.md`.
- **A `position: sticky` element with no compositing layer of its own can go stale/blank for a stretch of frames during a fast scroll on iOS Safari** (a WebKit momentum-scroll re-tiling quirk, not a z-index bug) — fix with `transform: translateZ(0)` on any sticky element inside touch-scrollable content; skip it on desktop/pointer-only sticky elements, which aren't at risk and don't need the extra compositing layer. Full detail: `docs/architecture-rules.md`.
- **A self-rendered `MobileHeader`/`MobileFooter` (`shellConfig.mobileHeader`/`mobileFooter: false`) publishes its own measured height onto `#sv-app-shell` automatically** — both components call `usePublishShellChromeHeight` internally; don't hand-roll this measurement in a plugin, and don't use `ResizeObserver` for it (documented as unreliable here — prefer `getBoundingClientRect()` in `useLayoutEffect` plus a `resize` listener). Full detail: `docs/architecture-rules.md`.
- **A signed-download route's token lives in its own `[token]` path segment** — never read `token` off `params` without a matching folder in the route's own directory tree. Found in the RFC 0084 backup-download route: it destructured `token` from `params` with no `[token]` segment anywhere in the path, so every request 404'd from the moment it shipped. Mirror `runtime/app/api/storage/[token]/route.ts`'s shape for any new one. **A second, distinct bug was later found in this same route:** its own doc comment claimed a `runtime/middleware.ts` matcher exemption "by design," but `api/backup-jobs` was never actually added to the matcher — a request with a genuinely valid signed token still 303-redirected to `/login` before the route's own check ever ran. A doc comment claiming a middleware exemption is not proof it was applied — check the real `matcher` array. Full detail: `docs/architecture-rules.md`.
- **RESOLVED (epic task 8.16): the production `runner` Docker image can now run instance backup/restore without `bin/sv.ts`.** `runner` is a minimal traced Next.js standalone build with no `bin/`/`scripts/`/`tsx`, so a `runtime`-hosted background worker shelling out to the full `sv` CLI (RFC 0084's backup worker) used to fail with `ENOENT`. Fixed by `tsup`-bundling just the `backup`/`restore` commands (`bin/sv-backup-cli.ts`) into `runtime/dist-cli/`, run there with plain `node` — no `pnpm`/`tsx` needed. Full detail: `docs/architecture-rules.md`.
- **`packages/db/src/bootstrap.ts` is a second, hand-written schema definition for at least `instance_config` and `backup_jobs`, not automatically kept in sync with `schema/{sqlite,postgres}/platform.ts` or the generated migrations.** A new column on one of these tables needs three edits, not two: both dialect schema files, the migration, and this file's matching `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`. Missing the third one passes a clean local `pnpm exec vitest run` (the affected test is `describe.skipIf(!PG_URL)`-gated) but fails in CI, which bootstraps a bare `postgres:16` container without running migrations first. Full detail: `docs/architecture-rules.md`.
- **A storage object uploaded with `ownerUserId` set is unreadable from any job/schedule handler, with no background-context fallback that fixes it** — `JobContext` carries a plugin id, never a user id (unlike `sdk.db.getClient()`'s `pluginId`, which falls back to `getBackgroundPluginContext()`), so a job/schedule handler's `userId` always resolves to `null` and the ownership check denies it. `sdk.storage.put()` for an object a background handler will read back must omit `ownerUserId`; the plugin's own tenant/user-scoped DB rows remain the real access-control boundary. Tradeoff: an unowned object is invisible to the account-deletion storage sweep — a plugin relying on this needs its own deletion path. Full detail: `docs/architecture-rules.md`.
- **Adding a new top-level member to `SdkHost` (`packages/sdk/src/host.ts`) is a host-implementer-facing breaking change per NFR-04** — bump `@sovereignfs/sdk` **minor**, not patch, the same bar as widening an existing field's type (e.g. `StorageContext.pluginId`, RFC 0044). It also breaks `pnpm typecheck` immediately, not just in theory: five hand-rolled mock `SdkHost` objects that exist specifically to test the SDK-side wrapper functions in isolation, without a real runtime — `packages/sdk/src/__tests__/{events,jobs,mailer-email-plugin-id,sdk,webhooks}.test.ts` — each need a matching stub added for the new member before typecheck passes again, since none of them import the real `runtime/src/sdk-host.ts`. Found generalizing the `sdk.storage`/`sdk.db.getClient()` background-invocation fallback to `sdk.env.get()`, which needed a new `env` host member with no prior host indirection at all.
- **A plugin's `schedules`/`jobs`/`events` handler must be imported from its real source file, never its composed route-tree copy.** `scripts/generate/*.ts`'s three handler-import generators point `runtime/generated/plugin-{schedules,jobs,events}.ts` at the plugin's own `plugins/<dir>/app/...` path directly — not `runtime/app/(platform)/(plugins)/<id>/...`, which is a real symlink in production (`compose-routes.ts`). That generated file is not excluded from `runtime/tsconfig.json`'s scope the way composed plugin directories themselves are, so TypeScript resolves the handler's own relative imports (e.g. a sibling `_lib` module importing an npm package) against the symlink's _apparent_ location, not its real target, when reached this way — silently breaking resolution of any package the handler imports that isn't independently redeclared in `runtime/package.json`. Found live: the first plugin ever to combine a `schedules` declaration with such an import (`nanoid`, not already redeclared like `drizzle-orm` was) failed a real production build with "Cannot find module." Full detail: `docs/architecture-rules.md`.
- **`checkAdminKey()` compares `SOVEREIGN_ADMIN_KEY` with `crypto.timingSafeEqual` on length-checked buffers, never plain `!==`** — it's the sole authorization boundary for all of `/api/admin/*` on both `runtime` and `apps/auth` (middleware deliberately excludes this path), so a plain string comparison is a timing side-channel with nothing else in front of it. A dedicated per-IP limiter (`admin-rate-limit.ts`, one per service) backs it, counting only failed comparisons — never successful ones, so normal Console admin traffic is never throttled — and once tripped, rejects every request from that IP with `429`, including one presenting the correct key. Full detail: `docs/architecture-rules.md`.
- **Trust between two first-party services reuses `apps/relay`'s signed-token pattern — never invent a new mechanism per service pair.** A self-verifying, HMAC-SHA256 `header.payload.signature` token (hand-rolled with `node:crypto`, verified via `timingSafeEqual`, no persistent state, revocation by secret rotation). Already reused beyond `apps/relay` itself for `apps/harness` ↔ Warden (RFC 0063). Full detail: `docs/architecture-rules.md`.
- **Composed route pruning (`scripts/generate/compose-routes.ts`) is recursive to the leaf composed directory, not top-level-only, and backed by a generate-time consistency check.** A renamed/uninstalled multi-segment `routePrefix` plugin (`shell: minimal` allows this) must have its stale nested directory actually removed, not just orphaned in place because the shared parent segment still matches an active entry — `pruneGeneratedEntriesAt`/`collectComposedLeaves` walk the full relative path at every depth; `composePlugins()` also runs `assertNoOrphanedRouteDirectories()` after composing, which independently re-derives the composed tree by `page.tsx`/`layout.tsx` presence and fails loudly on any mismatch. That leaf-detection walk must check the active-entry set _before_ falling back to the file-presence heuristic — checking the heuristic first false-positives on a plugin's own internal Next.js route groups (e.g. `(home)`), which sit above a real `page.tsx` with no `routePrefix` of their own. Full detail: `docs/architecture-rules.md`.

## Design system (`packages/ui`)

`packages/ui` is the **Sovereign Design System** — a public contract for plugin
developers, versioned with the same discipline as the SDK. Breaking a token name
or component API breaks every third-party plugin that uses it.

### Technology

- **Tokens:** CSS custom properties in plain `.css` files — universally
  consumable from any CSS, framework-agnostic, RSC-safe. No JS import required
  to use tokens.
- **Components:** React + CSS Modules — zero extra dependencies, built into
  Next.js, familiar, RSC-safe by default.
- **No Tailwind.** No runtime CSS-in-JS. No third-party component framework.

### Token architecture — two tiers

```
Primitive tokens     raw scale, no semantic meaning
  --sv-grey-50 … --sv-grey-950
  --sv-space-1 … --sv-space-16
  --sv-font-size-sm … --sv-font-size-2xl
  --sv-radius-sm / -md / -lg
        │
        ▼  mapped by semantic layer
Semantic tokens      contextual meaning, what plugin devs use
  --sv-color-surface
  --sv-color-text-primary
  --sv-shadow-card
  --sv-radius-md
```

Plugin developers reference **semantic colour tokens** — never primitive
colours directly. The semantic colour layer is the theming surface that instance
theming (CON-08) and dark mode override at `:root` / `[data-theme]`; primitives
stay fixed. The scale tokens (`--sv-space-*`, `--sv-radius-*`,
`--sv-font-size-*`) have no separate semantic tier — they are theme-stable and
used directly. See `docs/design-system.md` for the full model. The v1 identity
is **monochrome** (accent = near-black on light, near-white on dark); an instance
admin adds colour by overriding `--sv-color-accent`.

### Token prefix

All tokens use `--sv-*` — short, consistent with the `sv` CLI identity, and
unambiguous. **Never abbreviate after the prefix** — use full descriptive names:
`--sv-color-text-primary` not `--sv-ctp`.

### What plugin developers consume

```ts
// Components — typed React components
import { Button, Card, Input, Badge } from '@sovereignfs/ui';

// Tokens — already injected globally by the runtime shell.
// Reference directly in plugin CSS without any import:
// color: var(--sv-color-text-primary);
// background: var(--sv-color-surface);
```

### Scope rules

- The runtime shell and Console plugin use both tokens and components.
- Plugin developers may use any component or token.
- Components must never hardcode values — always reference `--sv-*` tokens.
- Dark mode and instance theming work by swapping semantic token values at `:root`;
  no component changes required.
- **DS-first: plugins are consumers.** Reusable UI/UX capability — interaction
  hooks (long-press, double-tap, `useIsMobile`), overlay surfaces, secondary
  headers, motion, controls — is implemented in `packages/ui` (or the runtime
  shell for shell chrome) and consumed by plugins, never implemented
  plugin-locally "to be promoted later". React-coupled UI utilities belong in
  `@sovereignfs/ui`, not the SDK. Full statement: `docs/design-system.md`
  Design principles; rule detail: `docs/architecture-rules.md`.

### Storybook hygiene (enforced per-PR)

`packages/ui` is the public design system contract — Storybook is its living
documentation. Whenever a task touches anything under `packages/ui/src/`:

- **New component** → add a story file `packages/ui/src/stories/<Name>.stories.tsx`
  covering at least the default state and one variant; add the component to the
  **Component Gallery** section of `DesignSystemOverview.stories.tsx`.
- **New or renamed token** → add or update the token in `TokenGallery.stories.tsx`
  _and_ in the relevant color/scale section of `DesignSystemOverview.stories.tsx`.
- **Component API change** → update the matching story's args/controls to reflect
  the new props; update the import snippet in `DesignSystemOverview.stories.tsx`
  if the public API visible there changed.
- **After any of the above** → run `pnpm --filter @sovereignfs/ui typecheck` to
  verify the stories are type-correct. The CI `storybook-build` job enforces a
  clean build on every PR.

The rule is: the story must reflect reality, not the state the component was in
when the story was first written. Stale stories are silent lies in documentation
that plugin developers will trust.

## Native mobile app (post-v1 plan)

Mobile is out of scope for v1 but the approach is decided — do not treat it as
an open question or suggest alternatives.

**Model:** Universal Capacitor shell app — one binary on the App Store / Play
Store. On first launch the user enters their self-hosted instance URL. The app
loads it in a WebView. All Sovereign functionality is served by the user's
instance and runs unchanged. Multiple instances supported. Same pattern as
Nextcloud, Bitwarden, Element (Matrix).

**Shell:** Capacitor (single TypeScript codebase for iOS + Android). Lives in a
separate `sovereign-mobile` repository, not this monorepo.

**Device API tiers — in priority order:**

1. **Web APIs** — `navigator.geolocation`, `getUserMedia` etc. Work natively in
   WebViews, also work in browser/PWA. Use these first.
2. **Capacitor plugins** — for what Web APIs can't cover: native photo picker,
   APNs/FCM push notifications, Face ID / fingerprint, haptics, background
   location.
3. **`sdk.device.*`** — the SDK abstraction plugin developers call. Detects
   environment, routes to the correct tier. Plugins never call Web APIs or
   Capacitor directly.

**Plugin developers use `sdk.device.*` only.** This keeps plugins portable
across browser, PWA, and native shell without changes.

See SRS §3.12 for the full specification.

## Desktop app (post-v1 plan)

Desktop is out of scope for v1 but the approach is decided — do not treat it as
an open question or suggest alternatives.

**Model:** Universal Tauri shell app — direct `.dmg`/`.exe` download. On first
launch the user enters their self-hosted instance URL. The app loads it in a
WebView. All Sovereign functionality is served by the user's instance and runs
unchanged. Multiple instances supported. Same model as the mobile shell and the
same pattern as Nextcloud, Bitwarden, and Element desktop clients.

**Shell:** Tauri 2.x (TypeScript shell logic; system WebView — WKWebView on macOS,
WebView2 on Windows, WebKitGTK on Linux; ~5 MB binary). Lives in a separate
`sovereign-desktop` repository. macOS ships first; Windows and Linux follow with
the same codebase.

**Distribution:** Direct download via GitHub Releases (`.dmg`, `.exe`/`.msi`,
`.AppImage`/`.deb`). Mac App Store deferred.

**Device API tiers — same three-tier model as mobile:**

1. **Web APIs** — work natively in the system WebView. Use these first.
2. **Tauri plugins** — for native capabilities beyond Web APIs: system tray,
   OS notifications, keychain, deep links, auto-updater. Added post-v1 as needed.
3. **`sdk.device.*`** — the SDK abstraction plugin developers call. Detects
   environment (`"desktop"`), routes to the correct tier.

**Plugin developers use `sdk.device.*` only.** No plugin changes required when
the desktop shell launches.

See RFC 0038 and SRS §3.19 for the full specification.

## Tech stack

Next.js 15 (App Router) · TypeScript · Turborepo + pnpm workspaces ·
better-auth (`apps/auth`) · Drizzle ORM (SQLite/Postgres) · nodemailer SMTP
(`packages/mailer`) · CSS Modules + CSS custom properties (`packages/ui`) ·
`tsup` (package bundler, ESM only) · Vitest + Testing Library / jsdom (tests in per-dir `__tests__/`; root `__tests__/{integration,e2e,visual}/` scaffold for future tiers) ·
Zod (manifest
validation) · `citty` + `consola` (`bin/sv` CLI) · `@ducanh2912/next-pwa` ·
Docker Compose.

## Monorepo layout

```
apps/
  auth/             better-auth wrapper (the only separate Next.js app)
packages/
  tsconfig/         shared TS configs (base/nextjs/library) — extend these
  db/               Drizzle client factory + schema + migration runner
  manifest/         manifest schema, types, validation
  mailer/           SMTP abstraction (no-op when unconfigured)
  ui/               shared component library + design tokens
  sdk/              plugin↔platform contract (types + impls)
  bridge/           device bridge implementation (RFC 0083) — published
  create-plugin/    `npm create @sovereignfs/plugin` scaffolder — published
runtime/            Sovereign Core (Next.js shell, middleware, registry, SDK bridge)
  generated/        built from manifests — never hand-edit, gitignored (run `pnpm generate`)
plugins/console/    core admin plugin (platform type)
plugins/launcher/   home screen plugin (platform type)
plugins/account/    per-user profile plugin (platform type)
example-plugins/    in-repo reference/teaching plugins — composed only when
                    SOVEREIGN_EXAMPLES_ENABLED is set (docs/epics/example-plugins.md)
registry/           public plugin index (plugins.json) + submission process
scripts/            install-plugins.ts, generate-registry.ts, dev.ts
bin/sv              CLI (v0.5)
```

### Package naming and scope

One owned npm scope for everything: **`@sovereignfs/*`**. The `fs` denotes
_federated systems_ — reflecting the project's long-term federated direction
(federation itself is a post-v1 concern; see SRS §1.4 non-goals).

- `packages/sdk` → `@sovereignfs/sdk` — **published** (plugin contract).
- `packages/ui` → `@sovereignfs/ui` — **published** (design system).
- `packages/create-plugin` → `@sovereignfs/create-plugin` — **published**
  (`npm create @sovereignfs/plugin` scaffolder; ships a `create-plugin` bin).
- `packages/bridge` → `@sovereignfs/bridge` — **published** (RFC 0083 device
  bridge implementation: transports, protocol, shell-side helper; consumed
  directly by the external `sovereign-mobile`/`sovereign-desktop` repos).
- `packages/db` → `@sovereignfs/db` — internal, `"private": true`.
- `packages/manifest` → `@sovereignfs/manifest` — internal, `"private": true`.
- `packages/mailer` → `@sovereignfs/mailer` — internal, `"private": true`.
- `packages/tsconfig` → `@sovereignfs/tsconfig`. Not published and not imported
  in code; consumed only via TypeScript `extends`
  (`@sovereignfs/tsconfig/base.json` etc.), declared as a `workspace:*`
  devDependency by each consumer.

The "do not publish" signal is `"private": true` in the package's
`package.json` — **not** the scope. A single scope we own avoids the
dependency-confusion risk of aliasing a scope owned by someone else
(`@sovereign` is taken on npm; `@sovereignos`/`-stack`/`-core` collide with
existing products). Only `sdk`, `ui`, `create-plugin`, and `bridge` ever reach npm.

## Commands

```bash
pnpm install            # install workspace deps
pnpm build              # turbo build — packages (tsup) → generate → apps (next build)
pnpm dev                # start dev servers; generate runs automatically on startup
pnpm format             # write Prettier formatting fixes across repo
pnpm format:check       # check formatting without writing (CI)
pnpm lint               # ESLint incl. SDK import-boundary rule
pnpm lint:fix           # ESLint with auto-fix
pnpm typecheck          # tsc --noEmit across packages
pnpm design:tokens:check # verify --sv-* token refs resolve + no hardcoded colour literals in packages/ui, runtime/app, plugins/*/app
pnpm test               # run Vitest across the repo
pnpm test:watch         # Vitest in watch mode
pnpm test:unit          # unit/component tests with verbose output
pnpm test:integration   # cross-service integration tests (root __tests__/integration/)
pnpm test:e2e           # end-to-end tests (root __tests__/e2e/)
pnpm test:all           # unit → integration → e2e in sequence (local full-suite run)
pnpm kill-port          # kill any process listening on port 3000 or 3001 (frees dev ports)
pnpm install:plugins    # clone sovereign/community plugins declared in sovereign.plugins.json
pnpm registry:validate  # fetch + validate registry/plugins.json entries, write content-hash provenance
pnpm registry:check     # verify-only (no write) — CI runs this on registry/ changes
```

## Dev DX notes

- **No manual rebuilds in dev.** `pnpm dev` runs `scripts/dev.ts`: composes plugins, starts the generate watcher, and runs `next dev` on `:3000`. HMR handles all subsequent changes.
- **`pnpm dev` requires Docker running, for `DB_DIALECT=sqlite`'s sqld dependency.** Root `package.json`'s `dev` script is `tsx scripts/ensure-sqld.ts && turbo dev` — `ensure-sqld.ts` starts (or reuses) a persistent `sovereign-sqld-dev` container before `turbo dev` fans out into the runtime's and auth's separate dev processes, which run in parallel with no ordering between them and would otherwise race on which starts sqld first. Deliberately a different container/volume than `docker-compose.yml`'s own `sovereign-sqld`/`sovereign_sqld_data` — native `pnpm dev` and the Dockerized stack must never silently share (or fight over) the same sqld data.
- **Package changes → instant HMR.** All workspace packages are in `transpilePackages` in both `next.config.ts` files. Next.js compiles source directly — no `tsup --watch`.
- **Plugin changes → HMR via re-copy.** `scripts/dev.ts` runs generate in `--watch` mode; an edit under `plugins/[id]/app/` re-copies into the route group and Next hot-reloads. Plugins are copies, not symlinks — Next's dev watcher does not follow symlinks.
- **tsup is production-only** — not part of the dev pipeline. It runs during `pnpm build` to emit `dist/` for Docker/npm.
- **Profile self-mutations must clear both `session_data` cookie variants** (`better-auth.session_data` + `__Secure-better-auth.session_data`, `maxAge: 0`). The session cache is 300s; without clearing it, the chrome still shows the old name/avatar until it expires. Done in `runtime/app/api/account/avatar/route.ts` and `plugins/account/app/actions.ts`.
- **Middleware verifies sessions offline** via better-auth's signed cookie cache (`session.cookieCache`, 300s, HMAC key = `SOVEREIGN_AUTH_SECRET ?? AUTH_SECRET`), then falls back to `GET /api/verify`. Forward better-auth's `Set-Cookie` on cache miss so the cache self-refreshes. The runtime service needs `AUTH_SECRET` in every compose file.
- **Account profile page reads authoritative session** with `GET /api/auth/get-session?disableCookieCache=true` — plain `get-session` honours the cache and returns a stale value after a self-edit.

> For local dev setup, email (Mailpit), and Docker Compose details: see `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

## Environment notes

- Node ≥20 (dev on 24.x), pnpm 11.5.2 (pinned via `packageManager`).
- pnpm 11 blocks dependency build scripts by default. `esbuild` (via `tsx`) and
  `better-sqlite3` are allowlisted in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` under `allowBuilds`
  — required for their native bindings. `simple-git-hooks` is set to `false`
  there (the root `prepare` script installs the hooks instead).
- **Shared dev tooling is pinned via the pnpm `catalog:`** in
  `pnpm-workspace.yaml` (`typescript`, `tsup`, `react`, `next`, `@types/node`,
  …). Every package references them as `"typescript": "catalog:"` /
  `"tsup": "catalog:"` — never a literal version. This stops `pnpm add` from
  floating one package onto a different major (it once pulled TS 6 into a
  single package, and separately let `@types/node` drift to a new major via
  an unpinned transitive peer range, cascading into a 2000+ line
  `pnpm-lock.yaml` diff for an unrelated change). When adding a dependency
  that's already in the catalog, reference it with `catalog:`; when adding a
  _new_ shared dep, add it to the catalog first rather than pinning a literal
  version in just one package.
- **Run `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` for routine installs** (after a
  `git pull`, before running tests/build) — reserve a bare `pnpm install` for
  when you're intentionally adding, removing, or bumping a dependency. A
  non-frozen install re-resolves the whole dependency graph, and if any
  transitive package has a loose/unpinned peer range, it can silently pull
  in a newer major and rewrite `pnpm-lock.yaml` far beyond what your actual
  change touched — this is what caused the `@types/node` drift above. If
  `pnpm-lock.yaml` shows unexpected changes after a routine install, treat it
  as a signal something's unpinned that should be, not something to shrug
  off and commit anyway.
- **Exception: after cloning a `.local` plugin** (via `pnpm install:plugins`
  or a manual `git clone … plugins/<name>.local`), `--frozen-lockfile`
  will always fail — the plugin is a new workspace project (matched by the
  `packages:` glob in `pnpm-workspace.yaml`) with dependencies the committed
  lockfile has never seen, and it always will, because `.local` plugins are
  gitignored by design (`docs/plugin-development.md`) and never enter the
  committed lockfile. This is expected, not lockfile drift. Run a bare
  `pnpm install` once to resolve it locally, then don't commit the resulting
  `pnpm-lock.yaml` diff — check `git diff pnpm-lock.yaml` before committing
  anything else and make sure it's scoped to your intended change, not
  leftover `.local`-plugin entries.

## Status

Current platform version: **`0.130.0`**. All roadmap tasks through slot `0.13.0` are complete; later minor bumps track post-slot tasks and patch versions are hotfixes. `0.129.0` → `0.130.0` is workstream 0023 leg 6 (epic task 8.42) — `sv restore --age-identity` + docs, the workstream's final leg. `bin/backup-restore.ts`'s `restore` command gains `--age-identity <file>`: a new `parseIdentityFile()` splits the file's raw content into candidate key lines the same way the real `age` CLI's own `-i` flag does (blank lines and `#`-prefixed comments ignored) — necessary because `age-encryption`'s own `Decrypter.addIdentity()` accepts only a bare `AGE-SECRET-KEY-1...` string and throws on anything else, unlike the real CLI, and `age-keygen -o <file>`'s default output has exactly the comment-plus-key shape that needed tolerating. A new local `decryptWithIdentity()` — duplicated in this file rather than imported from `runtime/src/backup-encryption.ts`, matching `bin/backup-restore.ts`'s own established convention of never crossing that boundary, for its bundle-ability into the dependency-free `sv-backup-cli.js` artifact — decrypts into a temp file inside a dedicated `.sv-restore-decrypt-*` directory, which the existing restore logic then reads unmodified, cleaned up in a `finally` block regardless of outcome. `age-encryption` is a genuine new dependency for `bin/` (root `package.json` devDependencies as `catalog:`; `bin/tsup.config.ts`'s `noExternal` list alongside `citty`/`consola` — all three pure JS, no native addon, so the bundle's dependency-free property for the production `runner` image is unaffected). No passphrase-mode flag was added — a passphrase-encrypted archive still needs the standalone `age` CLI to decrypt first, exactly as before this leg; only recipient mode gets the convenience flag, matching this task's own scoped deliverable. Verified live end to end against real Postgres infrastructure, reusing the real recipient-mode archive and operator identity generated during leg 5's own verification rather than regenerating them: built the `tools` image (has `pnpm`/`tsx`/`pg_restore`, unlike `runner`) with this leg's changes, spun up two fresh throwaway Postgres containers, and ran `sv restore --age-identity <identity> <archive.tar.gz.age>` against the first — it decrypted correctly and populated 4 real schemas (`public`, `drizzle`, `sovereign_auth`, `plugin_fs_sovereign_warden`) and 58 real tables, not an empty or partial restore. Separately confirmed the existing plain-archive path is completely unaffected: a fresh `sv backup` off that now-populated instance, restored with no `--age-identity` flag into the second throwaway Postgres, produced identical schemas/tables with no decryption step logged. Also confirmed live that an independently-generated, non-matching identity fails cleanly (`Decryption failed: the identity does not match...`, exit 1) before ever reaching `pg_restore`, leaving the target database untouched. All three runs used real `pg_dump`/`pg_restore`/`tar`/`age` subprocess invocations inside the actual container image, not mocked. Full `pnpm exec vitest run` (3213 passed, up from 3205 — 8 new tests generating real ciphertext via `age-encryption`'s own `generateIdentity()`/`identityToRecipient()` rather than mocking the crypto), `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check`, and `pnpm exec tsx scripts/design-tokens-check.ts` all green. `docs/security.md` documents both backup encryption modes and states the recipient-mode no-server-side-escrow property plainly; `docs/self-hosting.md`'s pre-existing "Backups" section (its manual `age`/GPG recipe, written before this workstream existed) was also corrected as a drive-by fix — it no longer reads as if no backup is ever encrypted automatically. No `runtime` version bump — nothing under `runtime/` changed this leg; root platform bumped minor per the `feat/` convention. **This completes workstream 0023 in full** — all six legs (encryption primitives; per-user identity/connection/push/restore; operator recipient destination; operator CLI restore + docs) are now ✅. `0.128.1` → `0.129.0` is workstream 0023 leg 5 (epic task 8.41) — an operator age-recipient backup destination, picked up as the next item once epic 8.17's backup half (task 8.41's own dependency, alongside 8.37/8.39, all three now shipped) and the Docker-spawn gap (task 8.16) were both confirmed genuinely resolved, not merely merged, per this leg's own "do not proceed if" clause. A new `SV_BACKUP_GIT_AGE_RECIPIENT` env var (plain config, not `sdk.secrets`, alongside the existing `SV_BACKUP_GIT_REPOSITORY`/`_BRANCH`/`_TOKEN`) lets an operator's instance-scope git-push encrypt to a long-held identity instead of the one-off passphrase typed into the Console trigger form each time — via a **separate** `encryptToRecipients()` pass over the same plaintext, never combined into one multi-recipient `age` file. This was a real implementation-time design call this leg's own workstream doc explicitly deferred ("age supports multiple recipients per file natively... though whether to expose that combination in the UI is an implementation-time call, not a locked decision here") — resolved in favor of genuine isolation (mirroring task 8.39's own per-user precedent, `pushUserBackupToDestination`'s existing separate-pass design) specifically because this task's own review checklist requires the pushed copy to decrypt "only with the matching identity," which a combined passphrase+recipient file would silently fail (it would also accept the passphrase). The direct-download archive stays passphrase-only always, completely unaffected by this setting. `pushBackupToGit()`'s existing plain `manifest.json` gained one field, `encryptionMode: 'passphrase' | 'recipient'`, so a human inspecting the bare git repo directly — the whole point of this workstream's design — can tell which decrypt path applies without guessing. No Console UI change: the recipient is instance-wide operator config exactly like the other three `SV_BACKUP_GIT_*` vars, not a per-backup choice. Verified live end to end against real Docker/Postgres infrastructure (a native `pnpm dev` SQLite checkout can't produce a real instance backup at all, a pre-existing unrelated limitation): rebuilt the `auth`/`runtime` images, brought up the stack with a real local bare git repo and a freshly `age-keygen`-generated operator identity, and triggered a real backup with `pushToGit: true`. Confirmed a correctly-tagged, zero-parent orphan commit landed with the right `manifest.json`/`encryptionMode: "recipient"`, and ran a full four-way cross-decrypt: the git-pushed copy decrypts with the real, independently-installed `age` v1.3.1 CLI and the generated identity (Sovereign-independent proof) but is correctly rejected by `age` when given the passphrase instead; the direct-download copy decrypts with the real `backup-encryption.ts` `decrypt()` using the passphrase, and that same passphrase is correctly rejected against the git-pushed bytes — proving the two copies are genuinely, independently encrypted, not a same-file coincidence. Also confirmed via `docker exec` that the new env var reaches the running container from `docker-compose.prod.yml` as configured. New tests nested under `backup-run.test.ts`'s existing git-push describe block; the pre-existing no-recipient-configured test required zero changes and continues to lock in that behavior byte-for-byte. Full `pnpm exec vitest run` (3205 passed, up from 3202), `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check`, and `pnpm exec tsx scripts/design-tokens-check.ts` all green. `runtime` bumped `0.98.1` → `0.99.0`. This completes workstream 0023's operator-scope leg 5; leg 6 (`sv restore --age-identity` + docs, epic task 8.42) remains, depending only on this task. `0.128.0` → `0.128.1` fixes a real bug found live-verifying epic task 8.17's own just-shipped backup-trigger UI, reported directly by the developer with a full reproduction and suggested fix: the signed backup-archive download route (`runtime/app/api/backup-jobs/[jobId]/download/[token]/route.ts`, epic task 8.16) claims in its own doc comment that it's exempt from the session-gated middleware surface "by design," mirroring `api/storage`'s already-correct exemption — but `runtime/middleware.ts`'s matcher never actually excluded `api/backup-jobs`, so a request carrying a genuinely valid, unexpired signed token still 303-redirected to `/login` before the route's own token check ever ran. Confirmed the route's authorization is otherwise fully self-contained before touching the matcher (the signed token plus a path-containment check, nothing else read from the request — it doesn't even bind its own `request` parameter), and confirmed via a repo-wide grep for the same HMAC/timing-safe-token pattern that only `api/storage` (already correctly exempted) and this route share it. `api/account/restore-jobs/[id]` looked similar but is a genuinely different case — it reads a middleware-injected `x-sovereign-user-id` trust header and correctly needs to stay inside the matcher. Fixed by adding `api/backup-jobs` to the matcher's negative-lookahead list — safe here for the same reason `api/storage` is safe and unlike `api/instance`: no other route shares this exact prefix, so excluding it carries none of the risk that keeps `api/instance` deliberately inside the matcher. New regression test in `runtime/src/__tests__/middleware-regression.test.ts`'s `middleware matcher` describe block, confirmed to fail against the reverted pre-fix matcher (`git stash`) before being trusted. Verified live twice: against the local SQLite dev server, a request with no session cookie now reaches the route handler itself (404 from token validation) instead of being redirected to `/login`; against a real Postgres-backed Docker stack (rebuilt images, same technique as epic 8.17's own verification), a genuinely completed backup job's signed URL, fetched with an empty cookie jar, returned 200 with the correct `Content-Length`/`Content-Disposition` and decrypted correctly with the real passphrase via the real `backup-encryption.ts`. This affects both epic 8.18's existing Account download flow and epic 8.17's new Console one equally, since both surface the same download URL — not scoped to either task's own UI. Full `pnpm exec vitest run` (3202 passed, up from 3201), `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check`, and `pnpm exec tsx scripts/design-tokens-check.ts` all green. `runtime` bumped `0.98.0` → `0.98.1`. `0.127.0` → `0.128.0` is epic task 8.17 (Console instance backup UI, RFC 0084) — **backup half only**, picked up as a prerequisite for workstream 0023 leg 5 after research found leg 5 (epic 8.41) only needs this half ("extends task 8.17's Console git-push settings"), not the guarded restore flow. Confirmed the split with the developer before writing any code (`AskUserQuestion`): restore + a brand-new maintenance-mode primitive + a brand-new typed-confirmation UI pattern are a separate, not-yet-started follow-on task; epic 8.17 itself stays 📋 until that lands. `bin/backup-restore.ts`'s `backup` command gains a repeatable `--exclude-plugin <id>` flag (`pg_dump --exclude-schema=plugin_<id>` per id — safe and complete alone since epic 8.28 already made every non-platform-type plugin unconditionally schema-isolated, so there's no shared-table-prefix case left) and now writes a small non-secret `sovereign-backup-manifest.json` into the archive (schema version, platform version, dialect, excluded plugins) for the restore half's future compatibility-preview step to read. Confirmed empirically by reading citty's own source that its `defineCommand` args have no "repeatable" type at all — it delegates straight to `node:util`'s `parseArgs` with `multiple` never set for any option, so a repeated flag would just silently keep the last occurrence — fixed by reading `rawArgs` directly instead of trusting citty's own parsed value for this one flag. `runInstanceBackup()` (`runtime/src/backup-run.ts`) now applies the passphrase encryption RFC 0084 requires unconditionally, reusing task 8.18's `backup-passphrase-store.ts`/`backup-encryption.ts` completely unchanged: the CLI writes a raw archive to a sibling temp path, encrypted into `job.archivePath` (extension `.tar.gz.age`, was `.tar.gz`) with the temp file always deleted, including on every error path. Optional git-push reuses `runtime/src/git-backup.ts`'s `pushBackupToGit()` completely unchanged (confirmed generic, zero `@sovereignfs/db`/`userId` coupling), resolving the destination from plain `SV_BACKUP_GIT_REPOSITORY`/`SV_BACKUP_GIT_BRANCH`/`SV_BACKUP_GIT_TOKEN` env vars (HTTPS-token auth only, per RFC 0064's own text) rather than a `plugin_connections` row — a real correction to this task's own original deliverable list, which had said the git token would be "stored via the same encrypted-secret pattern SmtpSettingsForm.tsx establishes"; cross-checking epic 8.41/8.42's clearer, later-written text (both frame `SV_BACKUP_GIT_*` as a plain `docs/self-hosting.md`-documented env var surface) settled this before any UI was built, avoiding a wasted Console-managed-secret implementation. Since there's only one secret here (the passphrase, unlike the per-user git-push's second age-recipient encryption pass), the exact same ciphertext already written to `job.archivePath` is what gets pushed — no second encryption. New Console page `plugins/console/app/backups/` gated on a new `instance:backup` capability (`runtime/src/capabilities.ts`, admin + owner), checked independently in every Server Action per this repo's own server-action-authorization rule. **A real architectural correction made before writing any component code, not after**: Console cannot import `@sovereignfs/db` or `@sovereignfs/manifest` directly — the SDK boundary ESLint rule blocks this for Console too, even though its own `runtime/src`/`@/*` restriction is lifted (confirmed by reading `eslint.config.ts`'s actual two plugin-boundary rule blocks directly, not assumed) — so `enqueueBackupJob`/`getBackupJob`/`listBackupJobs` and the isolated-plugin-list computation (`manifestDatabaseIsolation`) had to move into two new routes, `runtime/app/api/admin/backup-jobs/route.ts` (GET list+metadata, POST enqueue) and its `[id]/route.ts` (GET poll), admin-key authenticated like every other `/api/admin/*` route; Console's own actions call these over HTTP, matching `entitlements/actions.ts`'s already-established pattern exactly. New `listBackupJobs()` in `packages/db/src/platform-db.ts` (no schema change). The job-list UI mirrors task 8.18's `PortabilityPanel.tsx` polling shape exactly (a plain `setInterval`, signed `downloadUrl` embedded once `complete`, download via `window.location.assign`), generalized to poll every in-flight job in the list on one shared timer. Verified via the full existing + new automated suite plus a real `pnpm --filter runtime build` — not optional here, since Console has no `tsconfig.json`/`typecheck` script of its own and was never actually covered by `pnpm typecheck` at all (confirmed directly: `cd plugins/console && npx tsc --noEmit` silently resolves a different, unrelated root tsconfig with no knowledge of Console's own files); only a genuine build compiles its composed route tree, and it compiled clean. The new component tests — written before that build — already caught two real bugs in the new code along the way: `@sovereignfs/ui`'s `EmptyState` component's actual prop is `heading`, not `title` (used wrong in both `page.tsx` and `BackupJobList.tsx`, silently invisible to `tsc` for the reason above, caught only because `getByText('No backups yet')` genuinely found nothing in the rendered output); and a test-fixture bug where a mocked poll response used `id` instead of the real `BackupJobStatus` shape's `jobId`, which made a correctly-working poll loop look permanently stuck in a first draft of the test — both fixed before this shipped. SQLite (sqld) instance backup remains unimplemented (epic 8.16's own accepted limitation, unchanged); age-recipient encryption for the git-push copy is epic 8.41's own explicit future addition, not attempted here. `@sovereignfs/db` bumped `4.12.0` → `4.13.0`; `runtime` bumped `0.97.0` → `0.98.0`; `plugins/console/manifest.json` bumped `0.14.1` → `0.15.0`; no `@sovereignfs/ui` bump — the one new curated icon (`database`, for the new Console nav entry) doesn't require one, matching established precedent. Live end-to-end verification followed the automated suite, run against real Docker/Postgres infrastructure rather than just the dev server: built `p6-auth`/`p6-runtime`/`p6-tools` images, brought the stack up via `docker-compose.prod.yml`+`docker-compose.postgres.yml` plus a scratch Compose override bind-mounting a real local bare git repository into the `runtime` container and setting `SV_BACKUP_GIT_REPOSITORY`/`SV_BACKUP_GIT_TOKEN`, then triggered a real backup via a direct `curl` against the running container's own `POST /api/admin/backup-jobs` — deliberately not a separate `tools`-container script the way task 8.16's own verification worked, since `storeBackupPassphrase()`'s in-memory store is anchored on `globalThis` and cannot cross OS process boundaries, so the passphrase has to reach the exact process running the worker — with one plugin excluded (`fs.sovereign.warden`) and `pushToGit: true`. Confirmed live: the job reached `complete` with a nonzero `sizeBytes`; a correctly-tagged orphan commit landed in the bare git repo; the archive (read directly off the host-mounted `./backups/` volume, working around the download-route bug below) decrypted successfully with the real `runtime/src/backup-encryption.ts` `decrypt()` and the right passphrase, and failed cleanly with a wrong one; the embedded `sovereign-backup-manifest.json` matched exactly (`excludedPlugins: ["fs.sovereign.warden"]`, correct platform version/dialect); and `pg_restore --list` against the underlying dump confirmed the `plugin_fs_sovereign_warden` schema was completely absent, by direct comparison against task 8.16's own earlier verification output where that same schema was present in an unexcluded backup — proving `--exclude-plugin` genuinely works end-to-end, not just at the CLI-argv level the unit tests already covered. Also confirmed directly inside the running container (`docker exec`) that both `SOVEREIGN_BACKUP_WORKER_ENABLED` and the new `SV_BACKUP_GIT_*` vars reach it from `docker-compose.prod.yml` as configured, not merely declared in `.env.example`. **A real, pre-existing bug in task 8.16's own already-shipped code was found during this verification and is deliberately not fixed here**: the signed backup-download route (`/api/backup-jobs/[jobId]/download/[token]`) 303-redirects to `/login` instead of serving the file — its own doc comment claims a `runtime/middleware.ts` matcher exemption "by design," modeling itself on the `api/storage` route (which genuinely is excluded), but `api/backup-jobs` was never actually added to that exclusion list, confirmed live via `curl -v` showing the redirect. This affects epic 8.18's existing Account download flow equally, not just this task's new Console one, so it's a shared-middleware fix rather than a backup-triggering one — flagged separately as a follow-up rather than folded into this PR. `0.126.0` → `0.127.0` is epic task 8.16 (backup job infrastructure & signed download delivery, RFC 0084), picked up as a prerequisite for workstream 0023 legs 5–6 after those legs turned out blocked on it. The schema, worker orchestration, encryption helper, download route, and notification wiring were already built and tested; the one genuinely incomplete deliverable — instance-scope jobs could not succeed in the documented production Docker deployment — is closed here, for the **Postgres dialect only**. Root cause: `runInstanceBackup()` (`runtime/src/backup-run.ts`) spawns `pnpm sv backup`, but the `runner` image has no `bin/`/`scripts/`/`tsx`/full `node_modules` to run it. Fixed by extracting the `backup`/`restore` command bodies out of `bin/sv.ts` into a shared `bin/backup-restore.ts` (zero behavior change for `pnpm sv backup`), registering them from a new minimal entrypoint `bin/sv-backup-cli.ts`, and `tsup`-bundling that into `runtime/dist-cli/sv-backup-cli.js` (new `bin/tsup.config.ts`, `pnpm build:cli`, wired into the Dockerfile's `app-builder` stage and copied into `runner`) — `citty`/`consola` are both genuinely zero-dependency so the bundle needs no native addon. `runInstanceBackup()` now spawns adaptively: the bundled CLI via plain `node` when present (production `runner`), falling back to the unchanged `pnpm sv backup` otherwise (native `pnpm dev`/`tools`) — exactly one of the two always runs. A real architecture alternative — moving worker execution to a separate `tools`-based persistent service instead of bundling into `runner` — was evaluated and rejected: the portability export-hook registry (`runtime/src/portability/registry.ts`) is populated by request-scoped plugin code as pages load, never at boot, so a headless worker process that never serves an HTTP page would always see an empty registry and silently produce user-scope backups missing every plugin's data. Two more already-shipped-code production bugs were found and fixed along the way, neither previously documented: **`runner` had no `git` binary at all** — confirmed empirically (`docker run --rm node:24-alpine sh -c "git --version"` → not found) — meaning workstream 0023 legs 3–4's git-push/restore-fetch code (`runtime/src/git-backup.ts`) could not have worked in real production either; and **`docker-compose.prod.yml` mounted `runtime`'s `/app/backups` read-only** while both `runInstanceBackup()` and `runUserBackup()` write archives to exactly that path, and `SOVEREIGN_BACKUP_WORKER_ENABLED` was entirely absent from the `runtime` service's `environment:` block, so setting it in `.env` had no effect on the container regardless of anything else. All three fixed: `builder`/`runner` now `apk add git postgresql16-client` (version-pinned to `docker-compose.postgres.yml`'s `postgres:16-alpine`); the `./backups` mount lost its `:ro`; `SOVEREIGN_BACKUP_WORKER_ENABLED` is wired through. `runner`'s non-root user also gained a pinned UID/GID (`1001:1001`, previously auto-assigned) so operators have a stable value to `chown` the host `./backups` directory to on upgrade (`docs/upgrade.md`). SQLite/sqld instance backup remains explicitly unimplemented — `sv backup`/`sv restore`'s SQLite branch still correctly refuses, matching `docs/research/0017-sqld-backup-and-restore.md`'s own explicit RFC gate; not attempted here, since it's a missing feature, not a Docker-topology problem. Verified live end-to-end against real Docker images, not just unit tests: built the actual `p6-auth`/`p6-runtime`/`p6-tools` images via `docker compose -f docker-compose.prod.yml -f docker-compose.postgres.yml build`, brought up `postgres`+`auth`+`runtime`, and confirmed inside the real running `runtime` container that `git`/`pg_dump`/`pg_restore` are all present (`git version 2.54.0`, `pg_dump (PostgreSQL) 16.15`, `pg_restore (PostgreSQL) 16.15`) while `pnpm`/`tsx` are correctly absent (`which pnpm tsx` found neither — the exact gap this task closes, reproduced as a live baseline before trusting the fix) and that `id` reports the pinned `uid=1001(nextjs) gid=1001(nodejs)`. Enqueued a real `backup_jobs` row (`scope: 'instance'`) directly via `enqueueBackupJob()` from a `tools` container on the same Docker network — no Console UI exists yet to do this through; that's task 8.17 — and watched it transition `queued` → `complete` in under 2 seconds with a nonzero `sizeBytes` (16208). The real archive landed on the host `./backups` bind mount and `pg_restore --list` against its extracted `sovereign.pgdump` confirmed a genuinely valid, uncorrupted custom-format dump — 258 real TOC entries spanning the `public`, `sovereign_auth`, and `plugin_fs_sovereign_warden` schemas (this dev instance's own migrated data, correctly captured whole). Separately proved `tools`'s own long-advertised-but-previously-broken `sv backup`/`sv restore` commands now fully round-trip against the same Postgres overlay end to end (`pnpm sv backup` succeeded, `pnpm sv restore` succeeded, `GET /api/health` still returned `{"status":"ok"}` afterward) — closing the second bug this task found (`tools` was missing `pg_dump`/`pg_restore` too, despite already advertising these commands as directly runnable). Every container/volume/network created for this verification was torn down afterward (`docker compose down -v`), leaving the pre-existing native `pnpm dev` `sovereign-sqld-dev` container/volume untouched throughout. Along the way, `runtime/dist-cli/` (the new build output, gitignored) turned out to not be excluded from ESLint's scope — a stray local build briefly failed `pnpm lint` with 61 errors against third-party-inlined bundle code; fixed by adding it to `eslint.config.ts`'s ignore list alongside the other generated-output entries. Full `pnpm exec vitest run` (3168 passed, 233 skipped `.pg.test.ts` files unaffected), `pnpm typecheck` (all 29 packages), `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check`, and `pnpm exec tsx scripts/design-tokens-check.ts` all green. No `@sovereignfs/db` version bump — this task touched no schema. `runtime` bumped `0.96.0` → `0.97.0`. Marking epic 8.16 ✅ reflects the backup infrastructure being complete and proven end to end for the Postgres dialect — an instance owner still cannot trigger a backup from the UI (task 8.17, not started), and workstream 0023 legs 5–6 remain blocked on that, not fully unblocked by this task alone. `0.125.0` → `0.126.0` is workstream 0023 leg 4 (epic task 8.40) — the gate leg: a user lists, fetches, and decrypts their own git-backed backups entirely client-side, landing the result in the existing unmodified `POST /api/account/import`. `backup_jobs` gained a `kind` column (`'backup' | 'restore-fetch'`, `NOT NULL DEFAULT 'backup'`, both dialects) since `scope` alone can't distinguish a restore-fetch job from a real backup (both are always `scope: 'user'`). `runtime/src/git-backup.ts` (task 8.39's shared module) gained `listBackupTags()` (`git ls-remote --tags`, parsed via a new `parseBackupTag()`, the exact inverse of `backupTagFor()`) and `fetchBackupBlob()` (shallow `git fetch --depth=1` of one tag, then `git show <tag>:<filename>` to read the ciphertext blob with no checkout), both sharing a `buildGitCredentialEnv()` helper factored out of task 8.39's own inline push logic. A new `runRestoreFetch()` (`runtime/src/backup-run.ts`) is `runBackupJob()`'s dispatch target for `kind: 'restore-fetch'` — resolves the destination the same way the push step does, fetches the chosen tag's blob, and writes it verbatim to the job's `archivePath`, never decrypting or inspecting it. Three new routes under `runtime/app/api/account/restore-jobs/`: `GET tags` (sync listing), `POST` (enqueue, same fail-fast destination-ownership check as task 8.39's push route), and `GET [id]` (poll, scoped to `kind: 'restore-fetch'` in addition to the existing owner/scope checks). The download step reuses task 8.16's existing signed `/api/backup-jobs/[jobId]/download/[token]` route completely unmodified — it only ever proves "this job's archive may be downloaded," with no notion of job `kind`. New `plugins/account/app/_components/GitRestorePanel.tsx`: select a destination, list its tags, pick one, supply the backup key via a `FileDropzone`, and restore — the identity is read via `File.text()` inside the restore handler's own function scope only, never assigned to React state, re-prompting on a second attempt rather than remembering it. Decrypt uses `age-encryption`'s `Decrypter` against the downloaded response's own `body` (a `ReadableStream`), so the browser processes ciphertext as it arrives rather than buffering the whole download first — the streaming construction this task's own deliverables call for. **Correction to this task's own original deliverable list:** no `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` CSP addition was made or is needed — task 8.37 already established `age-encryption` is pure JS, not WASM; this task's own deliverable bullet had simply never been updated to match until now.
