Verification for the original PR caught two real, unrelated-to-the-feature gaps at push time: `runtime`'s typecheck failed on missing `runtime/generated/*` (this working tree had never run `pnpm generate` that session) and `pnpm design:tokens:check` initially missed the new CSS file's undefined-token usage entirely, since it scans `git ls-files` and the file was still untracked on the first local run — both fixed (the second via the script's existing `TOKEN_ALLOWLIST` escape hatch, already used for three other JS-computed custom properties). This follow-up's own doc pass added a `docs/design-system.md` "Swipe stack (card triage)" section matching the carousel section's depth; the other docs referencing `SwipableMobileCarousel` (`docs/plugin-development.md`, `docs/epics/design-system.md`, `docs/architecture-rules.md`) were checked and deliberately left alone — each is a point-in-time record of that specific task's or example plugin's own scope, not a living component index, so retrofitting them would misrepresent what those tasks actually built. `0.110.0` → `0.111.0` is workstream 0022 leg 5 (task 13.21), the final leg of the Console shell/`ThreeColumnLayout` conversion: `/console/oauth-clients` gains a 3rd detail column, but unlike legs 2-4 there was no dialog to replace — reading `OAuthClientsClient.tsx` (per this leg's own instruction, not assumed) confirmed every field on a client card (name, ID, redirect URIs, status) was already visible inline, and the only "reveal" state (a freshly created/rotated secret) is a page-level, one-time-only banner unrelated to selection. Escalated per the workstream's own "do not proceed if" clause rather than deciding unilaterally; the developer chose to add the pane anyway for visual consistency with the other three converted pages, not because it reveals new information — its only real job is relocating the Rotate secret/Revoke buttons off the card. Implemented with `useSearchParams()` called directly inside `OAuthClientsClient.tsx` (a new `OAuthClientDetailPane.tsx` component) rather than a `page.tsx` `searchParams` prop, since this page — unlike Users/Groups/Plugins — has no server-side data fetch to thread one through; everything here is client-driven against `/api/auth/oauth2/*`. `<Link href="?client=<id>">` still keeps selection linkable, matching legs 2-4's convention. The revealed-secret banner was deliberately left exactly where it already rendered (page-level, not pane-scoped) rather than moved for cosmetic symmetry — it's a security-sensitive, already-well-tested "shown once, unrecoverable" flow not worth touching for this leg. A mobile-only fallback (`.cardManageMobile`, the same mechanism legs 3-4 already use) keeps Rotate/Revoke reachable on mobile, which has no detail column to select into. **Found and fixed a real, pre-existing bug live while checking the mobile viewport, not assumed correct from prior legs' code review alone**: legs 3-4's `.cardChevron` class sets `display: none` to hide the desktop-only selection chevron below the mobile breakpoint, but at equal CSS specificity against `Icon.module.css`'s own `.root { display: inline-block }`, which one wins is decided by Next.js's per-route CSS bundle order — not something either stylesheet declares or controls, and not guaranteed consistent across routes. It happened to resolve correctly on Groups (leg 3) and Plugins (leg 4) — confirmed by re-checking, not just recalling — but silently failed here (the chevron rendered visible on a 375px viewport too, though harmlessly inert since row selection itself has no mobile UI to select into). Fixed by qualifying the selector as `svg.cardChevron` (element+class specificity) everywhere it's used, in `plugins/console/app/console.module.css` — strictly beats a plain class selector regardless of bundle order. Verified via computed-style inspection at 375px both before (`display: block`) and after (`display: none`) the fix, on the live dev server. Verified live end-to-end on both desktop and mobile beyond just that fix: registering a client, selecting it to open the desktop pane, rotating its secret from within the pane (the page-level banner shows the new secret correctly, pane stays open and selected throughout), revoking it (card and pane both disappear together, matching leg 3's delete precedent — no manual redirect needed), and the mobile card's own Rotate/Revoke buttons working identically to before this leg. `plugins/console/manifest.json` bumped `0.9.0` → `0.10.0`. This completes the Console shell/`ThreeColumnLayout` conversion itself (legs 1-5); leg 6 (an unrelated stale in-repo docs-server reference cleanup, folded into this workstream by direct request) remains independent and not yet started. `0.109.0` → `0.110.0` is workstream 0021 leg 4 (epic task 22.11 — Warden composer redesign, RFC 0063 third revision), closing out the workstream: `ModelPickerPopover` (new) replaces the inline `<select>` with a `Popover` grouped by provider (local model first, then each connected provider's own group), mirroring `ModelsView`'s own `displayLabel`/grouping logic (duplicated, not imported — that component's version is entangled with search/badge concerns this popover doesn't need), with a footer linking into Settings → Providers/Models. Incognito moved from the chat header's `Toggle` + label into the composer toolbar as an icon `Button` (`eye-off`, wrapped in `Tooltip`, `aria-pressed` reflecting state); the chat header itself, its "Manage providers"/"Manage models" links, and the disabled "Web search — Soon" toggle are all removed outright, not hidden. The centered-vs-docked composer positioning (RFC 0063 §12) needed more than "swap between two JSX trees on `turns.length === 0`": a first implementation nested the composer inside a wrapper only rendered in the empty-state branch, which meant React unmounted and remounted the entire composer subtree — including the incognito toggle button — every time the empty/non-empty boundary was crossed (sending the first message, or toggling incognito while an existing conversation has messages, since incognito's own context starts empty). Caught by a unit test asserting the incognito toggle survives a send-triggered centered→docked transition as the _same_ DOM node, which failed against the first implementation; fixed by keeping the composer (and the `EmptyState`/message-list branch) as permanently-stable siblings of `.chat` in both states, with only a `chatCentered` modifier class (`justify-content: center`) changing, never the tree shape. Verified live end to end against the same dev database leg 3 used, re-adding (then re-removing) the same OpenRouter test provider and model: the centered "Ask Warden anything" composer, the model picker popover's correct provider grouping and a working round trip through "Manage providers" and back via the breadcrumb's "Chat" link, the incognito toggle's visible pressed state and "Incognito chat" empty state, and the old header links/toggle-based incognito/disabled web-search toggle all confirmed absent from the DOM. `plugins/warden`'s own test suite grew from 278 to 291 tests (13 new, covering `ModelPickerPopover`'s grouping/selection/footer and the composer's no-remount guarantee); full existing suite, `pnpm --filter runtime typecheck`, `pnpm --filter @sovereignfs/ui typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check`, and `pnpm design:tokens:check` all green. `plugins/warden` manifest `0.8.0` → `0.9.0`. Full account in workstream 0021's own changelog and the epic file's task 22.11 completion note. `0.108.0` → `0.109.0` is workstream 0022 leg 4 (task 13.20): Console's Plugins/Apps page (`/console/plugins`) gains a 3rd `ThreeColumnLayout` column on desktop — selecting a row shows a detail pane (permissions list, access-policy select, selected users/groups grant lists) that replaces the old `PluginAccessDialog` dialog there, mirroring legs 2/3's Users/Groups conversions. `PluginAccessDialog`'s body was extracted into a shared `PluginAccessFields.tsx`, reused by both a slimmed, mobile-only `PluginAccessDialog` and the new `PluginDetailPane.tsx` — the same split legs 2/3 established. Deliberately narrower scope than those two legs, though: Activate/Toggle enable-disable/Open/Remove stay row-level actions rather than moving into the pane too, since "Open" especially is a frequent, low-risk action an admin shouldn't need to open a detail pane to reach, and this leg's own technical note (workstream 0022) names only `PluginAccessDialog`'s content as moving — a real scope decision, documented rather than assumed. A row is selectable (`isSelectable()` in `PluginsTable.tsx`) under exactly the condition `PluginAccessDialog` used to render (`!row.isChrome && status is enabled/disabled`) — chrome plugins (Account/Console/Launcher) and inactive/incompatible rows get no selection `<Link>` or chevron, matching what was and wasn't manageable before. Selection lives in `?plugin=<id>`, resolved server-side in `page.tsx` against the full (unfiltered) row list — `PluginsTable`'s own filter/search/examples-toggle state is local, client-only React state that was never URL-synced even before this leg, so it's a genuinely separate concern from the server-computed selection and survives the parent Server Component's re-render untouched (same component instance, same position in the tree, only its props change). This was the exact coexistence risk workstream 0022's own leg 4 technical note flagged in advance ("changing a filter while a plugin is selected shouldn't silently clear the selection unless the selected plugin actually drops out of the filtered set") — verified live against the real dev server, not assumed: selected a row (Warden, the one non-chrome plugin already enabled in this dev database), typed a search query that filtered it out of the visible table entirely, and confirmed the detail pane — including its live-editable access-policy select, which was also exercised (switched to "Selected users", confirmed the picker/grant-list sections appeared, switched back to "Everyone" to leave the dev database clean) — stayed fully intact and functional the whole time; clearing the filter brought the row back into view with its `.trSelected` highlight still correctly applied. Also verified the Close link clears `?plugin=` back to the full-width table, and that the mobile kebab menu's "Access" entry still opens the (unslimmed-behavior) `PluginAccessDialog` completely unaffected — Plugins has no separate mobile/desktop render fork the way Users does, so this dialog's own controlled `open`/`onOpenChange` API (already built for exactly this consumer) needed no changes at all. `pnpm --filter runtime build` confirmed `/console/plugins` compiles cleanly. `plugins/console/manifest.json` bumped `0.8.0` → `0.9.0`. `0.107.0` → `0.108.0` is workstream 0022 leg 3 (task 13.19): Console's Groups page (`/console/groups`) gains a 3rd `ThreeColumnLayout` column on desktop — selecting a card shows a detail pane (details form, member list/picker, danger zone) that replaces the old `ManageGroupDialog` dialog there, mirroring leg 2's Users conversion exactly. `ManageGroupDialog`'s body was extracted into a shared `GroupDetailFields.tsx`, reused by both a slimmed, mobile-only `ManageGroupDialog` and the new `GroupDetailPane.tsx` — the same `UserCapabilitiesFields`/`UserDetailPane` split leg 2 established. `CreateGroupDialog` was confirmed live to need no change, matching `InviteDialog`'s precedent exactly rather than assuming it. Leg 2's `.userDetail*` CSS classes were generalized to `.detail*` (dropping the `user` prefix) now that a second consumer exists, reused as-is by `GroupDetailPane` — `UserDetailPane.tsx` was updated to the renamed classes in the same change, no visual difference. Groups has no separate mobile/desktop render fork the way Users does (one shared card grid handles both) — the desktop selection `<Link>`/chevron indicator and the mobile `ManageGroupDialog` trigger are each CSS-gated to their own side of the existing 768/769px breakpoint (`.cardChevron`/`.cardManageMobile`, the latter using `display: contents` so the wrapper itself doesn't disturb `.card`'s flex layout) rather than duplicating Users' separate-component split — a simpler fit for Groups' smaller surface, and the card stays fully `?group=<id>`-linkable either way. Verified live end-to-end against the real dev server, not just the type system: creating a group, selecting it to open the desktop detail pane, editing its name/description, the empty members state, the delete-confirmation flow (both the card and the detail pane disappear correctly once the group is gone, no manual redirect needed since the next render's data fetch simply no longer contains it), the 900px detail-collapse breakpoint, and the mobile `Manage` dialog opening completely unaffected. `pnpm --filter runtime build` confirmed `/console/groups` compiles cleanly (composed plugin directories are excluded from `runtime`'s own `tsc --noEmit` scope, per this file's own architecture-rules cross-reference). `plugins/console/manifest.json` bumped `0.7.0` → `0.8.0`. `0.106.0` → `0.107.0` is workstream 0021 leg 3 (epic task 22.10 — Warden sidebar UI, RFC 0063 third revision): a collapsible two-column layout (`WardenLayoutShell`, new) plus the sidebar itself (`WardenSidebar`, new) turn task 22.8's session data model into something a user can actually see and act on — pinned (≤5, sorted by `pinnedAt` descending) and recent (≤10, sorted by `lastActiveAt` descending) session groups, "+ New," a per-row overflow menu (rename/pin/unpin/delete, no "recently deleted" recovery, matching incognito's own no-recovery posture), and a Settings entry point pinned outside the scrollable list. `WardenLayoutShell` bypasses `@sovereignfs/ui`'s `ThreeColumnLayout` entirely when collapsed rather than passing `sidebarWidth={0}` — that component's `.sidebar` slot always carries a `border-right`, which a zero-width flex item still renders as a visible 1px hairline down the left edge of the screen, not a real "collapsed" look; collapse state reads `localStorage` only inside `useEffect`, defaulting to expanded, per this file's own hydration-mismatch rule for client components reading browser globals. `WardenSidebar` itself is purely presentational — `app/page.tsx` does the pinned/recent split and sort, so the component never re-derives grouping. The one behavior change needed to keep the sidebar's session list and the composer's active session from ever disagreeing about which session is "open" (this leg's own "do not proceed if" condition, and workstream 0021's own for the whole feature): `ChatView.tsx` now calls `router.replace('/warden?session=' + id)` once a brand-new session's id comes back from the chat API, on top of its existing `x-warden-session-id` header handling. Two new curated icons added to `@sovereignfs/ui`'s `Icon` set (`panel-left`, `pin`) via `scripts/icon-list.ts` + `pnpm generate:icons` (93 total, up from a stale-but-unrelated "52 bundled icons" count found and fixed as a drive-by in `DesignSystemOverview.stories.tsx` — `Icon.stories.tsx`'s own `AllIcons` story derives from `ICONS` directly, so no manual per-icon story edit was needed for either). Verified live end to end, but not against the first account tried: `owner@sovereign.local` (this same shared dev instance's existing seeded account) turned out to carry a pre-existing, unrelated data-corruption gap from an earlier session — a `plugin_secrets` row encrypted under a `SOVEREIGN_VAULT_KEY` no longer configured in this environment, throwing `Unsupported state or unable to authenticate data` straight out of `decryptSecretValue` on every single Warden page load, confirmed via server logs to originate in unchanged `_lib/model-discovery.ts`/`sdk-host.ts` code, not a regression from this leg. Not fixed as part of this leg (out of scope, no `plugin_secrets` row for `fs.sovereign.warden` should have survived a lost vault key in a healthy instance) — verification instead moved to a second seeded account (`user@sovereign.local`, `pnpm sv seed`, per direct developer authorization for this dev database: "you can create any number of users... do whatever you want with the database to achieve the goal"). That account needed a real `SOVEREIGN_VAULT_KEY` set in the local `.env` (leg 2's own entry below already flagged this exact env gap; `openssl rand -base64 32`, gitignored, not committed) and a real provider to exercise session creation — added OpenRouter's actual `https://openrouter.ai/api/v1` with a deliberately-fake key, whose `/v1/models` endpoint is public and returned a real 424-model catalog (only the chat-completion call itself needs a valid key), enough to create real sessions end to end without ever needing a genuinely reachable model backend. Confirmed live: the two-column layout and empty state render correctly; a session is created server-side on the very first send attempt regardless of whether the model call itself succeeds (`createSession` runs before the provider dispatch in `api/chat/route.ts`), and reappears in the sidebar on reload even after a failed send; rename commits on blur (the iOS-safe path — `Enter` alone did not reliably commit under this session's own browser-automation tooling, a tooling quirk rather than an app bug, confirmed by blur committing correctly every time) with the input starting empty rather than pre-filled with the "New chat" _display_ fallback; pin/unpin move a row between the Pinned/Recent groups live; delete opens a name-specific confirm dialog and correctly navigates to `/warden` when the deleted session was the active one, or just refreshes when it wasn't; pinning a 6th session is rejected with a clear toast ("You can pin up to 5 sessions — unpin one first") while the sidebar itself stays correctly unchanged, not silently corrupted. All six test sessions and the test provider created for this verification were deleted again through the app's own UI before finishing, leaving the account clean. `plugins/warden`'s own test suite grew from 256 to 278 tests (22 new, covering `WardenSidebar`'s groups/rename/pin/delete and `WardenLayoutShell`'s collapse/persistence); full existing suite, `pnpm --filter runtime typecheck`, `pnpm --filter @sovereignfs/ui typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, and `pnpm format:check` all green. `plugins/warden` manifest `0.7.0` → `0.8.0`. Full account in workstream 0021's own changelog and the epic file's task 22.10 completion note. `0.105.0` → `0.106.0` is workstream 0022 leg 2 (task 13.18): Console's Users page (`/console/users`) gains a 3rd `ThreeColumnLayout` column on desktop — selecting a row shows a detail pane (role assignment, capabilities, status actions) that replaces the old `CapabilitiesButton` dialog and per-row icon-button row. Selection lives in `?user=<id>` (looked up against the full fetched member list, not just the current `?page=` slice, so it resolves regardless of which page is showing); closing uses `<Link replace>`, selecting uses a plain `<Link>` — the same select/close asymmetry `sovereign-tasks` already documents for its own `?task=<id>` pattern. Built via a small reusable mechanism rather than a Users-specific one, since legs 3-5 need the identical shape: a new `ConsoleDetailPaneContext`/`useConsoleDetailPane` pair (`plugins/console/app/_lib/detail-pane.tsx`) mirrors `@sovereignfs/ui`'s `Dialog`/`useOverlaySecondRow` pattern exactly — a descendant page registers detail content via a new `ConsoleDetailSlot` client component (so a Server Component page doesn't need its own `'use client'` directive), and `ConsoleLayout` owns the actual `ThreeColumnLayout` 3rd-column slot, rendering whatever's currently registered. Below 900px — narrower than the 768px mobile breakpoint, where `ResponsiveSurface` already forks away from this tree entirely — the detail column collapses to sidebar+table only (`DETAIL_COLLAPSE_BREAKPOINT_PX` in `layout.tsx`, mirroring `sovereign-tasks`' `DesktopTasksShell` precedent exactly), verified live via viewport resize. `CapabilitiesButton.tsx`'s dialog content was split into a shared `UserCapabilitiesFields` (the actual grant/revoke list) and a thin button+`Dialog` wrapper kept only for `UserCard.tsx`'s mobile card list, which has no detail column to render into — confirmed unaffected live, including its own capabilities dialog still opening and loading correctly on a real mobile viewport. The table's role column became read-only (`RoleBadge`) on desktop; actual role assignment moved exclusively into the detail pane. `InviteDialog` was confirmed to stay an untouched plain dialog, resolving the open Decisions-locked question leg 2 was the first to actually hit. Verified end-to-end in a real browser against the dev server (not just the type system): selecting a user shows the detail pane with working role/capabilities/actions, the owner account shows a protected no-actions state, closing clears the pane back to a full-width table, and a hard reload of a `?user=<id>` URL renders the detail pane directly. A full `pnpm --filter runtime build` was run for the same reason leg 1's entry names — `runtime/tsconfig.json` excludes composed plugin directories from its own `tsc --noEmit` scope, so that standalone check alone would not have caught a type error in these actual route files. `plugins/console/manifest.json` bumped `0.6.0` → `0.7.0`. Legs 3-5 (Groups/Plugins/External-clients → the same detail-column treatment) are separate, not-yet-started tasks. `0.104.0` → `0.105.0` is workstream 0022 leg 1 (tasks 9.28, 13.17): Console (`plugins/console`) moves off `shell: "overlay"` back to `shell: "default"` — a full reversal of task 2.5's original migration, deliberate and scoped to Console only (Account is untouched, still `shell: "overlay"`) — and its `layout.tsx`/`page.tsx` are rebuilt around a new `packages/ui` component, `NavList` (task 9.28): a vertical list of icon+label rows, optionally grouped under section headers, in two variants — `static` (active row highlighted, no chevron, for a persistent desktop sidebar) and `drilldown` (trailing chevron on every row, no active state, for a native-Settings-app-style mobile index). `NavList` is framework-agnostic like the existing `NavTabs` — no `next/navigation`/`next/link` import; active state and navigation come from the consumer via `item.active` and an optional `renderLink` prop — confirmed by reading `NavTabs.tsx` before writing `NavList.tsx`, since the epic task's own description as originally drafted had assumed `NavList` would call `usePathname()` internally, which would have broken that established convention; caught before any code shipped. Desktop Console now renders `@sovereignfs/ui`'s `ThreeColumnLayout` (`NavList variant="static"` sidebar + a main column), with a `data-plugin-fullbleed` root so the two columns scroll independently instead of the whole page scrolling as one unit (mirrors `sovereign-tasks`' `DesktopTasksShell`/Warden's own fullbleed pages) — `PageContainer` is dropped from that desktop tree; new `.sidebar`/`.main` CSS gutters replace it. Mobile has no persistent sidebar: the bare `/console` route (Overview) forks via `ResponsiveSurface` into a `NavList variant="drilldown"` index — grouped exactly like the desktop sidebar (People: Users/Groups; Apps: Plugins/Entitlements/External clients; Configuration: Settings/Identity; Monitoring: Health/Activity; Communication: Broadcast — new module `plugins/console/app/_lib/sections.ts`, one source of truth for both surfaces, plus its own `activeConsoleSectionId()` longest-href-prefix-match helper, unit tested) — and every other section gets a plain `‹ Console` back link above its content instead; mobile keeps `PageContainer`'s normal gutter, unlike desktop. The dead horizontal tab strip (`.nav`/`.navLink`/`.navLinkActive`/`.header`/`.headerHiddenOnMobile` and their mobile media queries) and the now-unused `ConsoleNavLink.tsx`/`useOverlaySecondRow` usage are deleted outright, not left dead. `pnpm generate` confirmed the `@modal/(.)console/*` interception tree (the overlay Dialog's composed copy) is gone; a full `pnpm --filter runtime build` was also run specifically because `runtime/tsconfig.json` excludes composed plugin directories from its own `tsc --noEmit` scope (`docs/architecture-rules.md`) — the standalone workspace typecheck would not have caught a type error in Console's actual route files, and the real Next.js build compiled every `/console/*` route cleanly. Docs updated in the same leg: `docs/architecture-rules.md`'s overlay-size bullet narrows to Account only; `docs/plugins/console.md`'s manifest table/JSON/explanatory note now describe `shell: "default"`; `docs/epics/platform-shell.md` tasks 2.5 and 2.19 (not 2.20 — a different, unrelated rejected task, caught and corrected before this landed) get an "Update" callout rather than a rewrite of their own completed-task history; RFC 0001 gets the same treatment RFC 0037/0067 already modeled for their own later-relocated feature. `packages/ui` bumped `0.74.0` → `0.75.0` (minor, additive `NavList` component/exports); `plugins/console/manifest.json` bumped `0.5.8` → `0.6.0` (manifest-only, per this repo's plugin-versioning convention — its `package.json` stays pinned at `0.0.0`). Legs 2–5 of the same workstream (Users/Groups/Plugins/External-clients → selection-driven detail columns) are separate, not-yet-started tasks. `0.103.0` → `0.104.0` is workstream 0021 leg 2 (epic task 22.9 — Warden settings consolidation, RFC 0063 third revision): a new `/warden/settings` route (General/Providers/Models tabs, built on `@sovereignfs/ui`'s `Tabs` — confirmed the only tab component with any real consumer anywhere in this repo, `NavTabs` has none) replaces the standalone `/warden/providers` and `/warden/models` routes, which are removed outright with no redirect (per direct developer instruction, given how little real usage exists this early). The active tab syncs to `?tab=` via `router.replace`, not just local state, so leg 4's composer model-picker popover can later deep-link straight to `/warden/settings?tab=providers`/`?tab=models`. `ProvidersView`/`ModelsView` needed zero content changes — only their page-level breadcrumb/header wrapper moved into the new tabbed page. Three RFC 0063 open questions resolved at implementation time: default model for a new session is a new `warden_user_settings` table (one row per user, `defaultModelKey` nullable, get-or-create); retention is a manual, on-demand "delete sessions inactive for over N days" action that excludes pinned sessions unconditionally and is deliberately not a scheduled job (confirmed `manifest.json` declares no `sdk.schedules` capability), fixed at 2 delete queries via `inArray` matching task 22.7's own pattern rather than a per-session loop; export deep-links to the existing account-wide `/account/data` flow rather than adding a new Warden-only download mechanism. A `grep` for the literal old route strings (not assumed complete from memory) found two link sites needing repointing that this leg's own plan hadn't explicitly named — `ChatView.tsx`'s three links and `SetupPrompt.tsx`'s one — both fixed; `pnpm generate`'s `assertNoOrphanedRouteDirectories()` check confirmed no stale composed route directory survived deleting the old page directories. Verified live end to end against a real logged-in session — not just unit tests: since self-registration is disabled on this dev instance and the existing seeded test accounts (`user@sovereign.local` etc.) already existed among this database's real users, logged in with `pnpm sv seed`'s own idempotent seed (re-run with `SOVEREIGN_SEED_ALLOW_PROD=true` per direct developer authorization for this specific dev database) rather than creating a new one from scratch; confirmed all three tabs render, tab clicks update the URL and swap content correctly, the retention action makes a real server round trip (`POST /warden/settings 200` in server logs) and correctly reports "No inactive sessions to delete" for a fresh account, and both the chat page's first-run `SetupPrompt` and its header links resolve to the new `/warden/settings?tab=...` URLs. The Providers tab's own add-provider flow surfaced a pre-existing, unrelated `SOVEREIGN_VAULT_KEY`-unset environment gap (confirmed via server logs to be `sdk.secrets`' own vault requirement, thrown from unchanged `_lib/providers.ts` code) — not a regression from this leg; the generic error-fallback path handled it correctly regardless. Full existing `plugins/warden` suite (245 tests, including new coverage for `user-settings.ts` and `deleteInactiveSessions`) plus `pnpm --filter runtime typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, and `pnpm format:check` all green. `plugins/warden` manifest `0.6.0` → `0.7.0`. `0.102.2` → `0.103.0` is workstream 0021 leg 1 (epic task 22.8 — Warden sessions data model and API, RFC 0063 third revision): `warden_sessions` replaces `warden_conversation`'s single-conversation-per-user model with multiple named, pinnable sessions, via a clean-slate migration (drop and recreate, no backfill of existing rows, per direct developer instruction) rather than an in-place `ALTER`. `_lib/sessions.ts` (replacing `conversations.ts`) adds full CRUD — create (lazy, only on a session's first send, never on a future "+ New" click), list, rename, pin (max 5, server-side enforced, a 6th attempt rejected rather than silently auto-evicting the oldest pin)/unpin, delete — every session-scoped function ownership-checked via a private `getOwnSession()` that re-verifies `userId` after the read, not just via a query filter, so a guessed/leaked session id belonging to a different user never returns data. Two decisions were made at implementation time that RFC 0063 had deliberately left open: title generation uses no model call at all (`deriveTitle()` derives one synchronously from the session's first user message — trimmed, whitespace-collapsed, truncated to 60 chars — rather than spending a real LLM request, extra latency/cost/failure-mode, on something the user can always rename), and `drizzle-kit generate` could not run non-interactively for a schema change this size (a table rename plus new columns plus a sibling-table column rename triggers its own "renamed or created new?" TTY prompt, unavailable in this environment) — migration `0002` was hand-authored for both dialects to exactly match drizzle-kit's own output format (a straight drop/create pair, per the clean-slate decision), then verified two ways: `drizzle-kit check` confirms the hand-written snapshot/journal metadata is internally consistent, and a follow-up `drizzle-kit generate` against the updated `schema.ts`/`schema.postgres.ts` reports "No schema changes, nothing to migrate" — proving the hand-authored snapshot exactly matches what drizzle-kit itself would have produced. The chat API (`app/api/chat/route.ts`) gained an optional `sessionId` in its persisted-mode request shape; a lazily-created session's id comes back via a new `x-warden-session-id` response header, since a streaming body has no other place to carry it — incognito's request shape is untouched, since incognito stays a single global scratch context orthogonal to session selection, never referencing a session id at all. `app/page.tsx`/`ChatView.tsx` were adapted to keep today's single-thread UX working unchanged end to end (auto-selecting the most recently active session, or `null` for a brand-new user) since the sidebar that lets a user actually switch between sessions doesn't ship until leg 3 (task 22.10). Portability's export `schemaVersion` bumped `1` → `2` since the shape genuinely changed (one flat message list → sessions grouped with their own messages); `provideDelete` keeps task 22.7's fixed-4-query pattern, just against the renamed tables. Verified via a dedicated cross-user-isolation test exercising every mutation against a foreign session id, plus the full existing `plugins/warden` suite (225 tests, 21 new for `sessions.ts` alone) and the full repository suite (3729 tests), all green; `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, and `pnpm format:check` all clean. `plugins/warden` manifest `0.5.3` → `0.6.0` (minor — new capability, not a fix). `0.102.1` → `0.102.2` fixes a real performance problem in Warden reported directly by the developer ("the warden plugin is very slow... maybe because it fetches model details from a few providers and there are nearly 500 models"): `discoverModels()` (`plugins/warden/app/_lib/model-discovery.ts`) was called fresh, with zero caching, on every single render of `/warden`, `/warden/models`, and `/warden/providers` — for each configured provider this ran a live DNS lookup, a secrets-store decrypt, a `GET {baseUrl}/models` fetch with an 8-second timeout (`MODEL_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS`), and an unconditional DB health-status write, all live, on every navigation between those three pages, not just once per session. Fixed with a per-user, 30-second TTL in-memory cache (`discoveryCache`, keyed by `session.user.id`) fronting the existing live-discovery pass, plus explicit invalidation (`invalidateDiscoveryCacheForUser`) on every provider mutation (`createProviderAction`/`updateProviderAction`/`deleteProviderAction`, `actions.ts`) and a new `refreshModelDiscoveryAction` wired into the existing "Recheck providers"/"Recheck models" buttons (`ProvidersView.tsx`/`ModelsView.tsx`) so those buttons still force a genuine live re-check rather than replaying the cache. Verified with 4 new regression tests covering the caching behavior itself (cache hit avoids re-fetching, invalidation forces a fresh pass, separate users get separate cache entries, invalidating one user doesn't evict another's) plus the full existing `plugins/warden` suite (205 tests), `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, and `pnpm format:check`, all green. `plugins/warden` manifest `0.5.2` → `0.5.3`. `0.102.0` → `0.102.1` fixes a real authorization gap found while checking workstream 0020 leg 7's own prerequisite note before starting it: that note (drafted at repo HEAD `57e587c7`) named `sendInviteAction` (`plugins/console/app/users/actions.ts`) and two `email-templates-actions.ts` actions as still missing their `hasCapability` check, and a direct read of the current code confirmed the gap was still live on `main` — `sendInviteAction` called only `sdk.auth.requireSession()` before hitting `/api/admin/invites` (its own sibling `cancelInviteAction` already gated on `user:manage`), and `getEmailTemplateCopyAction`/`testSendEmailTemplateAction` likewise called only `requireSession()` before `adminFetch`-ing `/api/admin/email-templates*` with `SOVEREIGN_ADMIN_KEY` attached (their sibling `saveEmailTemplateCopyAction` already gated on `instance:configure`). Since server actions are reachable by action id independent of the Console page's `adminOnly` gate (`docs/architecture-rules.md`), any authenticated non-admin user could previously send an invite or read/test-send the password-reset/invite email templates. Fixed by adding the matching `hasCapability` check to each — `sendInviteAction` now requires `user:manage` (matching `cancelInviteAction`), the two email-template actions now require `instance:configure` (matching `saveEmailTemplateCopyAction`). New regression tests for all three (`plugins/console/app/users/__tests__/actions.test.ts`, and a new `plugins/console/app/settings/__tests__/email-templates-actions.test.ts` — no test file existed for that action module before this), each confirmed to actually fail against the reverted pre-fix code (a bare `fetch` mock that would have been called, and wasn't) before being accepted as real regression guards. Standalone fix, not part of workstream 0020's own leg numbering — landed specifically so leg 7's `13.11`/`13.12` tasks (which lock in the corrected behavior with their own regression tests) don't have to encode the pre-fix bug as expected behavior, the exact trap workstream 0012 leg 1 named explicitly for a prior, similar case. `plugins/console` manifest `0.5.5` → `0.5.6`. `0.101.9` → `0.102.0` is workstream 0020 leg 6 (`8.35`, `8.36`, `22.7` — DB connection & rotation hygiene): bounded Postgres pool sizes (new `POSTGRES_POOL_MAX` env var, default 5, applied to the platform/auth/isolated-plugin pools), batched `field-reseal.ts`'s per-row `UPDATE`s into grouped multi-row statements (plus a real, previously-undiscovered Postgres bug found in the process — `startHmacRotation()`'s `row?.started === now` comparison always failed on Postgres, since node-postgres returns a `bigint` column as a string, not a number, from a raw query; fixed with an explicit `Number()` coercion, caught only because this leg added the first-ever Postgres test coverage for that path), and collapsed Warden's account-deletion handler from `2n + 2` to 4 fixed queries via `inArray`. `packages/db` 4.8.2 → 4.8.3, `apps/auth` 2.2.2 → 2.2.3, `runtime` 0.91.9 → 0.91.10, `plugins/warden` manifest 0.5.1 → 0.5.2 (all patch); root platform bumped minor per `8.35`'s own review checklist, the first root bump in this workstream (legs 1-5 bumped only the specific touched packages) — see `docs/workstreams/0020-codebase-audit-remediation.md`'s leg 6 changelog entry for the full account. `0.101.8` → `0.101.9` fixes a real production outage, reported directly ("I deployed and now still app is not up and running") after the developer deployed with `sovereign-plugin-travellog` included and an unrelated redeploy triggered a container restart. Investigated live via SSH against the actual production host: `docker logs`/`pg_stat_activity`/`pg_locks` on the real Postgres instance showed every request hanging, traced to a stuck backend holding a session-level advisory lock that nothing would ever release. Root cause was in `packages/db/src/migrate.ts`'s Postgres migration lock, not travellog itself — any restart could have triggered it, travellog's inclusion just happened to be the one that did: `runMigrations()` acquired and released `pg_advisory_lock`/`pg_advisory_unlock` via two independent `dbGet(pdb, ...)` calls, and since `pg_advisory_lock`/`unlock` are session-scoped (not query-scoped) while `pdb.db` is backed by a `pg.Pool` free to serve each call from a different physical connection, the unlock call could silently no-op on a connection that never held the lock — leaking it forever. Every subsequent `runMigrations()` call (i.e. every later container restart) then hung indefinitely trying to reacquire a lock nothing would release, taking down the entire app on every request. Restored service immediately by terminating the stuck holder session (`pg_terminate_backend`, run with explicit developer authorization) — confirmed via a fresh `pg_stat_activity` read and a live `curl` against both the server-local port and the public URL. Root-cause fix: pin one dedicated client for the whole lock→migrate→unlock critical section via `pdb.db.$client.connect()` (`$client` is drizzle-orm's own exposed raw `Pool`, confirmed by reading its `.d.ts`; `PlatformDb`'s Postgres branch in `client.ts` widened to expose it) — `migratePg`/`seedPlatformData` keep using the shared pool as before, since only the lock/unlock pair needs session affinity. Proved the fix actually closes the leak, not just "looks right": a new regression test independently probes the lock via `pg_try_advisory_lock` from a completely separate connection after `runMigrations()` returns, and was confirmed to fail against the pre-fix code by temporarily reverting the fix and watching it fail. **Verifying that fix under CI-representative conditions surfaced two more, independent, pre-existing test-fixture bugs** — found because CI (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) runs the entire `.pg.test.ts` suite (10 files) together against one shared `postgres:16` container, and running that same combination locally reproduced real flakiness the fix's correctness had newly exposed (the old, broken lock never actually serialized concurrent migration runs the way the fix now correctly does). First: `migrate.pg.test.ts`'s own `freshPlatformDb()` helpers derived each test's `migrationsTable` by concatenating a fixed prefix onto a full 36-char UUID-based schema name, producing 75–87 character identifiers — over Postgres's 63-byte `NAMEDATALEN` limit, silently truncated with no error, producing colliding truncated names once several ran concurrently (confirmed via `wc -c`/`cut -c1-63` reproducing the exact truncated name seen in a live "relation ... does not exist" failure). Fixed by deriving `migrationsTable` from a short, independent `randomUUID().slice(0, 8)` suffix instead, matching the established short-id convention already used in `field-encryption.pg.test.ts`/`field-schema-e2e.pg.test.ts`. Second, more serious, found chasing the first fix's own remaining flake: `plugin-isolation-migration.pg.test.ts`'s `afterEach` ran a blanket `DROP SCHEMA "drizzle" CASCADE` — the fixed schema Postgres's own migrator always tracks applied migrations in, regardless of any connection's `search_path` — which raced and deleted _other_ concurrently-running test files' own in-use migrations tables mid-run. Reproduced live: this exact drop deleted `migrate.pg.test.ts`'s own tracking table between its two sequential `runMigrations()` calls within a single test, producing the identical "relation ... does not exist" symptom the identifier-length bug did, via a completely different mechanism. `migrate.pg.test.ts`'s own `afterEach` already carried a doc comment describing this exact class of bug for its own describe block (found and fixed in an earlier session) — it just hadn't been generalized to this sibling file. Fixed by scoping the cleanup to just the two fixed table names that describe block actually creates (`pluginMigrationsTableName`/`sharedToIsolatedMigrationsTableName` for the hardcoded `fs.sovereign.tasks` test plugin id), mirroring the sibling file's already-fixed pattern. Verified via 4 consecutive full 10-file `.pg.test.ts` suite runs (171/171 passing each time, previously flaky), plus a clean `pnpm typecheck`/`pnpm lint`/`pnpm format:check` and full repo `vitest run`. `@sovereignfs/db` bumped `4.8.0` → `4.8.1`. `0.101.7` → `0.101.8` hardens `instrumentation.ts`'s boot sequence, found while investigating a real production deployment failure reported directly (a developer deployed `sovereign-plugin-travellog`, "entire platform stopped working," rolled back — see the companion `fix/travellog-missing-runtime-deps` PR for the specific missing-dependency gap that investigation also found and fixed). `register()`'s `await import('./src/scheduler')` and `await import('./src/jobs')` had no error handling — both dynamic imports statically resolve `generated/plugin-schedules.ts`/`plugin-jobs.ts`, which in turn statically import every manifest-declared plugin's actual handler and that handler's own full transitive dependency graph, so a fault anywhere in that graph (a missing npm dependency, a syntax error, anything) threw uncaught, aborting `register()` before the rest of boot (job worker, backup worker, event broker) ever ran. Next.js surfaces this as "Failed to prepare server" — every request fails, not just ones touching the broken plugin. Reproduced live, not just reasoned about: a real `docker build` + `docker run` of this repo's own `Dockerfile` (against the actual published `sovereign-plugin-travellog` repo, via a real `sovereign.plugins.json` entry) hit exactly this failure mode from an uncaught error during platform migrations — different specific trigger than the missing-dependency theory, same mechanism, confirming the general vulnerability class independent of which fault happens to hit it first. Fixed by wrapping both dynamic imports in try/catch, logging clearly and letting boot continue — mirrors `runAllPluginMigrations()`'s own per-plugin isolation (added after the RFC 0071 incident, see `docs/incidents/`) in spirit, though coarser here: `PLUGIN_SCHEDULES`/`PLUGIN_JOBS` are each one statically-imported array, not a per-plugin call site, so a broken handler disables the whole scheduler or job-worker subsystem for this process rather than just its own plugin — coarser but far better than before, since every other plugin's pages/actions and the rest of boot still complete normally. Per-tick handler execution was already isolated (`tickOnce()`'s own try/catch, `claimNextJob`'s equivalent) — this closes the remaining gap at import time, before any tick or claim ever runs. Full existing `runtime` test suite (801 tests) green; no dedicated new test, since `instrumentation.ts` is a Next.js lifecycle hook with no existing test harness for it in this repo and forcing one would mean mocking Next.js's own instrumentation-loading machinery — verification here is the live `docker build`/`docker run` reproduction instead. `runtime` bumped `0.91.4` → `0.91.5`. The companion `fix/travellog-missing-runtime-deps` PR (the missing-dependency half of this same investigation) has already merged as of this rebase, so the version-number collision the two were expected to hit at merge time never materialized — this PR simply bumps from its already-merged result. `0.101.6` → `0.101.7` fixes a real production deployment failure reported directly (a developer deployed `sovereign-plugin-travellog`, "entire platform stopped working," rolled back): `runtime/package.json` never declared `nanoid`, `tz-lookup`, or `@dnd-kit/{core,sortable,utilities}` — all genuine dependencies of travellog's own composed code (`nanoid`/`tz-lookup` reachable from its manifest-declared `trip-mode-reminders` schedule handler and `import.swarm` job handler; `@dnd-kit` from the Planner's drag-and-drop UI). Root-caused to a real gap in the dependency-hoisting pipeline (RFC 0057, `bin/plugin-deps.ts`'s `hoistDepsForPlugin`/`syncLocalPluginDeps`): that mechanism only runs interactively, via `sv plugin add` or `pnpm dev`'s `.local`-plugin self-heal step — confirmed by reading both `scripts/install-plugins.ts` (what a real deployment's `pnpm install:plugins` actually calls, per the Dockerfile) and `scripts/generate-registry.ts` (what `pnpm build`/CI depends on): neither invokes it. A plugin added the documented way (`sovereign.plugins.json` + the normal Docker build) can silently ship with a `runtime/package.json` that doesn't match what the composed code needs, with nothing in the build pipeline to catch it. **First fix attempt was wrong and reverted before landing**: computed the diff via the real `hoistDepsForPlugin()` (not hand-edited) and committed travellog's specific deps straight into `runtime/package.json` and the ledger — caught in review as exactly the anti-pattern RFC 0057 exists to avoid. `runtime/generated/plugin-deps.json` has been `{}` throughout this repo's history except one accidental entry (kanban's `.local` dev clone, reverted in `46b06256` as noise, per that commit's own message: "this ledger tracks committed plugins only"); RFC 0057's own doc (`docs/rfcs/0057-plugin-dep-hoisting.md`) specifies hoisting happens via `sv plugin add` or `pnpm dev`'s local-plugin sync — both operating on an operator's own deployment checkout, never as a one-off commit to the shared platform repo for a specific third-party plugin. `sovereign.plugins.json` itself is local and gitignored (`scripts/install-plugins.ts`'s own doc comment) — the platform repo is never supposed to know which third-party plugins a given instance runs, let alone carry their dependencies. The actual fix: `scripts/install-plugins.ts` — the bulk installer real deployments use, invoked by the Dockerfile — never called `hoistDepsForPlugin()` after cloning a plugin, unlike `sv plugin add` (single interactive add) and `pnpm dev`'s sync (`.local` dev plugins), which both already did. Added a new `hoistDepsForClonedPlugins()` helper wired into its clone loop, mirroring `bin/sv.ts`'s own `pluginAdd` command pattern (`hoistDepsForPlugin` + a `reportHoistResult`-style summary). This closes the gap generically for every plugin `sovereign.plugins.json` declares, dynamically, on each operator's own checkout at install time — the platform repo's own `runtime/package.json` and `plugin-deps.json` stay exactly as they've always been, untouched by any specific plugin. No `runtime` version bump — nothing under `runtime/` changed; the fix lives entirely in root-level tooling. Verified via a new `hoistDepsForClonedPlugins` test suite (`scripts/__tests__/install-plugins.test.ts`) covering a no-op with nothing cloned, a single plugin with an external dep, several plugins hoisted independently in one pass, a plugin with no external deps left untouched, and a version-conflict warning — plus the full existing `bin/plugin-deps.ts` and `install-plugins.ts` suites, all green. A separate, independent PR (`fix/plugin-boot-isolation`) covers the boot-time hardening this same investigation surfaced and directly proved live (an uncaught `instrumentation.ts` error, regardless of cause, currently fails every request rather than just the affected plugin) — not folded into this one since they're separable concerns with their own review scope. `0.101.5` → `0.101.6` fixes a real Console UX bug, surfaced by a developer question ("does 'Show example apps' still appear when `SOVEREIGN_EXAMPLES_ENABLED` is `0`?") rather than found live-testing: `plugins/console/app/settings/page.tsx`'s "Example plugins" section (the CON-12 bulk visibility toggle) rendered unconditionally, with no check for whether the build actually composed any `example: true` plugin into the registry in the first place. On the default (examples-off) production build — confirmed via the `Dockerfile`'s `SOVEREIGN_EXAMPLES_ENABLED=0` default and the published GHCR image passing no build-args (see this file's own "Working conventions" version-bump section for the plugin/package split) — an admin saw a working-looking "Show example plugins" checkbox that silently did nothing: `computeDisabledPluginIds()` (`runtime/src/plugin-status.ts`) can only ever un-hide IDs already present in the registry's example set, which is empty whenever `example-plugins/` was never scanned in at build time. Fixed by adding `hasExamplePlugins` to `GET /api/admin/settings`'s response (`getExamplePluginIds(getInstalledPlugins()).length > 0`, `runtime/app/api/admin/settings/route.ts`) — distinct from the persisted `examplesEnabled` visibility setting the toggle itself writes — and gating the whole section on it in `page.tsx`, so a build with no example plugins hides the control entirely instead of showing an inert one. Verified against a real running local dev instance, not just the type system: hit the live `GET /api/admin/settings` directly via `SOVEREIGN_ADMIN_KEY` (no seeded test login available — this dev database already has real, non-test user accounts, so `sv seed` correctly refused to run) with the registry regenerated both ways — `SOVEREIGN_EXAMPLES_ENABLED=1` (`hasExamplePlugins: true`, this repo's own local `.env` default) and `=0` (`hasExamplePlugins: false`) — confirming the field flips correctly and independently of the persisted `examplesEnabled` setting, then restored the registry to its original examples-on state before finishing. `docs/plugins/console.md`'s CON-12 row updated to describe the new hide-when-empty behavior instead of the old "toggle with nothing to show" wording. `runtime` bumped `0.91.3` → `0.91.4`; `plugins/console`'s own `manifest.json` (plugins version only their manifest, never `package.json`, per this file's own convention) bumped `0.5.4` → `0.5.5`. (Note: the versions between `0.101.4` and `0.101.5` were bumped by another session without a matching entry here; not backfilled, since this session has no first-hand knowledge of what they contained.) `0.101.4` → `0.101.5` fixes a real bug found building `sovereign-plugin-travellog`'s T.23 (portability export/import hooks): `resolveStorageContext` (`runtime/src/sdk-host.ts`) already fell back a portability export/import resolver's `pluginId` to `getPortabilityPluginContext()` when no request header was present, but `userId` had no equivalent fallback and always resolved to `null` — `canAccessStorageObject` denies whenever a stored object's `ownerUserId` is set and the reading context's `userId` doesn't match (including `null`), so an export resolver reading back a user-owned object (e.g. a photo/attachment uploaded with `ownerUserId` set) via `sdk.storage.get()` was silently denied, returning `null` with no error surfaced. Fixed by threading `userId` through `runWithPortabilityPlugin`'s `AsyncLocalStorage` alongside `pluginId` (new `getPortabilityUserContext()`; `getPortabilityPluginContext()`'s own external signature unchanged) and adding the same fallback `resolveStorageContext` already has for `pluginId`. `runtime` bumped `0.91.2` → `0.91.3`. This entry was backfilled after the fact — the version bump itself landed in `c00392b4` (task T.23, this same session) with no matching Status entry written at the time; recorded here from that commit's own message, which this session has first-hand knowledge of, rather than left as an unexplained jump. `0.101.3` → `0.101.4` is an ad-hoc platform fix found live while implementing `plugins/sovereign-plugin-travellog.local`'s T.21 (offline capability wiring for its bare `routePrefix` page, not a platform roadmap task): `runtime/src/__tests__/offline-route-neutrality.test.ts`'s `collectRootSourceFiles()` flagged `actions.ts`'s `resumeImportAction` — a `'use server'` action dispatched only by a POST from the import-status UI, never part of this route's own SSR render — for calling `headers()`, a false-positive "user-neutral shell" violation (RFC 0074/0078). Root cause: the function collected every top-level file in a plugin's `app/` directory plus the unconditional full contents of any `_`-prefixed directory, with no check for whether the bare route's `page.tsx`/`layout.tsx` actually reaches that file. That blanket sweep happened to coincide with real reachability for Launcher — the only plugin this was ever tested against, a single-route plugin with one `_components` helper — but swept in unrelated top-level and co-located files for any plugin with more than one route. Rewritten to walk the real local-import graph from `page.tsx`/`layout.tsx` (and the sibling Next.js route-convention files — `template`/`default`/`loading`/`error`/`not-found`) via a regex-based relative-import scan, transitively. **Reachability alone would not have fixed the reported false positive**: `actions.ts` is still genuinely _imported_ by `layout.tsx`'s `OfflineSyncBoundary` component, to obtain the action reference it binds to a client call — so a pure import-graph walk still reaches it. Fixed by pruning three file categories outright regardless of reachability, mirroring the existing `isClientComponent` exemption's own reasoning: a `'use server'` module (grep for the directive), a manifest-declared `jobs`/`schedules` handler `entry`, and a test file (`__tests__/**`, `*.test.ts`/`*.spec.ts`). Pruning means not just omitting the excluded file but never following its own imports either — anything reachable only through an excluded file is, by the same reasoning, never SSR-executed either. Verified against the real false positive (Travellog's `actions.ts` no longer flagged; the `_components`/`_lib` files it doesn't reach are no longer swept in) plus 4 new synthetic-fixture regression tests added to the same file — generic, not hardcoded to Travellog's plugin id: transitive reachability through a co-located helper still catches a real violation, and a reachable `'use server'` module / manifest job-or-schedule handler / test file are each still excluded even when directly imported. Test-only change (`runtime/src/__tests__/`) — no `runtime` package version bump, matching this repo's own precedent for test-only fixes (`0.94.12`→`0.94.13`, `0.94.13`→`0.94.14`: only the platform version moved, not the touched package's own). `0.101.2` → `0.101.3` is an ad-hoc platform fix found live while implementing `plugins/sovereign-plugin-travellog.local`'s T.20 (a schedule handler wanting to read a manifest-declared `REMINDER_LEAD_MINUTES` env var, not a platform roadmap task) — the identical background-invocation gap `0.101.1`'s own `sdk.storage` fix (below) closed, just never generalized to `sdk.env.get()`: `packages/sdk/src/env.ts`'s `get()` called `next/headers()` directly with no try/catch, so any `sdk.env.get()` call from an `sdk.jobs`/`sdk.schedules` handler (no real Next.js request) threw outright instead of returning the resolved value, silently failing the handler every tick per `docs/plugin-development.md`'s documented schedule-error behavior ("thrown errors are caught and logged; the failed schedule waits out its own interval before running again"). T.20 itself worked around it with a hardcoded constant rather than hitting the bug, so this fix isn't unblocking anything in flight — closed generically the same way `sdk.storage` was. Fixed by mirroring `storage.ts`/`sdk-host.ts`'s exact split: `env.get()` now catches the `headers()` throw and passes the header-derived `pluginId` (possibly `null`) to a new host method, `requireHost().env.get(key, pluginId)`; `sdk-host.ts`'s new `resolveEnvPluginId()` falls back through `getPortabilityPluginContext() ?? getBackgroundPluginContext()` before doing the actual `SV_PLUGIN_<SLUG>_<KEY>` lookup — unlike `resolveStorageContext()`, it returns `null` rather than throwing when no plugin id is resolvable anywhere, preserving `sdk.env.get()`'s existing documented "outside a plugin route context → null" contract instead of turning it into a new throw. Verified via 5 new regression tests (`runtime/src/__tests__/sdk-host-env-routing.test.ts`, mirroring `sdk-host-storage-routing.test.ts`'s pattern) plus the full existing `packages/sdk`/`runtime` suites, all green. `@sovereignfs/sdk` bumped `1.46.0` → `1.47.0` — minor, not patch, on reflection: adding a new required `env` member to the `SdkHost` interface is a host-implementer-facing breaking change per NFR-04, the same category `sdk.storage`'s own `StorageContext.pluginId` widening was, and a plain patch bump would have understated it; `runtime` bumped `0.91.1` → `0.91.2`. `0.101.1` → `0.101.2` fixes a real production deployment failure, reported directly via a GitHub Actions run link (image build succeeded, deploy failed) rather than found live-testing: `scripts/generate/plugin-schedules.ts`/`plugin-jobs.ts`/`plugin-events.ts` all generated static handler imports pointing at a plugin's _composed_ route-tree copy (`runtime/app/(platform)/(plugins)/<id>/...` — a real symlink in production, per `compose-routes.ts`), not its real source file. `runtime/generated/plugin-schedules.ts` is not excluded from `runtime/tsconfig.json`'s scope the way the composed plugin directories themselves are, so TypeScript resolves a schedule handler's own relative imports (e.g. a sibling `_lib/ids.ts`'s `import 'nanoid'`) against the _symlink's apparent location_ when reached this way — walking up through `runtime/`'s own `node_modules`, which doesn't declare every package a plugin might use — instead of the plugin's real directory, the way Next's own route-file discovery correctly does. Root-caused via an isolated `git worktree` reproduction of the exact failing CI build (deliberately not against the live dev server sharing the main working directory): reproduced the identical error, then proved it wasn't a general `nanoid`-resolution problem by removing just the newly-added `sovereign-plugin-ledger`'s import and watching Kanban/Docs/Sheets — which use the identical `nanoid` import pattern — compile successfully in the same build; `tsc --traceResolution` on the generated file's own import chain then confirmed the exact symlink-vs-real-path mechanism. `sovereign-ledger` is the first plugin ever to combine a manifest `schedules` declaration with a dependency not already independently redeclared in `runtime/package.json` (`drizzle-orm` already was; `nanoid` wasn't) — Tasks (the only other schedule-declaring plugin) never hit this because its own job handler doesn't happen to import anything `runtime` doesn't already have. Fixed at the root, not with a `runtime/package.json` band-aid: all three generators now import the plugin's real source file directly (`srcFile`, already computed for the existence check) instead of computing a composed-tree path via `resolveComposeTargets`'s `targets[0]` — kept the `resolveComposeTargets` call itself for its real validation (overlay-shell multi-segment-`routePrefix` rejection), just stopped using its result for the import path. Verified via the same isolated-worktree reproduction with the fix applied (the full production build succeeds, `/ledger`'s route compiles), 55 updated/passing generator unit tests, and the full existing test suite (2907 tests). (Note: the narrative history below resumes at `0.94.16` — the versions between `0.94.16` and `0.101.0` were bumped by other sessions without a matching entry here; not backfilled, since this session has no first-hand knowledge of what they contained.) `0.101.0` → `0.101.1` is an ad-hoc platform fix found live while building the externally-maintained `sovereign-plugin-travellog` plugin's Swarm importer (`T.8`, `sdk.jobs`-backed, not a platform roadmap task): `sdk.storage.*` had no fallback for a background-invocation context, unlike `sdk.db.getClient()`, which already did. `storageContext()` (`packages/sdk/src/storage.ts`) called `next/headers()` directly with no try/catch, so any `sdk.storage` call from an `sdk.jobs`/`sdk.schedules` handler (no real Next.js request) threw outright — the exact background-context gap `db.getClient()` had already solved via `runWithBackgroundPlugin()`, just never generalized to storage. Fixed by mirroring that fix: `storageContext()` now catches the throw and passes `pluginId: null` through instead of failing itself; `runtime/src/sdk-host.ts`'s new `resolveStorageContext()` falls back to the same background-plugin `AsyncLocalStorage` context across all five storage methods (`put`/`get`/`delete`/`list`/`getSignedUrl`), throwing its own clear error only if that fallback also comes up empty. Verified via 4 new regression tests (`runtime/src/__tests__/sdk-host-storage-routing.test.ts`, mirroring `sdk-host-db-routing.test.ts`'s existing pattern) plus the full existing `packages/sdk`/`runtime` suites, all green. **A second, distinct bug surfaced live once this fix let the import job actually run**: every import failed with "the uploaded export is no longer available in storage," despite a raw table dump (bypassing Drizzle, same DB connection) proving the row and bytes were both genuinely present. Root cause: `sdk.storage.put()`'s separate `ownerUserId` ownership check (`packages/db`'s `canAccessStorageObject`) denies read access whenever `ownerUserId` is set and the reading context's `userId` doesn't match — including `null` — and a job/schedule handler's `userId` always resolves to `null`, since `JobContext` carries a plugin id, never a user id, with no equivalent background-context fallback possible (a job is plugin-scoped, not inherently user-scoped — there's nothing trustworthy to fall back to). This one is **not** a platform bug to fix generically: it's now documented as a footgun in `docs/architecture-rules.md` (and this file's own critical-violations list) — a plugin uploading an object that a background handler will read back later must omit `ownerUserId` entirely, accepting the tradeoff that the object becomes invisible to the account-deletion storage sweep. Applied in `sovereign-plugin-travellog`'s own upload route, with a regression test locking in the omission. Verified live end-to-end after both fixes: a fresh upload through the real route, the job running to completion, and the imported check-ins appearing in the plugin's timeline, with two deliberately-broken fixture photo URLs logged as skipped rather than aborting the job. `@sovereignfs/sdk` bumped `1.45.0` → `1.46.0` (minor — `StorageContext.pluginId` widened `string` → `string | null`, a host-implementer-facing type change per NFR-04, with a `docs/upgrade.md` migration note); `runtime` bumped `0.91.0` → `0.91.1`. `0.94.15` → `0.94.16` is task 1.19 (RFC 0086, workstream 0017 leg 4): `apps/auth/src/auth.ts`'s `rateLimit.storage` flipped `'memory'` → `'database'`, closing the multi-instance gap where running more than one `apps/auth` process gave each its own independent brute-force/credential-stuffing counters — a one-line config flip, since better-auth's own migrator already creates the `rateLimit` table from the changed option on every startup, no hand-written schema needed. `docs/security.md`'s "Login rate limiting" bullet, which claimed in-memory storage was a documented single-instance limitation, corrected to match. Verified live against the real dev database before committing, not just via the config change itself: confirmed the `rateLimit` table auto-creates, then confirmed the actual bug this closes — two independent `betterAuth()` instances built from the same options (simulating separate `apps/auth` processes) shared rate-limit counters through the database, with a 4th sign-in attempt split across the two instances correctly returning `429`. Verified against SQLite only (the local dev dialect); Postgres wasn't independently confirmed in this session, though the mechanism is dialect-agnostic (same migrator path every other better-auth table already uses on both dialects). A companion task, 2.29 (the `runtime`-side half of the same RFC 0086), was scoped for this session but paused before any implementation once a real blocker surfaced: `runtime/middleware.ts` runs in Next.js's Edge runtime, which cannot load `ioredis` (it depends on Node's `net`/`tls` built-ins for raw TCP sockets) — so the RFC's proposed "reuse `RedisBroker`'s lazy `require('ioredis')` pattern" cannot work from `checkGlobalRateLimit`/`clientIp`'s actual call site in middleware, confirmed by `RedisBroker` itself only ever being instantiated from Node-runtime API routes in the existing codebase, never from middleware. Not a design nitpick — enabling it properly means Next.js Node.js Middleware for the entire file, a platform-wide runtime change with its own blast radius (the DB-write-avoidance pattern and `console.log`-only dev-mode audit path both exist specifically because middleware is Edge today) that deserves its own scoped task and review rather than being folded into what was meant to be this workstream's smallest, lowest-risk leg. Written up in RFC 0086's Open questions, task 2.29's own correction note in `docs/epics/platform-shell.md`, and workstream `docs/workstreams/0017-auth-security-hardening.md`'s leg 5 detail section — no code shipped for 2.29. `0.94.14` → `0.94.15` fixes a real authorization bypass in three `runtime/app/api/admin/*` routes, found via a security review requested directly rather than live-testing: the middleware matcher deliberately excludes `/api/admin` from its normal session-verification path (every other `/api/admin/*` route instead checks the internal `SOVEREIGN_ADMIN_KEY` via `checkAdminKey`), but `connections/route.ts` and `data-grants/route.ts` (metadata-only external connection inventory, and every user's active consent grants under RFC 0002) both read `x-sovereign-user-role` straight off the incoming request and trusted it outright — since middleware never touches this path, that header is never platform-injected and never stripped, so any caller could forge `x-sovereign-user-role: platform:owner` (e.g. `curl -H`) and get full admin data. Fixed by switching both to `checkAdminKey`, matching every sibling route and how Console actually calls them server-side. `email-templates/preview/route.ts` had the identical bug but a harder fix: it's loaded as a Console settings `<iframe src>`, so it can't attach an `Authorization` header, and it must stay off the middleware surface — a middleware-applied `frame-ancestors 'none'` on this route's HTML response would stop Console from framing its own preview. Fixed by verifying the real signed session cookie in-route via the runtime's existing `verifySession` helper (the same one middleware itself uses) before the `instance:configure` capability check, binding authorization to an actual session instead of a caller-suppliable header. `runtime` bumped `0.87.0` → `0.87.1` for the same fix; see `docs/upgrade.md`'s Runtime version map. `0.94.13` → `0.94.14` fixes a CI failure reported directly via a GitHub Actions run link (`vitest run` exiting 1 despite every listed test file showing as passed) rather than found live-testing — `packages/sdk/src/__tests__/device-client.test.ts`'s `camera.photo` describe block, "hints at the device camera via capture=\"environment\" for source: camera", called `void camera.photo('camera')` (deliberately fire-and-forget, per the test's own comment, only to avoid leaking a dangling `change` listener into the next test) and never awaited the resulting promise. `camera.photo`'s web fallback (`device-client.ts`'s `pickViaFileInput`) reads the selected file via a real `FileReader.readAsDataURL()`, whose completion jsdom 29.1.1 schedules asynchronously via `setImmediate` — so it fires _after_ the un-awaiting test function had already returned, landing as an unhandled exception (`TypeError: Expected an Uint8Array`, thrown deep in `@exodus/bytes`' `toBase64`, called from jsdom's own `FileReaderImpl._setResult`) with no test boundary left to attribute it to, failing the whole run via Vitest's global unhandled-error tracking even though the per-file summary line showed 41/41 passed. The adjacent test one block up ("falls back to a hidden file input on the web transport") exercises the identical `File` → `dispatchEvent('change')` → `FileReader` path and has never had this problem, because it already awaits the resulting promise. Fixed by doing the same here — capturing `camera.photo('camera')`'s promise and awaiting it after dispatching `change`, so the `FileReader` resolves inside this test's own async boundary instead of racing the test runner's cleanup between tests. No source-code change — `device-client.ts` itself was never at fault; this was purely a test not waiting for real async work it had already started. Verified via 5 consecutive clean local runs of the single test file plus one full-suite run (`pnpm exec vitest run`, matching CI's own invocation) showing zero unhandled errors, versus the original CI log's single `Uncaught Exception` — a timing-dependent race that doesn't reproduce on every run, consistent with jsdom's `setImmediate`-scheduled completion sometimes losing the race against Vitest's own module/DOM teardown between tests and sometimes not. `0.94.12` → `0.94.13` fixes a `pnpm verify:push` failure blocking a routine tag push, reported directly ("I tried to push the new tag, and got this e2e test failure") rather than found live-testing: `apps/auth/src/__tests__/auth.test.ts`'s first test — the only one in the file to actually `await import('../auth')` cold, since every later test in the same file reuses that now-cached module — was timing out at vitest's 5000ms default. Root-caused by timing the import standalone outside vitest entirely (`tsx` running the same dynamic import): ~8.5s total, with no single slow culprit — `better-auth` (~2s), `@better-auth/passkey` (~1.4s), `@better-auth/oauth-provider` (~0.9s), and `@sovereignfs/mailer` (~2.7s) each individually add up, cumulative cold-parse/transform weight across several sizable packages that's likely grown past the 5000ms default as more better-auth plugins were added over time — not a hang, not a regression in behavior (the test's own assertion passes instantly once the import resolves). Fixed with an explicit 15s timeout scoped to just that one test (vitest's own suggested fix for a genuinely slow-but-not-hung test), not a global `testTimeout` bump across the ~2400-test suite, which would have papered over any future genuine hang everywhere else instead of just here. `0.94.11` → `0.94.12` fixes a dev-console-only "Each child in a list should have a unique key prop" warning firing on every mobile page load, found live-testing `0.94.11`'s own `MobileHeader` self-measurement change (browser console during a real reload, not a report) — `runtime/app/(platform)/layout.tsx` passes `bell`/`avatarMenu` into `MobileHeader` as two sibling elements with no `key`. Root-caused by empirical isolation (temporarily removing/re-adding each of the two children, one at a time, in a throwaway local edit, since React's dev-mode warning dedup meant only a genuine fresh page load — not a client-side nav or HMR reload — reliably re-triggered it): the pair together, both real elements with no key, is what React's `validateChildKeys` flags — one real element alongside a conditionally-`undefined` slot (`MobileHeader`'s own `logo`/`title` pair) doesn't warn, since there's no reconciliation ambiguity for a single item. Fixed by giving `<NotificationBell key="bell" />` and `<AccountMenu key="avatar-menu" .../>` explicit keys at their creation site. Purely a dev-console warning, not a functional bug — React still rendered correctly either way; verified fixed via a completely fresh browser tab (zero console history) showing no errors on load, where a same-tab reload alone wasn't trustworthy proof given the dedup behavior. `0.94.10` → `0.94.11` generalizes and hardens a batch of findings from the `sovereign-tasks` mobile-carousel work rather than fixing them only where they were found — the platform doesn't control externally-maintained plugin code, so the goal was to close the underlying gaps once, here, for every current and future consumer instead of relying on each plugin author to independently discover and patch the same class of bug. Two real, generic issues: (1) a self-rendered `MobileHeader`/`MobileFooter` (`@sovereignfs/ui`, the `shellConfig.mobileHeader`/`mobileFooter: false` pattern) had no way to tell the shell its real height, silently inheriting the shell's own `0px` override meant for "no chrome at all" — `Sheet`/`Drawer`/`Dialog` then extended full-height and were visibly covered by the chrome's own higher `z-index`, reported live in `sovereign-tasks` as "Drawer has broken"/an unreachable Delete button. Fixed once at the source: both components now self-measure via a new internal hook, `usePublishShellChromeHeight` (`packages/ui`, now `0.56.6`), and publish `--sv-shell-header-height`/`--sv-shell-footer-height` onto `#sv-app-shell` themselves — no plugin wiring required, before or after. Deliberately not `ResizeObserver`-based: live testing found a freshly created `ResizeObserver` never fired even once for an already-rendered, stably-sized element, in both this repo's Chromium-based preview tooling and a real WebKit iOS Simulator session (cause not fully root-caused) — a synchronous `getBoundingClientRect()` read in `useLayoutEffect` plus a `resize` listener worked immediately in both. (2) A `position: sticky` element with no compositing layer of its own can go stale/blank for a stretch of frames during a fast scroll on iOS Safari (a WebKit momentum-scroll re-tiling quirk, `transform: translateZ(0)` fixes it) — found and fixed across four elements in `sovereign-tasks` first, then applied proactively here to every other sticky element found to be at genuine risk (mobile, inside scrolling content): `@sovereignfs/ui`'s `MobileHeader` (also `0.56.6`) and `SystemBanner`, and the `account` (now `0.3.4`) and `console` (now `0.5.3`) platform plugins' own mobile tab-strip headers. Both findings are written up generically in `docs/architecture-rules.md` (not scoped to Tasks) since any plugin using these same DS patterns was equally exposed. `0.94.9` → `0.94.10` fixes a real gap in `0.94.8`'s own settle-detection redesign, found from a third user-supplied screen recording after live re-testing failed to reproduce it: `SwipableMobileCarousel`'s mount window was keyed _only_ off the debounced, settled index — never off where the scroll position actually was during an in-progress gesture. A single fast flick can carry native `scroll-snap-type: mandatory` momentum straight past an intermediate slide to one two-or-more away in one continuous motion; settle-detection correctly waits out the full debounce window before confirming that via `onSettle` (see `0.94.8`'s own entry for why it must), but during that window the slide the container had already visually scrolled to had never been in the mount window and had never rendered at all — `SwipableMobileCarouselSlide` returns `null` outside it, not even a loading skeleton. Confirmed via `ffmpeg` frame-by-frame review (`List 4` fully loaded → fully blank, no title, for several consecutive frames) after scripted `touch_path` gestures on the simulator repeatedly failed to produce enough velocity to trigger a multi-slide native skip, making this untestable by the same live-repro method the last two fixes relied on. Fixed by adding `useSnapCarousel`'s `liveIndex` — a synchronous (not debounced) read of "which slide the scroll position is nearest right now," updated on every `scroll` event — and unioning it with the settled index when computing each slide's mount eligibility, so wherever the container currently visually is stays mounted regardless of whether that position has been confirmed as a real settle yet. **Caught and fixed a second, independent bug surfaced only by writing this fix's own regression test**: `SwipableMobileCarousel`'s `scrollToIndex` (from `useSnapCarousel`) was never memoized, so every `liveIndex` state change gave it a new function reference, which re-fired a `useEffect` keyed on `[clampedActiveIndex, scrollToIndex]` purely because of that reference change — calling `scrollToIndex` again, which (now that it also sets `liveIndex`) immediately reset `liveIndex` right back to the stale `clampedActiveIndex`, silently undoing the very fix being added. Wrapped `scrollToIndex` in `useCallback`. This was latent and harmless before `liveIndex` existed (the redundant call only re-issued an already-current `scrollTo()`), but would have made the whole fix a no-op in production, not just in tests, had the regression test not caught it before shipping. See `plugins/sovereign-plugin-tasks.local/docs/ux-improvement-plan.md` Task 13's second update for the full account. `0.94.8` → `0.94.9`: user feedback that `SwipableMobileCarouselDots`' `density="compact"` gap was still too wide even after `0.94.5`'s halving — `packages/ui` (now `0.56.4`) adds a `.dotsCompact .dot` override shrinking each dot's own hit target 20px → 16px in compact mode, on top of the existing gap reduction; the visible 6px dot is unchanged. `0.94.7` → `0.94.8` fixes a regression in `0.94.7`'s own fix, found live-testing that fix rather than by report — `useSnapCarousel` (`packages/ui`, now `0.56.3`) gated settle-detection on "was there a touch/wheel/pointer event within `debounceMs * 2` of settle-check time," which seemed to pass its own test suite but broke down under real momentum scrolling: a fast flick's native snap-correction can legitimately keep producing `scroll` events for longer than that fixed slack with zero further touch input, so the fixed window silently dropped the settle — leaving `activeIndex` stuck on the old slide while the container had already scrolled to the new one.
