Because `SwipableMobileCarousel`'s mount window is keyed off that same stale index, the slide the user was actually looking at could fall outside `prefetchDistance` and unmount entirely — a fully blank carousel, recoverable only by reloading the page, reported directly ("It is buggier now than before") with a second screen recording showing an ~8–10s blank stretch, and reproduced on demand with two real swipes fired back-to-back on the iPhone 17 Simulator (no artificial delay needed — this was reachable by an ordinarily fast double-swipe, not just the zero-delay stress case that first surfaced it). Redesigned around a "trusted run" model instead of a fixed slack window: a run of `scroll` events (no gap ≥ `debounceMs` between consecutive ones) is trusted if a real gesture happened within `debounceMs` of the run's _first_ event, and that trust holds for the run's entire duration no matter how long momentum continues afterward with no further input; a later real gesture can also upgrade an already-in-flight untrusted run. Verified against the exact live repro that broke the prior fix (three rapid real swipes on the simulator, not synthetic/scripted) before considering this closed, in addition to two new unit tests — one holding a trusted run open across 400ms of momentum-only scroll events with no touch input (well past the old fix's 240ms cap), one covering two independent real swipes fired with no gap between them. See `plugins/sovereign-plugin-tasks.local/docs/ux-improvement-plan.md` Task 13's update for the full account. `0.94.6` → `0.94.7` fixes a real "carousel auto-swipes back to the previous slide" bug in `useSnapCarousel` (`packages/ui`, now `0.56.2`), found by frame-by-frame review of a user-supplied screen recording rather than live testing (synthetic touch gestures hadn't reproduced it) — `ffmpeg` scene-change detection pulled ~28 candidate transition timestamps out of a 50s recording, and one of them showed `List 6` fully loaded, its task rows visibly vanishing, then `List 5` (the previous slide) fully loaded again, all within ~150ms with no further touch input visible on screen. Root cause: `handleScroll`'s debounced settle-check computed `Math.round(scrollLeft / width)` off of _any_ `scroll` event on the container, with no check that the event was caused by an actual user gesture — a `scroll-snap-type: mandatory` container should never move on its own, but a slide's content changing size while it's still loading (exactly what was happening to `List 6` in the recording) can nudge `scrollLeft` by a fraction of a slide width, and if that stray event's rounded index happened to differ from the currently-settled one, it was treated as a second, legitimate settle — reproduced deterministically in a unit test (a lone second `scroll` event, no other input, was enough to fire a second, wrong `onSettle`). Fixed by gating the settle-check on a recent real-input timestamp (touch/wheel/mouse-drag, refreshed on `touchstart`/`touchmove`/`wheel`/`pointerdown`/`pointermove`-with-a-button-held), with slack of `debounceMs * 2` past the last input to still allow the browser's own momentum/snap-correction to finish settling after the finger lifts. **First attempt used a second parallel `setTimeout` to track "is a gesture recently active" instead of a timestamp comparison — this broke every existing settle-detection test**, including ones unrelated to the new behavior: two independently-scheduled timers with the identical delay, started microseconds apart, have no guaranteed firing order, and in the test environment the gesture-deactivation timer consistently ran before the settle-check timer for the same tick, so every settle looked like drift and got silently dropped. Switched to comparing `Date.now()` against a plain ref at settle-check time instead, which sidesteps timer-ordering entirely. Also investigated, via the same recording, a green solid-color rectangle that appeared for a single frame and a rainbow-colored flash on the star column during a fast scroll — both cleared up within ±0.2s in finer-grained frame extraction and are consistent with a known WebKit momentum-scroll re-tiling/video-encoding artifact already documented elsewhere in this codebase (`TaskItem.module.css`'s `.rowContainer` comment), not app bugs; the list-header-loading complaint from the previous entry was reconfirmed working as designed (title shows immediately, e.g. `List 4` alone before its 7 tasks arrive) in the same recording. See `plugins/sovereign-plugin-tasks.local/docs/ux-improvement-plan.md` Task 13 for the full account, including exact recording timestamps. `0.94.5` → `0.94.6` fixes `SwipableMobileCarousel`'s slide indicator (`packages/ui`, now `0.56.1`) rendering completely unpositioned — a plain static DOM element pushed below the full-height scroller and clipped out of view — for any caller supplying a custom `renderIndicator`, discovered live-testing `sovereign-tasks`' mobile carousel against a fresh user report ("swiping still not stable, list header still not loading before tasks") on both the in-app browser preview and a real iOS Safari simulator session. Root cause: the overlay-positioning CSS class (`position: absolute; bottom: var(--sv-space-3)`, renamed `.dots` → `.indicatorSlot`) was applied directly to the _default_ `SwipableMobileCarouselDots` instance inside `SwipableMobileCarousel.tsx`, not to a wrapper around whatever `renderIndicator` returns — so `0.94.4`'s own `density="compact"` adoption (which necessarily passes a custom `renderIndicator` to forward the new prop, since the carousel's `renderIndicator` callback signature has no `density` passthrough) silently lost positioning entirely. Confirmed via live computed-style inspection (`getBoundingClientRect`/`getComputedStyle` in the browser preview) before touching code: `position: static`, sitting below `.scroller`'s full-height box. Fixed by always wrapping the resolved indicator (default or custom) in the carousel's own positioned slot; added a regression test (`SwipableMobileCarousel.test.tsx`) asserting both branches get `.indicatorSlot`. **The two originally-reported symptoms were investigated separately and not reproduced as described**: a cold list-page load and multiple clean swipes (including on a real iPhone 17 simulator) both showed the list header appearing immediately via the existing `SlideHeaderSkeleton` mechanism, with no blank flash. However, live touch-gesture testing surfaced a real, distinct, related problem, root-caused but **not yet fixed pending a product decision**: `sovereign-tasks`' whole-row long-press drag-reorder (`useReorderSensors`' `TouchSensor`, `app/_lib/dndSensors.ts`) and the carousel's horizontal swipe both claim touch gestures starting anywhere on a task row when `sortBy: 'manual'` (the default). A swipe with any realistic vertical wobble — reproduced with a diagonal touch path on the iPhone 17 simulator — can be captured by the row's drag sensor instead of the carousel: the swipe fails to navigate at all, and iOS Safari's known "sticky `:hover`-after-touch" quirk leaves the row's drag handle visibly stuck revealed afterward. Traced to `@dnd-kit/core`'s `TouchSensor.setup()`, which registers a page-lifetime non-passive `window` `touchmove` listener specifically "to force `event.preventDefault()` calls to work... required for iOS Safari" (the library's own code comment) — a documented dnd-kit/iOS Safari interaction, not a simple regression, and not blindly patched here since a real fix means either narrowing touch drag-initiation back to a dedicated handle (partially reverting the deliberate, documented v0.12 whole-row-touch-drag UX improvement) or another gesture-disambiguation strategy — a product trade-off, not a mechanical fix. See `plugins/sovereign-plugin-tasks.local/docs/ux-improvement-plan.md` Task 12 for the full write-up. `0.94.4` → `0.94.5` adds an opt-in `density?: 'default' | 'compact'` prop to `@sovereignfs/ui`'s `SwipableMobileCarouselDots` (`packages/ui`, now `0.56.0`): `'compact'` halves the gap between dots (`--sv-space-2` → `--sv-space-1`), for a carousel with enough slides that the default spacing reads as long/cramped on a narrow viewport — only the gap changes, each dot keeps its own 20px hit target. Fully backward compatible — defaults to `'default'`, every existing consumer's spacing is unchanged unless it opts in by forwarding the prop through a custom `renderIndicator`(Note: the version bump from `0.121.1` to `0.122.0` — `sdk.id` for UUID and short-ID generation — was made by another session without a matching entry here; not backfilled, since this session has no first-hand knowledge of what it contained.) (Note: the versions between `0.119.0` and `0.121.0` were bumped by other sessions without a matching entry here; not backfilled, since this session has no first-hand knowledge of what they contained.) `0.121.0` → `0.121.1` fixes a real bug reported directly, with root cause confirmed live and via a direct DB query (sqld HTTP pipeline API against `plugin_secrets`) during workstream 0023 leg 2's own manual verification: `plugins/account/app/data/page.tsx`'s `disconnectConnection(id)` only updated its local `connections` state after `DELETE /api/account/connections/:id` — that route's `disconnectPluginConnection()` (`packages/db/src/platform-db.ts`) atomically soft-deletes the connection's linked secret too when one exists (`deleted_at` confirmed set server-side immediately), but the page's separate `secrets` state (from `GET /api/account/secrets`) was never told to refresh, so "Saved app credentials" kept showing the now-deleted entry until the next full page load. Fixed with a small, targeted refetch of just `/api/account/secrets` after a successful disconnect — not a call to the page's existing `load()` (the pattern `allowRequest` already uses elsewhere in this same file), since `load()` shares one `loading` flag across all four of this page's sections and would have flashed "Loading…" across data-grants/device-grants/connections/secrets simultaneously for what should be a small, targeted correction — this keeps `disconnectConnection`'s existing optimistic `setConnections` filter untouched, exactly as asked. New regression test in `plugins/account/app/data/__tests__/page.test.tsx` (the existing `mockFetch` helper's static secrets response became stateful, mirroring the real atomic server-side deletion) confirmed to fail against the pre-fix code first (temporarily reverted via `git stash`, re-tested, restored) before being trusted as a real regression guard. Live browser verification was attempted but blocked by an unrelated, pre-existing issue in this shared dev database: the one currently-connected provider's linked secret fails to decrypt (`Unsupported state or unable to authenticate data`, the same `SOVEREIGN_VAULT_KEY`-mismatch class of bug documented elsewhere in this file), crashing Warden's settings page — the only live path to create a fresh connection+secret pair to disconnect — before it can render its "add provider" form; not fixed here as it's unrelated to this bug and touching the shared connected provider was avoided as unnecessarily destructive to other sessions' dev state. Full repository suite green (302 files, 2950 tests, 0 failed — one `backup-encryption.test.ts` scrypt-timeout flake under system load reproduced and confirmed to pass cleanly in isolation, unrelated to this change), `pnpm --filter runtime typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, and `pnpm format:check` all clean. `plugins/account/manifest.json` bumped `0.4.4` → `0.4.5`. `0.118.2` → `0.119.0` is epic task 14.6 (re-derived from RFC 0085's own deferred-future-work note): Account's mobile nav now matches the drill-down pattern Console shipped for its own mobile nav (task 13.17, workstream 0022) — a full-screen index of tappable section rows instead of the horizontal scrollable tab strip task 14.5 left untouched; selecting a row navigates into that section, which shows a "‹ Account" back-link instead of any persistent nav. A real correctness risk was found and resolved during planning, the same bug class as task 14.5's own rounds 3-4 (redirect/paint races inside this same Dialog architecture): the naive approach — fork the bare `/account` route's `redirect()` by viewport, redirecting on desktop and rendering an index on mobile — is unsafe, because `ResponsiveSurface`'s `useIsMobile()` defaults to `false` (desktop) on first paint and only corrects itself in a `useEffect`, and React fires child effects before parent effects within the same commit — a redirect-triggering effect in the desktop branch would fire on every load, including real mobile devices, before `useIsMobile`'s own corrective effect ever runs, permanently defeating the mobile index. Avoided entirely by never forking a _navigation_ decision by viewport, only forking _content_, mirroring exactly how Console's own bare `/console` route works (it never redirects, in either viewport — the fork only changes what renders): `plugins/account/app/page.tsx`'s `redirect('/account/profile')` is removed outright; the route now unconditionally renders a `ResponsiveSurface` between a mobile `NavList variant="drilldown"` index and a desktop `EmptyState` ("Select a section" / "Choose a section from the sidebar") — neither branch has a navigation side effect, so the race is harmless. New `plugins/account/app/_lib/sections.ts` (`ACCOUNT_SECTIONS` + `activeAccountSectionId`, mirroring `plugins/console/app/_lib/sections.ts`'s already-shipped shape) is now the single source of truth for both the desktop rail and the new mobile index, extracted from `layout.tsx`'s previously-inline `SECTIONS` const. `layout.tsx`'s mobile header now shows `<h1>Account</h1>` on the bare index route or a `‹ Account` back-link (also `replace`, not push, for the same history-stacking reason task 14.5's own rail links already documented) on any section route; `useOverlaySecondRow` is now called with `null` on the index route specifically, not the header content — Dialog's own `OverlayHeader` already renders "Account" as its row-1 mobile title on every page inside the dialog, so handing the same heading up as a second row on the index would have duplicated it, a real gap caught during planning before any code shipped. The mobile drilldown's own `NavList` links also need `replace`, not just the back-link — a second gap caught the same way: it's a brand-new navigation hop (index → section) inside a Dialog dismissed via `router.back()`, and a push there would mean a single back only returns to the index instead of exiting the dialog. `plugins/account/app/_components/ActiveNavLink.tsx` is deleted (confirmed via repo-wide grep its only consumer was the now-removed tab strip). **Investigated but found not applicable to this repo**: fixing the three minimal-shell account menus (`runtime/app/(minimal)/{docs,kanban,travellog}/_components/*AccountMenu.tsx`, which still link to bare `/account`) to avoid a regression from the redirect's removal turned out to be unnecessary — `git check-ignore` plus a byte-identical diff against each corresponding gitignored `.local` dev plugin confirmed all three are generated copies, not real committed source; there is nothing to fix in this repository, and the platform's one real, committed entry point (`AccountMenu.tsx`, already fixed in task 14.5's round 4) is unaffected. Verified live end to end against the real dev server: mobile (375px) Dialog-overlay case — opening Account from the sidebar avatar menu lands on `/account/profile` unchanged, the back-link to bare `/account` shows the drilldown list with no duplicate "Account" heading, tapping a row lands on that section with the back-link showing, and a single browser-back from a drilldown-entered section exits the dialog entirely (the critical regression check, confirmed via a real `navigate("back")` after drilling into Security, not just reasoned about); mobile standalone hard-nav case — bare `/account` shows the plain title and drilldown list with no Dialog chrome, a direct hard-nav to `/account/security` shows the inline back-link with the correct href; desktop — bare `/account` shows the rail beside the new `EmptyState`, and clicking a rail item correctly opens the Dialog with that section active and highlighted (client-side `<Link>` clicks trigger the `@modal` interception regardless of whether the current page was itself reached via a hard navigation, confirmed directly rather than assumed). No console errors observed across the full pass. Full `pnpm exec vitest run` (new `_lib/__tests__/sections.test.ts`, 5 tests, plus the full existing `plugins/account` suite, 66 tests, all green), `pnpm --filter runtime typecheck`, `pnpm --filter runtime build` (composed plugin directories are excluded from `runtime`'s own `tsc --noEmit` scope, so only a real build compiles Account's actual route files — confirmed `/account` grew from a 350 B redirect stub to real rendered content), `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check`, and `pnpm exec tsx scripts/design-tokens-check.ts` all green. `plugins/account/manifest.json` bumped `0.4.3` → `0.4.4`; no `packages/manifest` or `@sovereignfs/ui` bump — `NavList`, `ResponsiveSurface`, `EmptyState`, and `useOverlaySecondRow` are all reused unmodified; root platform bumped minor per this repo's per-task convention. `0.118.1` → `0.118.2` fixes a real bug in `@sovereignfs/ui`'s `useSnapCarousel` hook (`packages/ui/src/hooks/useSnapCarousel.ts`), found live-testing `sovereign-plugin-tasks.local`'s mobile carousel at the developer's direct request for a mobile-UI review: `scrollToIndex`'s programmatic `scrollTo({ behavior: 'smooth' })` — the path both the mobile footer's "Lists" button and the slide-dot indicator route external index jumps through — could silently do nothing at all, desyncing the visible slide from the dot/footer state with no error and no `scroll` event. First suspected `scroll-snap-type: mandatory` swallowing the call and fixed by temporarily suspending it for the duration of the animated scroll — but a live isolation test (a plain, scroll-snap-free throwaway `<div>` in the same automated-browser environment, given the same `scrollTo({behavior:'smooth'})` call) failed identically, so `scroll-snap-type` alone doesn't fully explain it and the exact trigger was never nailed down with certainty. Given that, the real fix is a correctness-net, not a narrow patch for one theory: once the animation should have finished (the `scrollend` event where supported, a 500ms timeout fallback otherwise — pre-17.4 iOS Safari/WKWebView has no `scrollend`), the hook now verifies the container actually reached the target and force-corrects with an instant `scrollTo` if not — cheap and idempotent when the smooth scroll did work, the difference between a graceful animation and an abrupt correction if it didn't, rather than a carousel that silently desyncs forever with nothing to ever correct it. A new `restoreSnapRef` also cancels any still-pending restore from an earlier smooth scroll before starting a new one, so an impatient double dot-tap can't race two suspend/restore cycles against each other. Verified live end-to-end against the real dev server: both the footer "Lists" button and dot-jump-to-slide now correctly move the carousel and repainted content, confirmed via direct `scrollLeft`/ratio checks and screenshots, not just inferred from no errors. Existing `useSnapCarousel.test.tsx` suite (8 tests) still passes unmodified — jsdom's mocked `scrollTo` doesn't exercise the new suspend/verify path at all, so no new automated regression coverage exists for this specific fix; confidence here rests on the live verification above. Full `packages/ui` suite (569 tests), `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check`, and `pnpm exec tsx scripts/design-tokens-check.ts` all green. `@sovereignfs/ui` bumped `0.81.0` → `0.81.1` (patch — internal bug fix, no API change). Two sibling fixes from the same mobile-UI review landed the same session in `sovereign-plugin-tasks.local`'s own separate repo, not this one — see that repo's own `CLAUDE.md` for the full account. `0.118.0` → `0.118.1` fixes a real CI failure reported directly by the developer via a failing GitHub Actions run link on a freshly pushed `v0.118.0` tag (the "Publish Docker Images" workflow's e2e job): `console-auditor.spec.ts`'s "auditor can view system health" test failed both attempts, `getByRole('heading', { name: 'System health' })` never becoming visible. Root-caused from the failure's own captured accessibility-tree snapshot, not guessed at: the Console Overview page (`/console`) rendered its sidebar and `<h1>` correctly but then hit a client-side error boundary — "Something went wrong." / "Insufficient privileges to manage apps." — the exact string `plugins/console/app/plugins/actions.ts`'s `requirePluginManage()` throws when a session lacks the `plugin:manage` capability. Traced to `getPluginCatalogAction()` in that same file: a plain read (fetches `/api/admin/plugins/catalog` to compute app-status breakdowns), reused by both `plugins/page.tsx`'s own management page and, via `buildPluginRows()`, Overview's app-count stats (`../page.tsx`) — but it opened with `await requirePluginManage()`, the same guard this file's real mutation actions (`togglePluginAction`, `activatePluginAction`, etc.) correctly use. `platform:auditor`'s capability set (`runtime/src/capabilities.ts`) grants `health:view`/`user:view`/`activity:view` but never `plugin:manage` (admin/owner only), so the auditor role — which Console's own sidebar and `console:access` capability otherwise let reach Overview — crashed the whole page the moment `buildPluginRows` awaited this call inside `ConsoleHome`'s `Promise.all`. Confirmed this wasn't Overview-only: `/console/plugins` itself has no client-side capability gating on its Activate/Toggle controls either (visible to every role that reaches the page, with only the underlying server actions enforcing `plugin:manage` per mutation) — so an auditor navigating there via the very sidebar link Console shows them would have hit the identical crash, just not caught by any existing e2e test. Fixed by dropping `getPluginCatalogAction()`'s capability check to a plain `sdk.auth.requireSession()`, matching the other read-only Overview helpers in `page.tsx` (`getUsers`/`getGroups`/`getEntitlements`, which perform no capability check at all beyond the existing `/console` middleware gate) — the real mutation actions below it in the same file are untouched and still independently enforce `plugin:manage` on every actual activate/toggle/access-policy write. New `catalog-actions.test.ts` (3 cases: succeeds for a session without `plugin:manage` and never calls `hasCapability`; still requires a session at all; returns `[]` rather than throwing on a non-ok upstream response) — confirmed to fail against the pre-fix code first (temporarily reverted via `git stash`, re-tested, restored) before being trusted as a real regression guard. Verified live end-to-end against a real dev server, not just the type system: signed in as the seeded `auditor@sovereign.local` account and confirmed `/console` now renders "At a glance"/"System health" in full with no error boundary, and that `/console/plugins` (previously also broken by the same bug, just untested) loads correctly too. Full repository suite (3913 tests, up from 3907), `pnpm lint`, `pnpm --filter runtime typecheck`, and `pnpm format:check` all green. `plugins/console` manifest `0.12.0` → `0.12.1`. `0.117.0` → `0.118.0` is a fourth same-PR round on task 14.5 (still unmerged — PR #600), fixing a distinct bug from rounds 1-3's sizing work: a real content-rendering gap, reported directly from a live screenshot showing the Account dialog's rail fully rendered while the content pane stayed completely blank ("the empty space when loading until the content loaded"). Root cause: `runtime/app/(platform)/_components/AccountMenu.tsx`'s sidebar avatar menu "Account" item used a client-side `next/link` `<Link href="/account">`, and `/account`'s own `page.tsx` `redirect()`s to `/account/profile` — for a hard navigation this resolves server-side before any client paint, but `<Link>`'s client-side soft navigation into the intercepted `@modal` route processes the redirect as a second async client-router fetch: the ancestor `layout.tsx` (the rail) mounts immediately, while `{children}` stays empty until that fetch resolves. The `.local`/minimal-shell equivalents (`DocsAccountMenu.tsx`/`KanbanAccountMenu.tsx`/`TravellogAccountMenu.tsx`) use plain `<a href="/account">` — always a hard navigation — confirmed unaffected and left unchanged. Fixed two ways: `AccountMenu.tsx`'s link now points directly at `/account/profile`, skipping the redirect for the primary entry point entirely — verified live to fully eliminate the gap, with even the first mid-transition screenshot now showing correct content. As defense-in-depth for any other path that could still hit an async-loading gap (a bookmarked bare `/account` URL, or a future section needing its own data fetch on first paint), added `plugins/account/app/loading.tsx` + `loading.module.css`: Next.js's `loading.tsx` convention wraps only `{children}`, not the sibling `layout.tsx`, in an automatic `<Suspense>` boundary, so the rail keeps rendering immediately and only the content pane shows a centered `@sovereignfs/ui` `Spinner` until ready. Verified live: hard navigation to a bare `/account` URL still redirects server-side with no visible gap, as before. `runtime` bumped `0.92.1` → `0.92.2`; `plugins/account/manifest.json` bumped `0.4.2` → `0.4.3`; root platform bumped minor per this branch's own established "one minor bump per round" convention. `0.116.0` → `0.117.0` is a third same-PR round on task 14.5 (still unmerged — PR #600), replacing round 2's `overlaySize: "auto"` with a new `DialogSize`, `"fixed"`. Round 2's own fix (documented in the previous entry) turned out to have a real, concrete flaw the developer hit directly: opening Account from the sidebar avatar visibly flashed a smaller dialog before the correctly-sized one appeared, and separately, the dialog itself read as too small ("appropriate for the content" was the ask) — `auto`'s 768px cap read as cramped compared to what a settings dialog should be. Both traced to the same root cause: `/account`'s own `page.tsx` (and its `@modal`-composed copy) client-side redirects to `/account/profile`, so the very first paint after opening the dialog briefly renders `AccountLayout`'s rail next to essentially no content — `auto`'s `fit-content` sizing measured that near-empty intermediate page, then visibly grew once the redirect landed and real content replaced it. No amount of width/min-height tuning on `auto` fixes a redirect-shaped gap in the route tree — only a size that doesn't measure content at all does. `@sovereignfs/ui` gained `fixed`: a true fixed box like `lg` (width AND height both set, content never resizes it) but capped at `64rem × 44rem` rather than filling the viewport. **First implementation attempt was wrong and caught before shipping**: named the new size `panel`, which collides with `Dialog.module.css`'s own always-applied `.panel` wrapper class every dialog gets regardless of size — the two rules would have silently merged into one, corrupting the shared base styling instead of producing a distinct size variant. Renamed to `fixed` once caught. Also considered, before settling on a new size at all, redefining `md` itself (unused by any shipped plugin per earlier research) to be fixed-height instead of content-driven — rejected after actually checking current `size="md"` consumers rather than trusting that stale research: `plugins/console/app/groups/ManageGroupDialog.tsx` (a real, shipped component from workstream 0022 leg 3) uses it, and several `.local` plugins' dialogs do too — redefining `md`'s fundamental behavior would have silently changed all of their visual behavior. `packages/manifest`'s `overlaySize` enum gained `"fixed"` alongside the already-shipped `"auto"` (kept — a plugin whose footprint varies a lot with no redirect-shaped gap in its own route tree is still a legitimate `auto` case, just not Account's own anymore); Account's manifest switched to it. `account.module.css`'s round-2 `width`/`min-height` grid overrides became unnecessary now that the panel itself is a true fixed box, and were removed. Docs updated everywhere `overlaySize` is documented as a public manifest field, again: `docs/plugin-development.md`, `docs/architecture-rules.md`, the SRS, `docs/plugins/account.md` — plus `Dialog.stories.tsx` (new `Fixed` story, Storybook hygiene rule) and a new `Dialog.test.tsx` case. Round 2's own task 14.5 doc entry and RFC 0085's "Update 2" block (both still unmerged) were extended in place rather than layered with yet another correction block, since neither had shipped to `main` yet. Verified live end to end against the real dev server: the panel is now visibly larger (1024×704 at a 1280×720 viewport, vs. `auto`'s 768px cap) and confirmed to never resize — checked both on first open (route freshly compiled) and on a subsequent open (route already compiled) that the mid-transition frame and the settled frame share identical box dimensions, and that Profile (short) and Security (tall) render at the identical box size; sticky rail and full scroll depth through Security's session list reconfirmed correct; mobile (375px) reconfirmed unaffected. A couple of live clicks during this session's own manual testing appeared not to register at all (URL never changed even after a multi-second wait, unlike the compile-latency false alarms seen earlier in this same investigation) — an immediate retry of the identical action succeeded instantly each time, consistent with an automation-tool click-registration quirk rather than an application bug, though not independently proven either way. Full `pnpm exec vitest run` (3478 passed), `pnpm --filter runtime build`, `pnpm --filter @sovereignfs/ui typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check`, and `pnpm exec tsx scripts/design-tokens-check.ts` all green. `@sovereignfs/ui` bumped `0.80.0` → `0.81.0` (new `DialogSize` value — additive, not breaking, so no `docs/upgrade.md` migration note, unlike `auto`'s own original breaking introduction); `packages/manifest` bumped `5.11.0` → `5.12.0`; `plugins/account/manifest.json` bumped `0.4.1` → `0.4.2`. `0.115.0` → `0.116.0` is a same-PR follow-up to task 14.5 (still unmerged as of this entry — PR #600), correcting that task's own sizing call rather than a new task: the developer flagged, from a live screenshot, that keeping `overlaySize: "lg"` (round 1's own deliberate choice, to avoid `md`'s resize-between-sections problem) produced a full-viewport dialog with distracting empty space around Account's rail+content pair — correct functionally, wrong visually, for what is a compact settings form. Their own first proposed fix — a fully custom, manifest-driven per-plugin dialog size — was evaluated and set aside as more public-contract surface than the problem needed (`Dialog` size is deliberately a small curated enum, not free-form per-plugin dimensions); the smaller fix instead extends that same enum. `@sovereignfs/ui`'s `auto` `DialogSize` (content-driven on both width and height, each capped at `min(48rem, 100%)`) already existed and already covered exactly this shape, but was reachable only from runtime-direct `<Dialog>` callers (e.g. Kanban's `CardDetailOverlay`) — `runtime/src/overlay.ts`'s own doc comment had explicitly named this as the intended extension point ("extend the manifest enum if a real plugin use case for manifest-declared `auto` ever surfaces"), so no new `packages/ui` component or `Dialog.module.css` change was needed, just widening `packages/manifest/src/schema.ts`'s `overlaySize` enum to include it and pointing `plugins/account/manifest.json` at it. `auto`'s `fit-content` sizing needed one further plugin-side adjustment to be usable for a 7-section rail+content layout, not just a single-card modal: `account.module.css`'s desktop grid gained an explicit `width: min(48rem, 100%)` (pins to `auto`'s own cap instead of measuring whatever the current section happens to render) and a light `min-height: 24rem` floor (keeps the shortest sections, e.g. Notifications, from rendering as a tiny, oddly-shaped stub) — this bounds but does not fully eliminate resize-between-sections the way `lg`'s true fixed box did, accepted as the right trade-off here. Docs updated beyond the manifest schema itself since `overlaySize` is part of the public plugin-manifest contract: `docs/plugin-development.md` (two references), `docs/architecture-rules.md` (the Dialog-size hard rule, including a drive-by fix of a stale `lg`/`full` reference — `full` was already retired), `docs/sovereign-proposal-plan-srs.md` (the living overlay-shell spec text and its manifest-reference code comment — its own dated Version 0.25 changelog entry left untouched as a historical record), and `docs/plugins/account.md`'s manifest reference. Round 1's own task 14.5 doc entry and RFC 0085's "Update 2" block (both still unmerged) were corrected in place rather than layered with another "Update 3", since neither had shipped to `main` yet. Verified live end to end against the real dev server: the dialog now renders as a compact, scrim-bounded, centered box for short sections (Profile) instead of filling the viewport, and grows for long ones (Security) up to the shared cap; sticky rail and full scroll depth through Security's session list both reconfirmed correct at the smaller size; mobile (375px) reconfirmed completely unaffected, since every `DialogSize` — `auto` included — collapses to a full-screen sheet below the mobile breakpoint regardless of value. A new `packages/manifest` regression test asserts `overlaySize: "auto"` validates. Full `pnpm exec vitest run` (3476 passed), `pnpm --filter runtime build`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check`, `pnpm exec tsx scripts/design-tokens-check.ts`, and `pnpm exec tsx scripts/check-doc-links.ts` (no new broken links) all green. `packages/manifest` bumped `5.10.0` → `5.11.0` (real schema capability addition, private/internal package per this file's own semver convention); `plugins/account/manifest.json` bumped `0.4.0` → `0.4.1`; no `@sovereignfs/ui` bump, since nothing in that package changed. `0.114.2` → `0.115.0` is epic task 14.5 (re-derived from RFC 0085): Account's hand-rolled horizontal `.tabs`/`.tab` strip is replaced with `@sovereignfs/ui`'s existing `NavList` component (`variant="static"`, one ungrouped group of the 7 sections — Profile/Security/Preferences/Notifications/Billing/Data/Activity), reusing RFC 0085's design intent without building the originally-proposed `NavRail` component, since `NavList` has no dependency on `ThreeColumnLayout`'s `data-plugin-fullbleed` height-unlock hook the way Console's own workstream 0022 conversion does, and drops straight into Account's `Dialog` unmodified. One deliberate deviation from this task's own scoping, found live during implementation rather than anticipated: the planned `overlaySize: "lg" → "md"` resize was dropped after `Dialog.tsx`'s own code comment surfaced a reason `lg` was chosen for Account in the first place — it's the only `DialogSize` that's a true fixed 100%/100% box, specifically so "the panel holds still while [Account] switch[es] internal views"; `md` is content-driven height (capped, not fixed), which would have made the dialog visibly grow and shrink every time a user moved between a short section (Profile) and a tall one (Security). Given a direct instruction not to regress existing behavior, `shell: "overlay"` and `overlaySize: "lg"` both stayed unchanged — only the nav orientation changed, with no `packages/ui` `Dialog.module.css` edit, `@sovereignfs/ui` version bump, or `docs/upgrade.md` migration note needed as a result. The rail instead uses `position: sticky` (relative to `Dialog`'s own scrolling `.content` region) to stay visible while a long section scrolls — verified live scrolled all the way through Security's TOTP/Passkeys/Sessions content with the rail never leaving view. `account.module.css`'s root layout was restructured from a flex column to a CSS Grid with a `grid-template-areas` swap per breakpoint (`mobileHeader` above `content` on mobile; `rail` beside `content` on desktop) specifically so `{children}` mounts exactly once regardless of viewport, rather than needing a `ResponsiveSurface`-style JS fork that would duplicate it into a mobile copy and a desktop copy — mobile itself is otherwise completely unchanged, confirmed live at 375px in both the standalone hard-navigation route and the Dialog-overlay case. The new rail's own links use `<Link replace>`, not a plain push, matching `ActiveNavLink`'s existing mobile-strip behavior and `CLAUDE.md`'s own overlay-navigation rule — Account stays inside a `Dialog` dismissed via `router.back()`, so push-based navigation between sections would stack history and turn a single back into "close one section" instead of "close the dialog"; verified live by visiting all 7 sections in sequence and confirming one back press still exits the dialog entirely. `NavList` requires a per-item icon (RFC 0085's original `NavRail` sketch had it optional) — 6 of the 7 sections already had a suitable icon in `packages/ui`'s curated set (`user`/`shield`/`sliders-horizontal`/`bell`/`lock`/`activity`); `credit-card` was added for Billing via the established convention (`scripts/icon-list.ts` + `pnpm generate:icons`), matching how workstream 0022 leg 3 added `panel-left`/`pin` — that same commit (`4ae79d85`) confirmed a curated-icon-only addition doesn't require bumping `@sovereignfs/ui`'s own `package.json`, so this didn't either. A wireframe (`docs/adhoc/account-vertical-nav.md` + one SVG) was produced and shown to the developer for sign-off before any code was written, per the `sv-ui-design` skill's wireframe-before-build process. Verified live end to end against a real dev login (`user@sovereign.local`): all 7 sections reachable via the rail both inside the Dialog and via direct `/account/<section>` hard navigation, active-section highlighting correct in both, no console errors. A few apparent content/navigation desyncs during manual testing turned out to be dev-server on-demand route-compile latency outrunning an immediate screenshot check, not real bugs — each resolved correctly once re-checked after a short wait; confirmed genuinely clean via a full `pnpm exec vitest run` (3475 passed) with the dev server stopped, after one single flaky timeout in `admin-route-guards.test.ts` (unrelated to this change, a resource-contention artifact of running the suite alongside the live dev server) was independently confirmed to pass in isolation. `pnpm --filter runtime build` also run specifically because composed plugin directories are excluded from `runtime`'s own `tsc --noEmit` scope (`docs/architecture-rules.md`), so only a real build compiles Account's actual route files; `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check`, and `pnpm exec tsx scripts/design-tokens-check.ts` all green. `plugins/account/manifest.json` bumped `0.3.7` → `0.4.0`. `0.114.1` → `0.114.2` fixes a real bug reported directly by the developer, with a screenshot: in Console's Groups/Users/Plugins/External-clients desktop detail column, switching selection (e.g. clicking a different group card) left the pane's `defaultValue`-seeded form fields — `GroupDetailFields`' Name/Description inputs, `RoleSelect`'s role dropdown — frozen on whichever row was selected first, even though the header, id, and members list all updated correctly. Root-caused, not guessed at: `groups/page.tsx` (and `users/page.tsx`) already had a `key={selectedGroup.id}` on the detail-pane element, added in `015427a5` specifically to force a remount on selection change — and it looked like it should work, but a live reference-identity probe (`document.querySelector('input[name="name"]') === previousReference` across a selection switch) proved the underlying DOM node never actually changed. Traced with targeted instrumentation, not assumption: `ConsoleDetailSlot`/`useConsoleDetailPane` hands the selected row's detail-pane element to `ConsoleLayout` as a stored `ReactNode` via a context callback — since that element is produced by a Server Component (`GroupsPage`) and crosses into a Client Component's state as a `children` value, not as a direct sibling in the same JSX expression, Next.js's RSC (Flight) protocol hands it across that boundary as an opaque not-yet-resolved reference object (`$$typeof`/`_payload`/`_init`), not a plain React element — confirmed live by logging the object's own enumerable keys. That wrapper has no `.key` of its own (the key lives on the _inner_ element it eventually resolves to), so by the time `ThreeColumnLayout`'s `Children.toArray` — confirmed via its own logged output — read `.key` off it, it found none and fell back to a stable positional key, so React never saw a change and just updated the existing fiber in place. Fixed generically at the shared `_lib/detail-pane.tsx`/`_components/ConsoleDetailSlot.tsx`/`layout.tsx` layer, not per-page: a new required `detailKey` prop (a plain string, e.g. the selected row's id) threads through ordinary props/context/state — no element-identity games — and `ConsoleLayout` applies it as a `key` on a `Fragment` it creates itself, entirely client-side, which reconciliation actually honors regardless of what RSC-crossed content sits inside. All four call sites (Groups/Users/Plugins/External clients) updated; the now-inert per-page `key` props on `GroupDetailPane`/`UserDetailPane` were removed along with the comments that had (incorrectly) credited them with fixing this. Two small, directly-requested additions landed in the same pass since they touch the same Groups screen: each group card now shows its member count (`listUserGroupsWithMemberCount`, `packages/db` — a single `LEFT JOIN`/`GROUP BY` query, not one count query per group, so `listUserGroups` itself stays unwidened for callers that don't need it), and the sidebar's member rows gain a real `Avatar` (`@sovereignfs/ui`, already existed — no DS gap) instead of no avatar at all, with `ResolvedGroupMember` widened to carry `image` from `sdk.directory.resolveUsers()` through to it. Verified live end-to-end against the real dev server, not just reasoned about: reproduced the exact reported sequence (select "Group Name", select "Group Name 2" — Name field stuck on "Group Name", member row with no avatar) against the pre-fix code, then confirmed both the Name/Description fields and the member avatar update correctly after the fix, in both switch directions, plus confirmed `UserDetailPane`'s selection switch (owner → auditor → user) still renders correctly post-fix even though `RoleSelect`'s editable dropdown itself doesn't render in this dev environment (gated at verification level 2/MFA, which no seeded account has — expected, unrelated to this fix). One existing test updated (`groups/__tests__/actions.test.ts`'s `listResolvedGroupMembers` case, to cover the new `image` field); full repository suite (3325 tests), `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check`, and `pnpm design:tokens:check` all green. `@sovereignfs/db` `4.8.3` → `4.9.0` (minor — new exported `listUserGroupsWithMemberCount`); `plugins/console` manifest `0.11.0` → `0.12.0`. Two small follow-up spacing fixes landed in the same PR before merge, both from direct visual review of the shipped change: `.compactRowIdentity` (the `Avatar`+label wrapper) gained `padding-left: var(--sv-space-2)`, since the round `Avatar` sitting flush against `.compactRow`'s own equal padding read as tighter than the `Remove` button on the opposite side even though both sides measured equal in the box model; and `.compactList`'s own leftover nonzero padding — stacking with `.compactRow`'s per-row padding — was zeroed out, since `.compactRow` already owns the full inset. `0.114.0` → `0.114.1` fixes a real layout bug the developer found live-testing `0.114.0`'s own density change, with two before/after screenshots of `/warden` in the sidebar-visible and sidebar-hidden states: the vertically-centered empty state ("Turn on a model to get started" / composer) sat at a visibly different height depending on whether the sidebar was shown or hidden — the developer's own framing was "top and bottom margins stays the same" regardless of sidebar visibility. Root cause was in `WardenLayoutShell.tsx`/`warden-layout-shell.module.css`, not the density change itself: the collapsed-sidebar branch renders a `.toggleBar` (holding the "show sidebar" button) as an in-flow flex sibling above `.content`, while the expanded branch has no such element at all — its own toggle lives inside the sidebar column instead, injected via `cloneElement`. Since `.content`'s children (`ChatView`'s `.chat.chatCentered`, `align-items: center` / `justify-content: center` on a `height: 100%` box) center within whatever height `.content` actually has, and `.toggleBar` shrank that height only in the collapsed state, the centered content's vertical midpoint shifted down by exactly half the toggle bar's height whenever the sidebar was hidden — confirmed by the math, not just the screenshots (`.content` starts at `y = toggleBarHeight` and is `toggleBarHeight` shorter, so its center is `toggleBarHeight / 2` lower than the full-height expanded case). Fixed by making `.toggleBar` an absolutely-positioned overlay (`position: absolute; top: 0; left: 0`) instead of an in-flow flex sibling, so `.content` is always the full `.mainColumn` height in both states — the toggle button now floats over the top-left corner of the content area when collapsed, the same corner it already occupies inside the sidebar when expanded, rather than pushing content down. Verified live end-to-end against the real dev server (which the developer already had running independently outside this session's own preview tooling — attached to it directly rather than starting a redundant second instance): added a real OpenRouter provider to reach the exact "Turn on a model to get started" state from the developer's own screenshots, then used `getBoundingClientRect()` to confirm the empty-state heading and composer `textarea` land at the identical `y` coordinate (`216.5`/`432.5`) in both the expanded and collapsed states — pixel-identical, only `x` differs with the available width — before removing the test provider again. No new automated test: this is a pure visual-centering regression with no behavioral assertion to hang a unit test on beyond what the live pixel comparison already proved; the existing `WardenLayoutShell.test.tsx` suite (asserting `.toggleBar` renders only when collapsed) already covers the element's presence and was unaffected by switching its CSS positioning. Full `plugins/warden` suite (303 tests), `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check`, and `pnpm design:tokens:check` all green. `plugins/warden` manifest `0.9.7` → `0.9.8`; root platform version bumped patch to `0.114.1` (this fix, layered onto the same still-open `feat/nav-list-compact-density` branch/PR the developer was already reviewing when they found it, rather than a separate branch for an unrelated one-line CSS positioning fix). `0.113.2` → `0.114.0` adds an opt-in `density?: 'default' | 'compact'` prop to `@sovereignfs/ui`'s `NavList` (`packages/ui`, now `0.80.0`), requested directly by the developer against a screenshot of Warden's own sidebar ("Make the gap between navigations in the sidebar a bit smaller"). Root cause: the visible spacing between Warden's "New chat"/"Providers"/"Models" rows (`WardenSidebar.tsx`, added in the `0.112.3` sidebar-nav batch) is dominated by `NavList.module.css`'s `.row` rule — a full `--sv-touch-target-min` (44px) minimum height plus `--sv-space-2` vertical padding — not by the surrounding `.group`'s already-tight 2px `gap`. `NavList` is also Console's own sidebar component (`plugins/console/app/layout.tsx`), so a local Warden-only CSS override was never on the table — per this file's own DS-first convention ("reusable UI/UX capability... implemented in `packages/ui`... never implemented plugin-locally") and the precedent `0.112.0`'s own `SwipableMobileCarouselDots` `density="compact"` prop already set for exactly this shape of problem (an opt-in density variant on an existing published component, defaulting to the existing behavior so every other consumer is unaffected). `density="compact"` shrinks `variant="static"` rows to `min-height: 0` with `--sv-space-1` padding — full-height `variant="drilldown"` rows are untouched regardless of `density`, since that variant is mobile-only and its touch-target size isn't optional (documented explicitly in the new prop's own doc comment, not left implicit). `WardenSidebar.tsx`'s own `<NavList>` call is the only consumer to opt in; Console's sidebar passes no `density` prop and needed no code change at all — confirmed both by reading its call site (no `density` argument) and by a new unit test asserting the default render carries no compact class. Verified live end-to-end against the real dev server, not just the type system: added a real OpenRouter provider (a fake key, since only its public `/models` list is needed) to the seeded `user@sovereign.local` account, confirmed via `getComputedStyle` that each nav row shrank from the default's 44px tall box to 26px (`padding: 4px 12px`, `min-height: 0`), then removed the test provider again to leave the account clean. (The verification session's own preview dev server died silently partway through with nothing in its own logs and nothing listening on :3000 — unrelated to this change, recovered by a plain restart.) Two new `NavList.test.tsx` cases (default carries no compact class; `density="compact"` applies it, both asserted via a direct `NavList.module.css` import rather than a string literal, so the assertion tracks the real generated class name) plus a new `CompactDensity` story (`NavList.stories.tsx`) and an updated usage blurb in `DesignSystemOverview.stories.tsx`, per this file's own Storybook-hygiene convention. `pnpm --filter @sovereignfs/ui typecheck`, `pnpm design:tokens:check`, the full `plugins/warden` suite (303 tests), `pnpm format:check`, and `pnpm --filter runtime build` all green. `@sovereignfs/ui` bumped `0.79.0` → `0.80.0` (minor — a new additive public prop on a published design-system package); `plugins/warden` manifest `0.9.6` → `0.9.7` (patch, as a consuming plugin); root platform version bumped minor to `0.114.0`, matching the exact precedent the `SwipeStack` entry (`0.112.0`) set for a `packages/ui` addition. `0.113.1` → `0.113.2` fixes the same input-fields-stay-editable-while-pending bug `0.112.5` fixed in `AddProviderForm.tsx`, this time in its sibling `ProviderRow.tsx` — flagged as a separate follow-up in that same entry rather than fixed inline, since it wasn't part of what was originally reported. `ProviderRow`'s edit form (Name/Base URL/API key) had no `disabled={updatePending}` on its three `Input`s, unlike the Save button, which already reflected it — the exact same gap, in the sibling component. Fixed by adding `disabled={updatePending}` to each of the three inputs, matching `AddProviderForm.tsx`'s own fix. New regression test in `ProviderRow.test.tsx` (mirroring `AddProviderForm.test.tsx`'s own "disables the input fields while the submission is pending" test): opens the edit form, submits, and asserts all three fields report `disabled: true` while the mocked `updateProviderAction`'s promise is unresolved, then `false` once it resolves — confirmed to fail against the pre-fix code (reverted locally via `git stash`, re-tested, restored) before being trusted as a real regression guard. Full `plugins/warden` suite (303 tests, up from 302), `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check` all green. `plugins/warden` manifest `0.9.5` → `0.9.6`. `0.113.0` → `0.113.1` fixes a real CI failure reported directly by the developer via a failing pipeline link on an open PR: `pnpm audit --prod --audit-level=high` started failing on `main` itself, unrelated to that PR's own changes — confirmed by reproducing it locally against an unmodified checkout and by checking `dependency-audit.yml`'s last scheduled run against `main` (2026-08-31, green), meaning the underlying advisories were disclosed after that and before this session. Four newly-published `fast-uri` GHSAs (host confusion / SSRF via malformed URI, IDN, and IPv6 normalization — GHSA-5jgf-p345-68v8, GHSA-f65p-4m7j-42xc, GHSA-fph4-wmhf-6fwf, GHSA-jqff-g426-hqxp) are reachable via `ajv`, itself pulled in from two unrelated directions (`packages/mailer` → `react-email` → `conf` → `ajv`, and `runtime` → `@ducanh2912/next-pwa` → `webpack` → `schema-utils` → `ajv`), neither of which pins `ajv`'s own `fast-uri` dependency directly. Fixed the same way the existing `nanoid` GHSA-2v37-7h3g-55p8 override already models in `pnpm-workspace.yaml`: a workspace-level `overrides` entry forcing every resolution in the tree to a patched line, rather than an `ignoreGhsas` exception, since a real fix is available. First attempt used an unqualified `fast-uri: '>=3.1.6'`, which technically satisfies every advisory but let pnpm resolve the _latest_ satisfying version tree-wide — `4.1.3`, a major version past what the actual consumer, `ajv@8.20.0`, declares support for (`"fast-uri": "^3.0.1"` in its own `package.json`). Caught before pushing, not assumed safe: corrected to `'>=3.1.6 <4.0.0'`, which resolves to exactly `3.1.6` — the patched line within `ajv`'s own declared range, and the actually low-risk fix the `nanoid` override's own comment claims to be the standard here. Regenerating the lockfile needed its own care: this checkout has ten gitignored `.local` dev-plugin clones on disk that `pnpm-workspace.yaml`'s `plugins/*` glob picks up as real workspace members, so a normal `pnpm install` produced a 1300+ line lockfile diff dominated by their first-time importer entries (expected per this file's own environment-notes section, not a sign of a bad fix) — moved them out of `plugins/` to a scratch path for the `pnpm install` run and back immediately after, confirmed via `git status` that no other files were touched, and diffed the _first_ (unqualified) and _second_ (capped) lockfile results side by side to confirm the second genuinely differed only in landing on `3.1.6` instead of `4.1.3`. Verified `pnpm audit --prod --audit-level=high` exits `0` locally (the remaining 9 findings are all in the pre-existing `ignoreGhsas` list), plus `pnpm format:check` and `pnpm lint`, both clean. No application code changed — `fast-uri` is an internal implementation detail of `ajv`'s schema validation, not something either `packages/mailer` or `runtime` calls directly. (Note: the version bump from `0.112.5` to `0.113.0` was made by another session without a matching entry here; not backfilled, since this session has no first-hand knowledge of what it contained.) `0.112.4` → `0.112.5` fixes two more real bugs reported directly by the developer while re-testing `0.112.4`'s own fix: "it didn't reload the page... had to reload twice to see the chat message," plus "the form fields were editable" while a toast was already showing. Root-caused, not guessed at, both times: (1) `SetupPrompt`'s `onAdded` called `router.push('/warden')`, but this component only ever renders _at_ `/warden` (`page.tsx`'s `!hasAnyModel` branch) — there was no URL to navigate to. `push()` to a route's own current URL doesn't reliably force Next's client Router Cache to discard the already-rendered (stale, `!hasAnyModel`) payload for that exact URL and re-run the Server Component; `router.refresh()` is the explicit "re-fetch this route's server data" call, and is what `ProvidersView`'s own `refresh` already uses after every mutation — `onAdded` now calls that instead. (2) `AddProviderForm`'s `Name`/`Base URL`/`API key` `Input`s had no `disabled={pending}`, unlike the submit button — matching `ProviderRow.tsx`'s own edit form, which has the identical gap (not touched here; flagged as a separate follow-up, since it wasn't part of what was reported). Verified live against the real dev server, not just reasoned about: filled and submitted the form, and this time the chat view appeared in place with a single toast and no manual reload — server logs confirmed exactly one `POST /warden` and one `GET /warden`, same single-shot shape `0.112.4` already established for the request count, now paired with the response actually landing correctly the first time. New/updated tests: `SetupPrompt.test.tsx`'s redirect assertion now checks `router.refresh()` instead of `push()`; a new `AddProviderForm.test.tsx` case fills the form, submits, and asserts all three fields report `disabled: true` while the mocked action's promise is still unresolved, then `false` again once it resolves. Full `plugins/warden` suite (302 tests), `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check` all green. `plugins/warden` manifest `0.9.4` → `0.9.5`. `0.112.3` → `0.112.4` fixes a real bug reported directly by the developer with a screenshot: adding Warden's first provider from the setup screen produced four stacked "OpenRouter was added." toasts over several seconds — described as looking like a loop. Root-caused, not guessed at: `AddProviderForm`'s success `useEffect` (`app/_components/AddProviderForm.tsx`) lists its `onAdded` prop as a dependency (required — it's a value the effect closes over), but `SetupPrompt` passed `onAdded={() => router.push('/warden')}`, a fresh closure every render. `router.push('/warden')` while already on `/warden` still re-renders the segment (a soft navigation, not a true no-op), handing the effect a new `onAdded` reference on its own — which alone re-triggers it, since React reruns an effect whenever any dependency's identity changes, independent of whether `state` (from `useActionState`) actually changed. Each re-run replayed the stale success toast and called `onAdded()` again, pushing again, re-rendering again — a loop bounded only by how many render/effect cycles occurred before the server-rendered tree caught up and unmounted `SetupPrompt` in favor of `ChatView`. Confirmed `ProvidersView.tsx`, `AddProviderForm`'s other consumer, was never affected: its own `onAdded` (`refresh`) is already wrapped in `useCallback`, giving it a stable reference across re-renders — the established pattern `SetupPrompt` just hadn't followed. Fixed two ways, not just the minimal one: (1) `SetupPrompt` now memoizes its callback the same way (`useCallback(() => router.push('/warden'), [router])`); (2) `AddProviderForm` itself gained a `handledStateRef` guard so its success effect only fires once per distinct `state` object, regardless of whether a future caller remembers to memoize its own callback — a defense-in-depth fix, not just papering over this one call site. Verified the mechanism, not just the symptom: a new regression test (`AddProviderForm.test.tsx`) simulates exactly this — re-rendering with a new `onAdded` identity and no new submission — and was confirmed to fail against the pre-fix code (reverted locally, re-tested, restored) before being trusted as a real regression guard. Also verified live end-to-end against the real dev server: reproduced the original failure first (a leftover "OpenRouter" provider from the developer's own repro was still sitting in the shared dev database, confirming only one row existed despite four toasts — proof this was always a pure render loop, never a quadruple database write), then removed it and re-added a fresh provider with the fix in place — server logs showed exactly one `POST /warden` and one follow-up `GET /warden`, no repeated cycle, landing cleanly on the chat screen. Full `plugins/warden` suite (301 tests, up from 299), `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check` all green. `plugins/warden` manifest `0.9.3` → `0.9.4`. `0.112.2` → `0.112.3` is a batch of Warden sidebar/navigation UX changes, requested directly by the developer with annotated screenshots, landed together since they all touch the same two components: (1) `SetupPrompt`'s `onAdded` redirect changed from `/warden/settings?tab=providers` back to plain `/warden` — the developer's own follow-up correction to `0.112.2`'s just-shipped behavior ("I changed my mind"), since a user who came here to chat should land in the chat view once a model is reachable, not in Settings. (2) `ChatView`'s "Manage models" empty-state action was a bare `<Link>` with default browser underline styling, inconsistent with every other `EmptyState` `action` in this codebase (confirmed by grep — always a `<Button>`, `SetupPrompt`'s own "Add a provider" included); wrapped it in `<Button variant="secondary" size="sm">` to match. (3)-(5) `WardenLayoutShell`'s collapsible sidebar now defaults to **hidden** (`useState(true)`, with an explicit stored `'0'` — the user expanded it before — as the only thing that overrides the default; previously defaulted open), and the collapse toggle button relocates with visibility instead of always living in the main column: collapsed, it stays in the main column (the only place left to put it); expanded, `WardenLayoutShell` injects it into `WardenSidebar` via `cloneElement('onToggleCollapse')` so the control that hides the sidebar lives inside the thing it hides, matching the requested Claude-style placement. `WardenSidebar`'s own former `.newSessionRow` pill button is gone — "New chat" is now the first row of a new `NavList` (`variant="static"`, `@sovereignfs/ui` — DS-first per this file's own design-system rule, reusing the same icon+label/hover/active row component Console's sidebar already uses, rather than hand-rolling near-duplicate CSS in `warden-sidebar.module.css`) that also gains **Providers** and **Models** rows beneath it, linking straight to `/warden/settings?tab=providers`/`?tab=models` — previously reachable only via the Settings page's own tabs. Verified live end-to-end against the real dev server: adding a real provider (OpenRouter's endpoint, a fake key) from the first-run setup screen now lands on `/warden` with the newly-styled "Manage models" button visible; the sidebar loads collapsed by default with just the main-column toggle showing; clicking it reveals the sidebar with the toggle now inside it, plus New chat/Providers/Models rows above the (empty) session list; clicking "Providers" navigates correctly. Test updates across all four touched components' suites (`SetupPrompt.test.tsx`'s redirect assertion; `WardenLayoutShell.test.tsx` rewritten around the new default-collapsed behavior and a fixture sidebar that renders its own injected `onToggleCollapse` button, the same way `WardenSidebar` really does; `WardenSidebar.test.tsx` gains coverage for the two new nav links and the conditional collapse button, plus a `next/navigation` mock update for the newly-added `usePathname`/`useSearchParams` calls) — full `plugins/warden` suite (299 tests, up from 296), `pnpm --filter runtime build`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check`, `pnpm design:tokens:check` all green. `plugins/warden` manifest `0.9.2` → `0.9.3`. `0.112.1` → `0.112.2` is a UX fix for Warden's first-run empty state, requested directly by the developer with a before/after screenshot: `SetupPrompt`'s "Add a provider" button was a `<Link href="/warden/settings?tab=providers">`, so a brand-new user with no provider configured yet was sent straight to Settings before they'd had any chance to act on this screen. Changed to a client-side toggle — clicking it now expands the same `AddProviderForm` `ProvidersView` already uses (unmodified, just a new call site) in place, wrapped in a new `.setupForm` class (`warden.module.css`, `max-width: 32rem`, matching the existing `.errorCard` convention for a centered card inside `.emptyState`) rather than stretching to the full fullbleed width. Once the provider is actually created, `onAdded` now calls `router.push('/warden/settings?tab=providers')` — sending the user on to Settings only _after_ there's something there to see, not before. `SetupPrompt.tsx` became a client component for this (`useState`/`useRouter`), the same pattern `ProvidersView.tsx` already uses for the identical form. Verified live end-to-end against the real dev server: clicking "Add a provider" expands the form with no navigation (confirmed via the accessibility tree — the trigger is a `button`, not a link, and the URL never changes), and submitting a real provider (OpenRouter's actual endpoint, a fake key — its `/models` list doesn't require a valid one) correctly redirects to `/warden/settings?tab=providers`, where the newly added provider shows "Connected · 419 models". New `SetupPrompt.test.tsx` (3 tests: form hidden until clicked, expands without calling `router.push`, redirects on a successful add) — full `plugins/warden` suite (296 tests, up from 293), `pnpm --filter runtime build`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check` all green. `plugins/warden` manifest `0.9.1` → `0.9.2`. `0.112.0` → `0.112.1` fixes a real "Warden takes some time to open" complaint reported directly by the developer. Root-caused, not guessed at: `WardenPage` (`plugins/warden/app/page.tsx`) and `/warden/settings` (`app/settings/page.tsx`) both block their entire render on `discoverModels()` before sending any HTML — a live health probe against `apps/harness` plus a live `/models` fetch against every configured provider — and neither route had a `loading.tsx`, so a slow discovery pass left the browser showing nothing at all until every await resolved. Two independent fixes, verified together against the real dev server: (1) `checkHarnessHealth()`'s fetch (`harness-client.ts`) had no timeout at all, unlike `fetchProviderModels()`'s explicit 8s one — bounded it at a new `HARNESS_HEALTH_TIMEOUT_MS` (3s), so a stalled-but-not-cleanly-refused `apps/harness` can no longer hang the whole page indefinitely; (2) added `loading.tsx` for both `/warden` (fullbleed, reusing `warden.module.css`'s existing `.page`/`.emptyState` classes so the shell's hard-locked-viewport treatment matches) and `/warden/settings` (the `PageContainer`+`Spinner`+`.centered` convention already established by `sovereign-plugin-kanban`/`sovereign-plugin-travellog`'s own `loading.tsx` files). Verified live end-to-end, not just reasoned about: temporarily added a provider pointed at `10.255.255.1` (an unroutable "black hole" address — hangs rather than cleanly refusing, the exact failure mode a timeout guards against) against the real shared dev database, confirmed the dev server logs showed `GET /warden` genuinely taking 8855ms and `GET /warden/settings` 8289ms (the provider-fetch timeout, working as designed), then used a raw streamed `fetch()` read loop to prove the fix's actual effect: the first HTML chunk — containing the `loading.tsx` Spinner's "Loading Warden…" text — arrived in 7ms, while the full response with real content still took ~8.2s. That's the actual fix: the page now paints something immediately instead of appearing frozen, even though the underlying live-discovery latency for a genuinely slow/unreachable provider is unchanged (and shouldn't be masked — it's real information the Providers page surfaces). The test provider was removed again afterward, confirmed via a follow-up screenshot showing the account back to "No providers configured yet." Two new `harness-client.test.ts` cases lock in the timeout (asserting an `AbortSignal` is passed, and that a `TimeoutError` rejection is mapped to `unreachable` same as any other fetch failure) — full `plugins/warden` suite (293 tests, up from 291), `pnpm --filter runtime build` (needed since composed plugin directories are excluded from `runtime`'s own `tsc --noEmit` scope, and this change added two new route files), `pnpm lint`, and `pnpm format:check` all green. `plugins/warden` manifest `0.9.0` → `0.9.1`. `0.111.0` → `0.112.0` adds `SwipeStack` + `SwipeStackCard` to `@sovereignfs/ui` (PR #578, already merged; `packages/ui` `0.74.0` → `0.75.0`) plus this follow-up's own `docs/design-system.md` write-up and root version bump — not a roadmap task, an ad-hoc DS addition the developer asked for directly. `SwipeStack` is a mobile compound component for one-at-a-time card triage (drag left/right/up/down, dating-app-style) — the design system's first free-2D-drag interaction, alongside a `useSwipeStack` hook that extends `useSwipeReveal`'s hand-rolled Pointer Events technique (deadzone, transform written straight to a ref, threshold-only resolution — no velocity/fling) from one axis with two outcomes to four directions with a tilt. Design was worked out in a real back-and-forth with the developer before any code was written (see the `sv-ui-design` skill's wireframe-before-build spirit, applied via conversation rather than an SVG spec, since this was a single new component, not a multi-screen feature): the DS layer carries zero domain vocabulary (a `directions` config plus one `onSwipe(direction, cardId)` callback — the caller supplies label/icon per direction and decides what "up" means); every direction is independently optional and a direction left out is a wall, not just an unreachable outcome — `touch-action` is derived per-instance from which axes are actually configured, so an unused axis stays native scroll; behavior is uniform and forward-only, no reversible "go back" — `SwipeStack` tracks its own dismissed-card set locally so a swiped card never reappears even if the caller is slow to remove it from `children`; and the non-gesture fallback (a button per configured direction) is required in v1, not a follow-up, wired to the identical `triggerCommit` path and exit animation a real drag produces rather than a separate code path — the design skill's a11y bar for a DS component third-party plugins adopt as-is. Verification for the original PR caught two real, unrelated-to-the-feature gaps at push time: `runtime`'s typecheck failed on missing `runtime/generated/*` (this working tree had never run `pnpm generate` that session) and `pnpm design:tokens:check` initially missed the new CSS file's undefined-token usage entirely, since it scans `git ls-files` and the file was still untracked on the first local run — both fixed (the second via the script's existing `TOKEN_ALLOWLIST` escape hatch, already used for three other JS-computed custom properties). This follow-up's own doc pass added a `docs/design-system.md` "Swipe stack (card triage)" section matching the carousel section's depth; the other docs referencing `SwipableMobileCarousel` (`docs/plugin-development.md`, `docs/epics/design-system.md`, `docs/architecture-rules.md`) were checked and deliberately left alone — each is a point-in-time record of that specific task's or example plugin's own scope, not a living component index, so retrofitting them would misrepresent what those tasks actually built. `0.110.0` → `0.111.0` is workstream 0022 leg 5 (task 13.21), the final leg of the Console shell/`ThreeColumnLayout` conversion: `/console/oauth-clients` gains a 3rd detail column, but unlike legs 2-4 there was no dialog to replace — reading `OAuthClientsClient.tsx` (per this leg's own instruction, not assumed) confirmed every field on a client card (name, ID, redirect URIs, status) was already visible inline, and the only "reveal" state (a freshly created/rotated secret) is a page-level, one-time-only banner unrelated to selection. Escalated per the workstream's own "do not proceed if" clause rather than deciding unilaterally; the developer chose to add the pane anyway for visual consistency with the other three converted pages, not because it reveals new information — its only real job is relocating the Rotate secret/Revoke buttons off the card. Implemented with `useSearchParams()` called directly inside `OAuthClientsClient.tsx` (a new `OAuthClientDetailPane.tsx` component) rather than a `page.tsx` `searchParams` prop, since this page — unlike Users/Groups/Plugins — has no server-side data fetch to thread one through; everything here is client-driven against `/api/auth/oauth2/*`. `<Link href="?client=<id>">` still keeps selection linkable, matching legs 2-4's convention. The revealed-secret banner was deliberately left exactly where it already rendered (page-level, not pane-scoped) rather than moved for cosmetic symmetry — it's a security-sensitive, already-well-tested "shown once, unrecoverable" flow not worth touching for this leg. A mobile-only fallback (`.cardManageMobile`, the same mechanism legs 3-4 already use) keeps Rotate/Revoke reachable on mobile, which has no detail column to select into. **Found and fixed a real, pre-existing bug live while checking the mobile viewport, not assumed correct from prior legs' code review alone**: legs 3-4's `.cardChevron` class sets `display: none` to hide the desktop-only selection chevron below the mobile breakpoint, but at equal CSS specificity against `Icon.module.css`'s own `.root { display: inline-block }`, which one wins is decided by Next.js's per-route CSS bundle order — not something either stylesheet declares or controls, and not guaranteed consistent across routes. It happened to resolve correctly on Groups (leg 3) and Plugins (leg 4) — confirmed by re-checking, not just recalling — but silently failed here (the chevron rendered visible on a 375px viewport too, though harmlessly inert since row selection itself has no mobile UI to select into). Fixed by qualifying the selector as `svg.cardChevron` (element+class specificity) everywhere it's used, in `plugins/console/app/console.module.css` — strictly beats a plain class selector regardless of bundle order. Verified via computed-style inspection at 375px both before (`display: block`) and after (`display: none`) the fix, on the live dev server. Verified live end-to-end on both desktop and mobile beyond just that fix: registering a client, selecting it to open the desktop pane, rotating its secret from within the pane (the page-level banner shows the new secret correctly, pane stays open and selected throughout), revoking it (card and pane both disappear together, matching leg 3's delete precedent — no manual redirect needed), and the mobile card's own Rotate/Revoke buttons working identically to before this leg. `plugins/console/manifest.json` bumped `0.9.0` → `0.10.0`. This completes the Console shell/`ThreeColumnLayout` conversion itself (legs 1-5); leg 6 (an unrelated stale in-repo docs-server reference cleanup, folded into this workstream by direct request) remains independent and not yet started. `0.109.0` → `0.110.0` is workstream 0021 leg 4 (epic task 22.11 — Warden composer redesign, RFC 0063 third revision), closing out the workstream: `ModelPickerPopover` (new) replaces the inline `<select>` with a `Popover` grouped by provider (local model first, then each connected provider's own group), mirroring `ModelsView`'s own `displayLabel`/grouping logic (duplicated, not imported — that component's version is entangled with search/badge concerns this popover doesn't need), with a footer linking into Settings → Providers/Models. Incognito moved from the chat header's `Toggle` + label into the composer toolbar as an icon `Button` (`eye-off`, wrapped in `Tooltip`, `aria-pressed` reflecting state); the chat header itself, its "Manage providers"/"Manage models" links, and the disabled "Web search — Soon" toggle are all removed outright, not hidden. The centered-vs-docked composer positioning (RFC 0063 §12) needed more than "swap between two JSX trees on `turns.length === 0`": a first implementation nested the composer inside a wrapper only rendered in the empty-state branch, which meant React unmounted and remounted the entire composer subtree — including the incognito toggle button — every time the empty/non-empty boundary was crossed (sending the first message, or toggling incognito while an existing conversation has messages, since incognito's own context starts empty). Caught by a unit test asserting the incognito toggle survives a send-triggered centered→docked transition as the _same_ DOM node, which failed against the first implementation; fixed by keeping the composer (and the `EmptyState`/message-list branch) as permanently-stable siblings of `.chat` in both states, with only a `chatCentered` modifier class (`justify-content: center`) changing, never the tree shape. Verified live end to end against the same dev database leg 3 used, re-adding (then re-removing) the same OpenRouter test provider and model: the centered "Ask Warden anything" composer, the model picker popover's correct provider grouping and a working round trip through "Manage providers" and back via the breadcrumb's "Chat" link, the incognito toggle's visible pressed state and "Incognito chat" empty state, and the old header links/toggle-based incognito/disabled web-search toggle all confirmed absent from the DOM. `plugins/warden`'s own test suite grew from 278 to 291 tests (13 new, covering `ModelPickerPopover`'s grouping/selection/footer and the composer's no-remount guarantee); full existing suite, `pnpm --filter runtime typecheck`, `pnpm --filter @sovereignfs/ui typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check`, and `pnpm design:tokens:check` all green. `plugins/warden` manifest `0.8.0` → `0.9.0`. Full account in workstream 0021's own changelog and the epic file's task 22.11 completion note. `0.108.0` → `0.109.0` is workstream 0022 leg 4 (task 13.20): Console's Plugins/Apps page (`/console/plugins`) gains a 3rd `ThreeColumnLayout` column on desktop — selecting a row shows a detail pane (permissions list, access-policy select, selected users/groups grant lists) that replaces the old `PluginAccessDialog` dialog there, mirroring legs 2/3's Users/Groups conversions. `PluginAccessDialog`'s body was extracted into a shared `PluginAccessFields.tsx`, reused by both a slimmed, mobile-only `PluginAccessDialog` and the new `PluginDetailPane.tsx` — the same split legs 2/3 established. Deliberately narrower scope than those two legs, though: Activate/Toggle enable-disable/Open/Remove stay row-level actions rather than moving into the pane too, since "Open" especially is a frequent, low-risk action an admin shouldn't need to open a detail pane to reach, and this leg's own technical note (workstream 0022) names only `PluginAccessDialog`'s content as moving — a real scope decision, documented rather than assumed. A row is selectable (`isSelectable()` in `PluginsTable.tsx`) under exactly the condition `PluginAccessDialog` used to render (`!row.isChrome && status is enabled/disabled`) — chrome plugins (Account/Console/Launcher) and inactive/incompatible rows get no selection `<Link>` or chevron, matching what was and wasn't manageable before. Selection lives in `?plugin=<id>`, resolved server-side in `page.tsx` against the full (unfiltered) row list — `PluginsTable`'s own filter/search/examples-toggle state is local, client-only React state that was never URL-synced even before this leg, so it's a genuinely separate concern from the server-computed selection and survives the parent Server Component's re-render untouched (same component instance, same position in the tree, only its props change).
